# sa_std vs. Rust std: Interface Comparison Report

This document provides a 1:1 interface comparison between the current `sa_std` implementation in the `sci` project and the Rust standard library (`std`).

## Summary of Implementation Parity

`sa_std` uses a hybrid paradigm of `@extern` / `@export` functions and `[MACRO]` assembly macros, whereas Rust uses a high-level Trait, Struct, and Module system.

## Scope and Plugin Boundary

This report counts only the compiler-shipped standard library surface under `sa_std/` and the in-tree runtime ABI that those files import directly. External `sa_plugin_*` projects are intentionally excluded from `sa_std` parity, even when they expose familiar std-like APIs through `.sai` / `.sal` files.

Plugin capabilities are installable and uninstallable at any time, so they must not be used as evidence that a Rust `std` API is implemented by compiler std. When a capability has moved to a plugin, this document records it as a **plugin alternative** or **plugin-only** surface and keeps the corresponding Rust `std` gap open for `sa_std` unless an in-tree `sa_std/` module provides that API.

Current external capability buckets that should stay outside this report's `sa_std` implemented counts:

- `sa_plugin_deno`: Deno compatibility facade for env/args, fs, io, net, process, time, JSON/text/base64, and Responses/Chat compatibility helpers.
- `sa_plugin_http_client` / `sa_plugin_http_server`: HTTP request/response/client/server facade; Rust `std` has no high-level HTTP module, so these are ecosystem capabilities, not std parity.
- `sa_plugin_db`, `sa_plugin_sax`, `sa_plugin_wgpu`, `sa_plugin_3dengines`: database, XML/SAX, GPU, and 3D engine native capabilities; these are plugin domains, not compiler std.
- `sa_plugin_pkg`, `sa_plugin_bc2sa`, `sa_plugin_node`, `sa_plugin_ts`, `sa_plugin_vm`: package/toolchain/runtime integration plugins; these are host or ecosystem extensions, not `sa_std` APIs.

`sa_std` tests should therefore avoid reading `../sa_plugins/...` or depending on `$SA_PLUGINS_HOME` state. Plugin smoke coverage belongs with the plugin or plugin-host test suite.

---

*   **Additional Vec IntoIter aggregate/reduce aliases**: `VEC_INTO_ITER_SUM_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_PRODUCT_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_MIN_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_MAX_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_REDUCE_U64`, and `VEC_INTO_ITER_TRY_REDUCE_U64` expose the supportable concrete `u64` sum/product/min/max/reduce surfaces over the same explicit backing-Vec cursor.
*   **Scope note (Vec IntoIter aggregate/reduce)**: these helpers are concrete `u64` aggregate/reduce aliases over the cursor and do not model Rust `Sum` / `Product` / `Ord` trait dispatch, `Option<T>` ABI, or generic `Try` residual conversion.
*   **Additional Vec IntoIter search/predicate aliases**: `VEC_INTO_ITER_FIND_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_TRY_FIND_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_FIND_MAP_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_POSITION_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_RPOSITION_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_RFIND_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_ANY_U64`, and `VEC_INTO_ITER_ALL_U64` expose the supportable concrete `u64` find/find_map/position/reverse-position/reverse-find/any/all surfaces over the same explicit backing-Vec cursor.
*   **Scope note (Vec IntoIter search/predicate)**: these helpers consume through the cursor and short-circuit like the underlying iterator primitives, returning explicit bool, `(has, value)`, `(has, index)`, or `(ok, has, value)` scalars. They do not model Rust closure capture, `Option<T>` / `Option<usize>` ABI, generic `Try` residual conversion, or owned `IntoIter<T,A>` object layout/drop glue.
*   **Additional Vec IntoIter for_each aliases**: `VEC_INTO_ITER_FOR_EACH_U64` and `VEC_INTO_ITER_TRY_FOR_EACH_U64` expose the supportable concrete `u64` `for_each` / `try_for_each` surfaces over the same explicit backing-Vec cursor.
*   **Scope note (Vec IntoIter for_each)**: these helpers consume through the cursor, with `try_for_each` short-circuiting after the callback returns false and returning an explicit success scalar. They do not model Rust closure capture, generic `Try` residual conversion, panic/drop cleanup, or owned `IntoIter<T,A>` object layout/drop glue.
*   **Additional Vec IntoIter comparator/key aggregate aliases**: `VEC_INTO_ITER_MIN_BY_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_MAX_BY_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_MIN_BY_KEY_U64`, and `VEC_INTO_ITER_MAX_BY_KEY_U64` expose supportable concrete `u64` `min_by` / `max_by` / `min_by_key` / `max_by_key` surfaces over the same explicit backing-Vec cursor.
*   **Scope note (Vec IntoIter comparator/key aggregates)**: these helpers consume through the cursor and return explicit `(has, value)` scalars. Comparator callbacks use a concrete signed ordering result, key callbacks return concrete `u64` keys, and the surface does not model Rust `Option<T>` ABI, generic `Ord` trait dispatch, closure capture, or owned `IntoIter<T,A>` object layout/drop glue.
*   **Additional Vec IntoIter sortedness aliases**: `VEC_INTO_ITER_IS_SORTED_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_IS_SORTED_BY_U64`, and `VEC_INTO_ITER_IS_SORTED_BY_KEY_U64` expose supportable concrete `u64` `is_sorted` / `is_sorted_by` / `is_sorted_by_key` surfaces over the same explicit backing-Vec cursor.
*   **Scope note (Vec IntoIter sortedness)**: these helpers consume the remaining cursor and return concrete bool scalars. They do not model Rust generic `Ord` trait dispatch, closure capture, borrowed reference item types, or owned `IntoIter<T,A>` object layout/drop glue.
*   **Additional Vec IntoIter lexicographic comparison aliases**: `VEC_INTO_ITER_CMP_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_CMP_BY_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_PARTIAL_CMP_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_PARTIAL_CMP_BY_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_EQ_BY_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_EQ_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_PARTIAL_EQ_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_NE_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_LT_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_LE_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_GT_U64`, and `VEC_INTO_ITER_GE_U64` expose supportable concrete `u64` iterator comparison/equality surfaces over explicit backing-Vec cursors.
*   **Scope note (Vec IntoIter lexicographic comparison)**: these helpers consume both cursors and return concrete ordering/bool scalars, with `PARTIAL_CMP_BY` returning explicit `(ok, ordering)` instead of Rust `Option<Ordering>`. They do not model generic `Ord` / `PartialOrd` trait dispatch, Rust `Option` ABI, closure capture, or owned `IntoIter<T,A>` object layout/drop glue.
*   **Additional Vec IntoIter materializing collect aliases**: `VEC_INTO_ITER_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_TRY_COLLECT_U64`, and `VEC_INTO_ITER_COLLECT_INTO_U64` expose supportable concrete `u64` collection surfaces over explicit backing-Vec cursors.
*   **Scope note (Vec IntoIter collect)**: these helpers consume the cursor and materialize caller-owned `Vec<u64>` outputs. `TRY_COLLECT` calls a concrete `(value, out_value_ptr) -> ok` mapper, returns an explicit success scalar, and leaves any suffix after the failing item available; it does not model Rust generic `FromIterator`, generic `Try` residual conversion, `Result` / `Option` ABI, or allocator-parametric collection.
*   **Additional Vec IntoIter eager adapter collect aliases**: `VEC_INTO_ITER_TAKE_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_SKIP_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_REV_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_TRY_STEP_BY_COLLECT_U64`, and `VEC_INTO_ITER_STEP_BY_COLLECT_U64` expose supportable concrete `u64` `take(...).collect`, `skip(...).collect`, `rev().collect`, and `step_by(...).collect` shapes over explicit backing-Vec cursors.
*   **Scope note (Vec IntoIter adapter collect)**: these helpers are eager materializing aliases that return caller-owned `Vec<u64>` outputs and consume the cursor according to the underlying primitive. They do not model Rust lazy adapter object identity/state, generic `Iterator<Item = T>`, panic object behavior, or owned `IntoIter<T,A>` object layout/drop glue.
*   **Additional Vec IntoIter transform/filter eager collect aliases**: `VEC_INTO_ITER_MAP_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_INSPECT_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_SCAN_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_FILTER_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_FILTER_MAP_COLLECT_U64`, and `VEC_INTO_ITER_MAP_WHILE_COLLECT_U64` expose supportable concrete `u64` `map(...).collect`, `inspect(...).collect`, `scan(...).collect`, `filter(...).collect`, `filter_map(...).collect`, and `map_while(...).collect` shapes over explicit backing-Vec cursors.
*   **Scope note (Vec IntoIter transform/filter collect)**: these helpers are eager materializing aliases with concrete SA callbacks and explicit `Vec<u64>` outputs. `scan`, `filter_map`, and `map_while` use explicit out-pointer/status callback shapes instead of Rust `Option` objects, closure capture, generic `Try`/residual behavior, lazy adapter identity/state, or panic/drop cleanup.
*   **Additional Vec IntoIter sequence eager collect aliases**: `VEC_INTO_ITER_INTERSPERSE_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_INTERSPERSE_WITH_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_CYCLE_TAKE_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_TAKE_WHILE_COLLECT_U64`, and `VEC_INTO_ITER_SKIP_WHILE_COLLECT_U64` expose supportable concrete `u64` `intersperse(...).collect`, `intersperse_with(...).collect`, `cycle().take(...).collect`, `take_while(...).collect`, and `skip_while(...).collect` shapes over explicit backing-Vec cursors.
*   **Scope note (Vec IntoIter sequence collect)**: these helpers are eager materializing aliases with explicit `Vec<u64>` outputs. `CYCLE_TAKE` is a finite helper and does not model Rust's lazy infinite `Cycle` adapter; none of these helpers model lazy adapter identity/state, generic closure capture, panic/drop cleanup, or owned `IntoIter<T,A>` object layout/drop glue.
*   **Additional Vec IntoIter pair/composition eager collect aliases**: `VEC_INTO_ITER_ENUMERATE_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_ZIP_COLLECT_U64`, and `VEC_INTO_ITER_CHAIN_COLLECT_U64` expose supportable concrete `u64` `enumerate().collect`, `zip(...).collect`, and `chain(...).collect` shapes over explicit backing-Vec cursors.
*   **Scope note (Vec IntoIter pair/composition collect)**: these helpers are eager materializing aliases that flatten pair outputs into caller-owned `Vec<u64>` values (`index,value` or `left,right` lanes). They do not model Rust tuple ABI, generic `IntoIterator` conversion, lazy adapter identity/state, or owned `IntoIter<T,A>` object layout/drop glue.
*   **Additional Vec IntoIter adapter-state eager collect aliases**: `VEC_INTO_ITER_BY_REF_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_FUSE_COLLECT_U64`, and `VEC_INTO_ITER_PEEKABLE_COLLECT_U64` expose supportable concrete `u64` `by_ref().collect`, `fuse().collect`, and `peekable().collect` shapes over explicit backing-Vec cursors.
*   **Scope note (Vec IntoIter adapter-state collect)**: these helpers are eager materializing aliases with caller-owned `Vec<u64>` outputs. `BY_REF` delegates through the existing borrowed cursor alias and mutates the original cursor; `FUSE` and `PEEKABLE` collect the current finite cursor remainder. They do not model lazy `ByRef` / `Fuse` / `Peekable` adapter identity/state, borrow lifetimes/reference items, generic `Iterator<Item = T>`, or owned `IntoIter<T,A>` object layout/drop glue.
*   **Additional Vec IntoIter partition aliases**: `VEC_INTO_ITER_PARTITION_U64` and `VEC_INTO_ITER_IS_PARTITIONED_U64` expose supportable concrete `u64` `partition` and nightly `is_partitioned` surfaces over explicit backing-Vec cursors.
*   **Scope note (Vec IntoIter partition)**: `PARTITION` returns explicit true/false caller-owned `Vec<u64>` outputs and consumes the cursor; `IS_PARTITIONED` returns a concrete bool and follows the existing consuming `all(predicate) || !any(predicate)` shape. These helpers do not model Rust generic `Default + Extend` collection targets, tuple ABI, closure capture, trait wiring, panic/drop cleanup, or owned `IntoIter<T,A>` object layout/drop glue.
*   **Additional Vec IntoIter array/window eager collect aliases**: `VEC_INTO_ITER_ARRAY_CHUNKS_COLLECT_U64` and `VEC_INTO_ITER_MAP_WINDOWS_COLLECT_U64` expose supportable concrete `u64` nightly `array_chunks::<N>().collect` and `map_windows(...).collect` shapes over explicit backing-Vec cursors.
*   **Scope note (Vec IntoIter array/window collect)**: these helpers are eager materializing aliases with explicit success scalars and caller-owned `Vec<u64>` outputs. `ARRAY_CHUNKS` flattens complete chunks and leaves an incomplete tail; `MAP_WINDOWS` uses a concrete `(window_ptr, window_len) -> u64` mapper and consumes valid finite inputs. They do not model Rust const-generic array item/reference ABI, lazy adapter identity/state, generic closure capture, panic/drop cleanup, or owned `IntoIter<T,A>` object layout/drop glue.
*   **Additional Vec IntoIter next_chunk alias**: `VEC_INTO_ITER_TRY_NEXT_CHUNK_U64` exposes the supportable concrete `u64` nightly `Iterator::next_chunk` cursor shape over the explicit backing Vec.
*   **Scope note (Vec IntoIter next_chunk)**: the helper writes up to `%count` values into caller-provided storage and returns explicit `(ok, filled)` scalars. Short requests consume and expose the available remainder directly; it does not model Rust const-generic `[T; N]`, `Result<[T; N], array::IntoIter<T, N>>` ABI/remainder objects, generic `T`, or owned `IntoIter<T,A>` object layout/drop glue.
*   **Vec/StringBuf capacity remaining aliases**: `VEC_CAPACITY_REMAINING`, `VEC_REMAINING_CAPACITY`, `STRING_BUF_CAPACITY_REMAINING`, `STRING_BUF_REMAINING_CAPACITY`, `STRING_BUF_SPARE_CAPACITY_MUT`, and `STRING_BUF_SPLIT_AT_SPARE_MUT` expose concrete remaining-capacity counts (`cap - len`) and StringBuf byte spare mutable views. They reuse the existing Vec spare-capacity subset with `elem_size=1` for StringBuf and do not model Rust `MaybeUninit` spare objects or allocator trait integration.
*   **Missing from Rust**: generic `retain` / `retain_mut` beyond the concrete U64 predicate forms, lazy `drain`/`splice` iterator semantics, and generic element support.
*   **Safety/performance note**: allocator-facing capacity arithmetic now uses `NUM_U64_CHECKED_*` before multiplying element counts by element sizes, and `VEC_PUSH*` grows capacity by doubling after the zero-capacity case instead of using linear `cap + 1` growth. `VEC_GET*`, `VEC_FRONT`, and `VEC_BACK` now use checked slice access and return `0` for out-of-range/default-empty cases; the previous unchecked direct-index behavior is retained under `VEC_GET_UNCHECKED` for callers that have already proven bounds.
*   **Scope note**: `VEC_APPEND*` appends by copying the source vector's current slice view into the destination and then clearing the source length. `VEC_EXTEND_FROM_SLICE*` copies from a caller-provided slice view. `VEC_TRY_EXTEND_FROM_WITHIN_U64` checks a concrete `(start, length)` range, reserves capacity before re-reading the source slice, and copies the in-range `u64` window. `VEC_TRY_RANGE_U64`, `VEC_TRY_GET_RANGE_U64`, and `VEC_TRY_GET_RANGE_MUT_U64` return range `Slice` views over vector storage. `VEC_ITER_U64`, `VEC_ITER_MUT_U64`, `VEC_REF_INTO_ITER_U64`, and `VEC_MUT_REF_INTO_ITER_U64` build the existing concrete value-yielding `Iter` cursor from Vec slice views without consuming the Vec; `VEC_ITER_DEFAULT_U64` creates an empty cursor; `VEC_ITER_AS_REF_SLICE_U64`, `VEC_ITER_SIZE_HINT_U64`, `VEC_ITER_EXACT_SIZE_LEN_U64`, `VEC_ITER_EXACT_SIZE_IS_EMPTY_U64`, `VEC_ITER_NEXT_U64`, `VEC_ITER_NEXT_BACK_U64`, and `VEC_ITER_CLONE_U64` expose the supportable borrowed `slice::Iter` AsRef, Iterator, DoubleEndedIterator, ExactSizeIterator, and Clone shapes over the same Vec storage without cloning elements; `VEC_ITER_MUT_AS_REF_SLICE_U64`, `VEC_ITER_MUT_AS_MUT_SLICE_U64`, `VEC_ITER_MUT_SIZE_HINT_U64`, `VEC_ITER_MUT_EXACT_SIZE_LEN_U64`, `VEC_ITER_MUT_EXACT_SIZE_IS_EMPTY_U64`, `VEC_ITER_MUT_NEXT_U64`, and `VEC_ITER_MUT_NEXT_BACK_U64` expose the supportable borrowed `slice::IterMut` AsRef/as-mut-slice, Iterator, DoubleEndedIterator, ExactSizeIterator, and fused exhaustion shapes while still returning concrete `u64` values or `Slice` views rather than Rust `&mut T` items. `VEC_INTO_ITER_U64` consumes the source Vec into an explicit caller-owned backing `Vec<u64>` and returns a cursor over that backing Vec; `VEC_INTO_ITER_DEFAULT_U64` creates an empty backing `Vec<u64>` plus empty cursor for Rust's `Default for vec::IntoIter` shape; `VEC_INTO_ITER_AS_SLICE_U64` and `VEC_INTO_ITER_AS_MUT_SLICE_U64` expose the cursor's current remaining range as a `Slice` view; `VEC_INTO_ITER_AS_REF_SLICE_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_SIZE_HINT_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_EXACT_SIZE_LEN_U64`, and `VEC_INTO_ITER_EXACT_SIZE_IS_EMPTY_U64` expose the supportable AsRef, exact size_hint, and ExactSizeIterator states over that same remaining cursor range; `VEC_INTO_ITER_NEXT_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_NEXT_BACK_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_NTH_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_NTH_BACK_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_COUNT_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_LAST_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_ADVANCE_BY`, and `VEC_INTO_ITER_ADVANCE_BACK_BY` are Rust-named aliases over the current concrete cursor operations, returning explicit bool/result scalars rather than Rust `Option<T>` or `Result<(), NonZero<usize>>` layouts; `VEC_INTO_ITER_FOLD_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_TRY_FOLD_U64`, `VEC_INTO_ITER_RFOLD_U64`, and `VEC_INTO_ITER_TRY_RFOLD_U64` are concrete callback-based forward/reverse fold aliases over the same cursor and do not model Rust closure capture, generic `Try` residuals, or unwind/drop cleanup; `VEC_INTO_ITER_CLONE_U64` clones that current remaining range into a new caller-owned backing `Vec<u64>` and returns a fresh cursor over it. They do not model Rust reference item types, mutable references, borrow lifetimes, owned `IntoIter<T,A>` object layout, generic `T: Clone`, Rust `Option<usize>` / `Option<T>` / `Result` ABI, marker trait wiring, allocator parameters/cloning, or drop glue. `VEC_TRY_ROTATE_LEFT_U64`, `VEC_TRY_ROTATE_RIGHT_U64`, `VEC_TRY_SWAP_WITH_SLICE_U64`, `VEC_TRY_BINARY_SEARCH_U64`, `VEC_PARTITION_POINT_U64`, `VEC_LOWER_BOUND_U64`, `VEC_UPPER_BOUND_U64`, `VEC_EQUAL_RANGE_U64`, `VEC_IS_SORTED_U64`, `VEC_IS_SORTED_BY_U64`, `VEC_IS_SORTED_BY_KEY_U64`, `VEC_SORT_U64`, `VEC_SORT_BY_U64`, `VEC_SORT_BY_KEY_U64`, `VEC_SORT_BY_CACHED_KEY_U64`, `VEC_SORT_UNSTABLE_U64`, `VEC_SORT_UNSTABLE_BY_U64`, `VEC_SORT_UNSTABLE_BY_KEY_U64`, `VEC_TRY_SELECT_NTH_UNSTABLE_U64`, `VEC_TRY_SELECT_NTH_UNSTABLE_BY_U64`, `VEC_TRY_SELECT_NTH_UNSTABLE_BY_KEY_U64`, and `VEC_FILL_WITH_U64` are concrete `u64` view operations over the vector buffer; the callback variants use macro-level `(u64, u64) -> u64` comparator, `u64 -> u64` key, or `() -> u64` generator functions. The Vec search helpers delegate to the concrete slice search helpers and assume ascending `u64` storage. `VEC_SORT*` delegates to the concrete slice sort helpers; `VEC_SORT_BY_CACHED_KEY_U64` is a Rust-named alias over the key-sort subset and does not cache key values. The `SORT_UNSTABLE*` aliases satisfy Rust's sorted-result contract but reuse the same stable adjacent-swap implementation and do not model Rust's optimized stable/unstable algorithms, allocation behavior, or comparator panic/drop behavior. `VEC_TRY_SELECT_NTH_UNSTABLE*` delegates to the concrete slice select helpers and returns `(ok, left_slice, pivot_ptr, right_slice)` rather than Rust's borrow-scoped tuple. `VEC_TRY_SPLIT_AT_CHECKED_U64` and `VEC_TRY_SPLIT_AT_MUT_CHECKED_U64` are Rust-named checked aliases over the current non-panicking split-at view helpers. `VEC_FIRST` / `VEC_LAST` and the explicit `VEC_FIRST_U64` / `VEC_LAST_U64` aliases are Rust-named aliases over the existing front/back helpers, `VEC_TRY_FIRST_MUT_U64` / `VEC_FIRST_MUT_U64`, `VEC_TRY_LAST_MUT_U64` / `VEC_LAST_MUT_U64`, and the nightly `vec_peek_mut`-named `VEC_TRY_PEEK_MUT*` / `VEC_PEEK_MUT*` helpers return raw element pointers rather than Rust `Option<&mut T>` or guard objects. `VEC_TRY_PUSH_WITHIN_CAPACITY*` returns a bool-style capacity result without growing, and the `*_MUT*` variants additionally return the inserted element pointer on success or null on failure rather than Rust's `Result<&mut T, T>` object layout. `VEC_TRY_SPLIT_FIRST_U64` / `VEC_TRY_SPLIT_LAST_U64` return concrete `(ok, value, rest_slice)` views instead of Rust `Option<(&T, &[T])>`, and the mut split variants return `(ok, value_ptr, rest_slice)` raw pointer views instead of borrow-scoped Rust `&mut` tuples. `VEC_TRY_FIRST_CHUNK*_U64`, `VEC_TRY_LAST_CHUNK*_U64`, and `VEC_TRY_SPLIT_*_CHUNK*_U64` return `Slice` views over vector storage rather than Rust const-generic array references; zero-length chunks succeed with an empty chunk and the full counterpart slice where applicable. `VEC_RCHUNK_*`, `VEC_CHUNKS_EXACT_*`, and `VEC_RCHUNKS_EXACT_*` expose count, remainder where applicable, and indexed `Slice` views rather than Rust iterator values. `VEC_CHUNK_COUNT_U64`, `VEC_TRY_CHUNK_AT_U64`, `VEC_WINDOW_COUNT_U64`, and `VEC_TRY_WINDOW_AT_U64` expose non-iterator concrete slice-view subsets over the vector storage; zero chunk/window sizes return no views. `VEC_TRY_POP_IF_U64` checks the current last `u64` with a caller-supplied predicate function and only pops when it returns nonzero. `VEC_RETAIN_U64` keeps values for which a caller-supplied `u64 -> u64` predicate returns nonzero and compacts the vector in place. `VEC_EXTRACT_IF_U64`, `VEC_DRAIN_U64`, and `VEC_SPLICE_U64` return removed values in new `Vec<u64>` buffers rather than Rust lazy draining iterator objects; `VEC_SPLICE_U64` inserts replacement values from a caller-provided `Slice<u64>` at the drained start index. `VEC_RESIZE_WITH_U64(out_len, vec, new_len, gen_fn)` grows with a macro-level `() -> u64` generator, truncates when the requested length is smaller, and returns the final length. `VEC_DEDUP_BY_U64` compares adjacent retained/current `u64` values with a caller-supplied `(u64, u64) -> u64` equality predicate. `VEC_DEDUP_BY_KEY_U64` compares keys produced by a caller-supplied `u64 -> u64` key function. `VEC_SWAP_REMOVE*`, `VEC_TRY_INSERT*`, `VEC_DEDUP_U64`, `VEC_DEDUP_BY_U64`, `VEC_DEDUP_BY_KEY_U64`, `VEC_RESIZE_WITH_U64`, `VEC_EXTRACT_IF_U64`, `VEC_DRAIN_U64`, `VEC_SPLICE_U64`, `VEC_RETAIN_U64`, and the chunk/window helpers currently target the concrete `u64`/8-byte element layout used by this SA vec surface.
*   **Scope note**: `VEC_RESERVE_EXACT*` maps to the current exact-growth reserve implementation; the allocator may still choose physical allocation details outside the SA surface. The strip helpers return `u64` slice views over the vector data rather than allocating or copying.

The unchecked helpers construct views or raw element pointers without bounds checks; callers must uphold Rust-style unsafe preconditions. `SLICE_TRY_FIRST_CHUNK*_U64`, `SLICE_TRY_LAST_CHUNK*_U64`, and `SLICE_TRY_SPLIT_*_CHUNK*_U64` return `Slice` views rather than Rust const-generic array references; zero-length chunks succeed with an empty chunk and the full counterpart slice where applicable. `SLICE_RCHUNK_*`, `SLICE_CHUNKS_EXACT_*`, and `SLICE_RCHUNKS_EXACT_*` expose count, remainder where applicable, and indexed `Slice` views rather than Rust iterator values. `SLICE_TRY_ROTATE_LEFT_U64`, `SLICE_TRY_ROTATE_RIGHT_U64`, `SLICE_TRY_SWAP_WITH_SLICE_U64`, `SLICE_IS_SORTED_U64`, `SLICE_IS_SORTED_BY_U64`, `SLICE_IS_SORTED_BY_KEY_U64`, `SLICE_SORT_U64`, `SLICE_SORT_BY_U64`, `SLICE_SORT_BY_KEY_U64`, `SLICE_SORT_BY_CACHED_KEY_U64`, `SLICE_SORT_UNSTABLE_U64`, `SLICE_SORT_UNSTABLE_BY_U64`, `SLICE_SORT_UNSTABLE_BY_KEY_U64`, `SLICE_TRY_SELECT_NTH_UNSTABLE_U64`, `SLICE_TRY_SELECT_NTH_UNSTABLE_BY_U64`, `SLICE_TRY_SELECT_NTH_UNSTABLE_BY_KEY_U64`, and `SLICE_FILL_WITH_U64` are concrete `u64` in-place/view operations and non-panicking where Rust would panic on invalid rotate/swap/select lengths; callback variants use macro-level `(u64, u64) -> u64` comparator, `u64 -> u64` key, or `() -> u64` generator functions. `SLICE_SORT*` uses a simple adjacent-swap stable ordering over the current slice storage; `SLICE_SORT_BY_CACHED_KEY_U64` is a Rust-named alias over `SLICE_SORT_BY_KEY_U64` and does not cache key values. `SORT_UNSTABLE*` aliases satisfy Rust's sorted-result contract but reuse the same implementation. `SLICE_TRY_SELECT_NTH_UNSTABLE*` fully sorts the concrete slice, returns explicit `(ok, left_slice, pivot_ptr, right_slice)`, and reports out-of-range `nth` as `ok=0` instead of panicking. These helpers do not model Rust's optimized stable/unstable sorting or selection algorithms, allocation behavior, panic/drop safety, cached-key optimization, or borrow-scoped returned references. Chunk/window helpers return caller-indexed `Slice` views, not Rust lazy iterator adapters. Binary-search helpers assume ascending `u64` slices; `SLICE_PARTITION_POINT_U64` uses a caller-supplied `u64 -> u64` predicate, while `LOWER_BOUND`, `UPPER_BOUND`, and `EQUAL_RANGE` provide concrete index pairs instead of Rust generic comparator APIs. Mutable helpers return raw element pointers for verifier-friendly in-place updates; they intentionally do not pretend to be Rust's generic `[T]` trait surface or borrow-scoped reference semantics.

### 1.1b ASCII (`std::ascii` / `u8` ASCII methods vs `sa_std/ascii.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: `ASCII_IS_ASCII`, `ASCII_SLICE_IS_ASCII`, checked/unchecked transparent slice views (`ASCII_SLICE_AS_ASCII`, `ASCII_SLICE_AS_ASCII_UNCHECKED`, `ASCII_SLICE_AS_ASCII_MUT`), `ASCII_IS_UPPERCASE`, `ASCII_IS_LOWERCASE`, `ASCII_IS_ALPHABETIC`, `ASCII_IS_ALPHANUMERIC`, `ASCII_IS_DIGIT`, `ASCII_IS_OCTDIGIT`, `ASCII_IS_HEXDIGIT`, `ASCII_IS_PUNCTUATION`, `ASCII_IS_GRAPHIC`, `ASCII_IS_WHITESPACE`, `ASCII_IS_CONTROL`, `ASCII_TO_UPPERCASE`, `ASCII_TO_LOWERCASE`, `ASCII_EQ_IGNORE_CASE`, `ASCII_BYTE_MAKE_UPPERCASE`, `ASCII_BYTE_MAKE_LOWERCASE`, `ASCII_SLICE_MAKE_UPPERCASE`, `ASCII_SLICE_MAKE_LOWERCASE`, `ASCII_SLICE_EQ_IGNORE_CASE`, `ASCII_ESCAPE_DEFAULT_WRITE`, plus a transparent one-byte `AsciiChar` layout with `ASCII_CHAR_FROM_U8`, unchecked construction, decimal digit construction, `to_u8` / `to_char` / `as_bytes`, case conversion, ignore-case comparison, in-place case mutation, `ASCII_CHAR_ESCAPE_DEFAULT_WRITE`, and classifier helpers.
*   **Missing from Rust**: full `std::ascii::Char` enum variant API, the `EscapeDefault` iterator object, true Rust typed `&[ascii::Char]` / `&mut [ascii::Char]` array and slice references, Rust trait impls, char/str/OsStr owned allocation-returning conversions, and Unicode-aware non-ASCII case mapping.
*   **Scope note**: The SA ASCII facade is a byte-level contract over `u8` values, transparent `Slice` views, a transparent `AsciiChar` byte wrapper, and mutable `Slice` bytes. It follows Rust's ASCII ranges, including WhatWG ASCII whitespace (`TAB`, `LF`, `FF`, `CR`, `SPACE`) and no vertical tab. `ASCII_SLICE_AS_ASCII` and `ASCII_SLICE_AS_ASCII_MUT` return `(ok, Slice)` views over the original bytes when all bytes are ASCII, or `ok=0` with an empty view on failure; the unchecked form preserves the source pointer/length and relies on the caller to uphold the ASCII precondition. `ASCII_ESCAPE_DEFAULT_WRITE` writes Rust-style `escape_default` bytes into caller-provided storage (`\n`, `\r`, `\t`, escaped quotes/backslash, printable ASCII as-is, and lowercase `\xNN` for other controls) and reports `(ok, len)` instead of materializing Rust's lazy iterator. In-place slice helpers mutate caller-owned bytes and do not allocate owned `String` / `Vec` results.

### 1.1c Character primitives (`std::char` / `char` methods vs `sa_std/char.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: char scalar constants and layout aliases (`CHAR_MIN`, `CHAR_MAX`, `CHAR_DEFAULT`, `CHAR_REPLACEMENT` / `CHAR_REPLACEMENT_CHARACTER`, `CHAR_MAX_LEN_UTF8`, `CHAR_MAX_LEN_UTF16`, `CHAR_SIZE`, `CHAR_ALIGN`), char error layout constants (`ParseCharError`, `DecodeUtf16Error`, `CharTryFromError`, `TryFromCharError`, and `PARSE_CHAR_ERROR_*` kind values), `CHAR_IS_VALID_SCALAR`, `CHAR_FROM_U32`, `CHAR_FROM_U32_UNCHECKED`, `CHAR_LEN_UTF8`, `CHAR_LEN_UTF16`, `CHAR_TRY_ENCODE_UTF8`, `CHAR_TRY_ENCODE_UTF16`, `CHAR_DECODE_UTF16_UNIT`, `CHAR_ESCAPE_UNICODE_WRITE`, `CHAR_ESCAPE_DEFAULT_WRITE`, `CHAR_ESCAPE_DEBUG_WRITE`, ASCII classification/case helpers (`CHAR_IS_ASCII*`, `CHAR_TO_ASCII_*`, `CHAR_EQ_IGNORE_ASCII_CASE`), and radix conversion helpers (`CHAR_TO_DIGIT`, `CHAR_IS_DIGIT`, `CHAR_FROM_DIGIT`).
*   **Missing from Rust**: Unicode table-driven alphabetic/numeric/whitespace/case-mapping APIs, `escape_unicode` / `escape_debug` / `escape_default` lazy iterator types, full UTF-16 encoding/decoding iterator objects, lazy iterator/error integration for decode/parse/try-from paths, char pattern traits, and formatted display/debug implementations.
*   **Scope note**: The SA char facade treats a character as a validated `u64` Unicode scalar value, while the new layout aliases record Rust's 4-byte scalar representation for size/align checks. It returns explicit `(ok, value)` pairs instead of Rust `Option` / `Result` objects and intentionally limits case conversion/classification to ASCII unless a future Unicode-table layer is added. `CHAR_TRY_ENCODE_UTF16` writes one or two `u16` units to caller-provided storage; `CHAR_DECODE_UTF16_UNIT` decodes a single lead unit plus caller-provided lookahead and returns `(ok, char, units_consumed)` instead of Rust's lazy `decode_utf16` iterator and error object. `ParseCharError` and `DecodeUtf16Error` are represented only as concrete SA layout constants, while `CharTryFromError` / `TryFromCharError` are recorded as zero-sized error layouts; Rust trait/display/source behavior is not modeled. `CHAR_ESCAPE_UNICODE_WRITE` writes Rust-style lowercase `\u{...}` bytes into caller storage and reports `(ok, len)`. `CHAR_ESCAPE_DEFAULT_WRITE` follows Rust `char::escape_default` for special ASCII escapes, printable ASCII pass-through, and Unicode fallback. `CHAR_ESCAPE_DEBUG_WRITE` follows a concrete `escape_debug` subset: the same special ASCII escapes, printable ASCII pass-through, raw UTF-8 for non-ASCII scalars, and `\u{...}` for non-printable ASCII controls. These helpers do not materialize Rust's lazy escape iterator objects or full Unicode general-category graphic classification.

### 1.1d Comparison (`std::cmp` vs `sa_std/cmp.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: `CMP_ORDERING_LESS`, `CMP_ORDERING_EQUAL`, `CMP_ORDERING_GREATER`, `CMP_ORDERING_IS_LT`, `CMP_ORDERING_IS_EQ`, `CMP_ORDERING_IS_GT`, `CMP_ORDERING_IS_NE`, `CMP_ORDERING_IS_LE`, `CMP_ORDERING_IS_GE`, `CMP_ORDERING_THEN`, `CMP_ORDERING_THEN_WITH`, `CMP_ORDERING_REVERSE`, `CMP_COMPARE_U64`, `CMP_COMPARE_I64`, `CMP_MIN_U64`, `CMP_MAX_U64`, `CMP_MIN_BY_U64`, `CMP_MAX_BY_U64`, `CMP_MIN_BY_KEY_U64`, `CMP_MAX_BY_KEY_U64`, `CMP_MIN_I64`, `CMP_MAX_I64`, `CMP_CLAMP_U64`, `CMP_CLAMP_I64`, `CMP_TRY_CLAMP_U64`, `CMP_TRY_CLAMP_I64`, and concrete `Reverse<u64>` / `Reverse<i64>` layout helpers (`CMP_REVERSE_*_NEW`, `GET`, `SET`, `COPY`, `DEFAULT`, `CLONE`, `CLONE_FROM`, reversed `COMPARE`, min/max-value helpers, and primitive-forwarding hash/hash_one helpers).
*   **Missing from Rust**: trait-level `PartialEq`, `Eq`, `PartialOrd`, `Ord`, generic `min` / `max` / `clamp`, generic `Reverse<T>`, derived comparison implementations, and custom comparator adapters.
*   **Scope note**: The current comparison facade is a primitive macro subset. Ordering values use `-1`, `0`, and `1` to represent Rust's `Less`, `Equal`, and `Greater`. `CMP_ORDERING_THEN_WITH` calls a macro-level `() -> i64` callback only when the first ordering is equal. `CMP_MIN_BY_U64` / `CMP_MAX_BY_U64` use a caller-supplied `(u64, u64) -> i64` comparator, while the key variants use a `u64 -> u64` key callback. `CMP_REVERSE_U64_*` and `CMP_REVERSE_I64_*` model the common `std::cmp::Reverse<T>` ordering inversion for concrete 8-byte primitive values; their default/clone helpers write or copy the inner primitive, and the Reverse hash helpers model Rust's transparent single-field derived `Hash` by forwarding to the matching primitive hash writer. They do not implement Rust's generic tuple struct, trait impl forwarding, `Debug` formatting, randomized `RandomState`, SipHash compatibility, or type-system-level ordering behavior. The min/max/clamp helpers are concrete `u64` and `i64` macros; `CMP_TRY_CLAMP_*` reports `ok=0` for invalid ranges (`min > max`) instead of Rust's panic-on-invalid-range behavior.

### 1.1e Array (`std::array` / `[T; N]` helpers vs `sa_std/array.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: `ARRAY_LEN`, `ARRAY_IS_EMPTY`, `ARRAY_AS_SLICE_U64`, `ARRAY_AS_MUT_SLICE_U64`, Rust-named concrete slice-view aliases for array `AsRef<[T]>` / `AsMut<[T]>` / `Borrow<[T]>` / `BorrowMut<[T]>` lowering (`ARRAY_AS_REF_SLICE_U64`, `ARRAY_AS_MUT_REF_SLICE_U64`, `ARRAY_BORROW_SLICE_U64`, `ARRAY_BORROW_MUT_SLICE_U64`), `ARRAY_FROM_REF_U64`, `ARRAY_FROM_MUT_U64`, `ARRAY_EACH_REF_U64`, `ARRAY_EACH_MUT_U64`, concrete array iterator aliases (`ARRAY_ITER_U64`, `ARRAY_ITER_MUT_U64`, `ARRAY_REF_INTO_ITER_U64`, `ARRAY_MUT_REF_INTO_ITER_U64`), `ARRAY_GET_U64`, `ARRAY_GET_MUT_PTR_U64`, Rust-named concrete `Index` / `IndexMut` aliases (`ARRAY_INDEX_U64`, `ARRAY_INDEX_MUT_PTR_U64`), `ARRAY_TRY_GET_U64`, `ARRAY_TRY_GET_MUT_PTR_U64`, `ARRAY_FIRST_U64`, `ARRAY_LAST_U64`, `ARRAY_FILL_U64`, `ARRAY_REPEAT_U64`, concrete primitive array defaults (`ARRAY_DEFAULT_U64`, `ARRAY_DEFAULT_I64`, `ARRAY_DEFAULT_USIZE`, `ARRAY_DEFAULT_BOOL`) plus `DEFAULT_ARRAY_*` aliases, `ARRAY_COPY_U64`, concrete array clone/copy aliases (`ARRAY_CLONE_U64`, `ARRAY_CLONE_FROM_U64`, `ARRAY_COPY_FROM_U64`), concrete array equality aliases (`ARRAY_EQ_U64`, `ARRAY_NE_U64`, `ARRAY_PARTIAL_EQ_U64`), concrete array ordering aliases (`ARRAY_CMP_U64`, `ARRAY_PARTIAL_CMP_U64`, `ARRAY_LT_U64`, `ARRAY_LE_U64`, `ARRAY_GT_U64`, `ARRAY_GE_U64`), concrete array hash aliases (`DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_ARRAY_U64`, `ARRAY_HASH_U64`, `BUILD_HASHER_DEFAULT_HASH_ONE_ARRAY_U64`, `RANDOM_STATE_HASH_ONE_ARRAY_U64`), `ARRAY_TRY_COPY_FROM_SLICE_U64`, Rust-named concrete `TryFrom<&[T]>` / `TryFrom<&mut [T]>` copy aliases (`ARRAY_TRY_FROM_SLICE_U64`, `ARRAY_TRY_FROM_MUT_SLICE_U64`), checked slice-to-array-ref helpers and Rust-named `TryFrom` aliases (`ARRAY_TRY_AS_ARRAY_REF_U64`, `ARRAY_TRY_AS_ARRAY_MUT_U64`, `ARRAY_REF_TRY_FROM_SLICE_U64`, `ARRAY_MUT_REF_TRY_FROM_MUT_SLICE_U64`), `ARRAY_TRY_SPLIT_ARRAY_REF_U64`, `ARRAY_TRY_SPLIT_ARRAY_MUT_U64`, `ARRAY_TRY_RSPLIT_ARRAY_REF_U64`, `ARRAY_TRY_RSPLIT_ARRAY_MUT_U64`, `ARRAY_SWAP_U64`, `ARRAY_REVERSE_U64`, `ARRAY_FROM_FN_U64`, `ARRAY_TRY_FROM_FN_U64`, `ARRAY_MAP_U64`, and `ARRAY_TRY_MAP_U64`.
*   **Missing from Rust**: const-generic `[T; N]` type semantics, generic element support, generic trait-level impls, by-value `array::IntoIter`, iterator item reference/lifetime semantics, `TryFromSliceError` object modeling, true `array::try_from_fn` / fallible map with Rust `Try` residual/error propagation and cleanup semantics, borrow-scoped `from_ref` / `from_mut` references, `each_ref` / `each_mut` array-of-reference return types, and split/as-array APIs that return Rust array references rather than slice views.
*   **Scope note**: The SA array facade is a primitive `u64` memory-layout macro subset over caller-owned contiguous storage. It exposes concrete slice views, raw mutable pointer views, checked slice-to-array-length views, eager copy/fill/reverse/from-index generation, eager mapping helpers, primitive zero-default fill helpers, concrete clone/copy/equality/ordering/hash helpers, concrete value-iterator construction aliases, and fallible callback-based generation/mapping helpers. `ARRAY_TRY_FROM_FN_U64` calls a concrete `(index, out_value_ptr) -> ok` callback and stops at the first zero status; `ARRAY_TRY_MAP_U64` calls a concrete `(value, out_value_ptr) -> ok` mapper and stops at the first zero status. Both return a bool-style status and write only successfully produced elements before failure; they do not model Rust residual values, partially initialized drop cleanup, or generic `Try` conversion. `ARRAY_DEFAULT_*` / `DEFAULT_ARRAY_*` fill caller-owned storage with the concrete primitive default value rather than implementing Rust's generic `Default for [T; N]`. `ARRAY_CLONE_U64`, `ARRAY_CLONE_FROM_U64`, and `ARRAY_COPY_FROM_U64` copy concrete `u64` storage and model Rust's array `Clone` / `Copy` lowering shape only for trivially copied elements. `ARRAY_INDEX_U64` and `ARRAY_INDEX_MUT_PTR_U64` delegate to the concrete element-load and mutable-pointer helpers for Rust array `Index` / `IndexMut` lowering, but do not model generic index types, borrow lifetimes, or Rust's bounds-check panic. `ARRAY_ITER_U64`, `ARRAY_ITER_MUT_U64`, `ARRAY_REF_INTO_ITER_U64`, and `ARRAY_MUT_REF_INTO_ITER_U64` build the existing concrete value-yielding `Iter` from array slice views, modeling Rust's array-reference iterator lowering shape without reference item types, mutable item references, or lifetimes. `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_ARRAY_U64`, `ARRAY_HASH_U64`, and the array `hash_one` helpers delegate to slice hashing over the deterministic SA `DefaultHasher`; they do not implement Rust's generic `Hash` trait, const-generic trait dispatch, or randomized hasher behavior. `ARRAY_EQ_U64` / `ARRAY_NE_U64` compare equal-length concrete arrays by delegating to the slice equality helper and do not implement generic `PartialEq` / `Eq` trait dispatch. `ARRAY_CMP_U64`, `ARRAY_PARTIAL_CMP_U64`, and the relation helpers compare equal-length concrete arrays lexicographically by delegating to the slice comparison helper and do not implement generic `PartialOrd` / `Ord` trait dispatch or `Option<Ordering>` object modeling. `ARRAY_TRY_FROM_SLICE_U64` and `ARRAY_TRY_FROM_MUT_SLICE_U64` copy when the slice length exactly matches the requested array length and return `ok=0` for length mismatch instead of materializing Rust's `Result<_, TryFromSliceError>`. The slice-to-array-ref helpers similarly return `(ok, Slice)` views for exact-length matches rather than Rust reference types or `Result` objects. The `from_ref` / `from_mut`, `each_ref` / `each_mut`, array `AsRef` / `AsMut` / `Borrow` / `BorrowMut`, `as_array`, `split_array`, and `rsplit_array` helpers return `Slice` views over `u64` storage rather than Rust borrow-scoped references, generic trait impls, or const-generic array reference types. It does not model Rust const generics, generic array traits, lazy iterators, or panic/indexing semantics.

### 1.1f Pointer (`std::ptr` vs `sa_std/ptr.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: `PTR_NULL`, `PTR_IS_NULL`, `PTR_IS_NONNULL`, `PTR_EQ`, `PTR_NE`, `PTR_ADDR`, `PTR_EXPOSE_ADDR`, `PTR_EXPOSE_PROVENANCE`, transparent pointer cast helpers, byte and concrete `u64` add/sub/wrapping add/wrapping sub helpers, byte and `u64` offset-from helpers including unsigned-return variants, `PTR_IS_ALIGNED_TO`, `PTR_GUARANTEED_EQ`, `PTR_GUARANTEED_NE`, concrete `u64`/`u8` reads and writes including Rust-named `*_UNALIGNED_*` and `*_VOLATILE_*` aliases, concrete `i32` volatile reads, `PTR_SWAP_U64`, `PTR_SWAP_NONOVERLAPPING_U64`, `PTR_REPLACE_U64`, `PTR_TAKE_U64`, `PTR_COPY_BYTES`, `PTR_COPY_NONOVERLAPPING_BYTES`, `PTR_WRITE_BYTES`, `PTR_COPY_U64S`, `PTR_COPY_NONOVERLAPPING_U64S`, and a transparent `NonNull` pointer wrapper with `NONNULL_NEW`, `NONNULL_NEW_UNCHECKED`, `NONNULL_AS_PTR`, `NONNULL_AS_REF`, `NONNULL_AS_MUT`, `NONNULL_ADDR`, expose-provenance, byte and `u64` add/sub/wrapping add/wrapping sub helpers, offset-from helpers, alignment checks, null/non-null validation, equality, guaranteed equality/inequality, and cast helpers.
*   **Missing from Rust**: strict provenance APIs, `addr_of!` / `addr_of_mut!`, integer-to-pointer construction from exposed addresses, true hardware volatile semantics, architecture-specific unaligned access semantics beyond SA `load`/`store`, metadata/fat-pointer APIs, `drop_in_place`, typed pointer casts beyond transparent wrapper casts, full `NonNull` provenance mapping APIs, and full aliasing/provenance contracts.
*   **Scope note**: The current pointer facade sticks to SA-supported raw pointer primitives: null checks, equality, address bitcasts, transparent casts, byte/u64 offsets, offset-difference helpers, aligned checks, concrete u8/u64 loads/stores, concrete i32 volatile loads, eager memory copy/set through `sa_mem_copy` / `sa_mem_set`, and a verifier-friendly `NonNull` wrapper over an existing pointer. Rust-named unaligned, volatile, wrapping-offset, expose-provenance, and guaranteed-equality helpers are explicit aliases over deterministic SA pointer/register operations; they improve frontend lowering compatibility but do not model hardware volatility, barriers, target-specific unaligned behavior, strict-provenance side effects, or Rust's compile-time unknown `None` case for `guaranteed_eq` / `guaranteed_ne`. `PTR_COPY_NONOVERLAPPING_*` maps to the same runtime copy primitive as `PTR_COPY_*`; non-overlap is a caller precondition. The facade intentionally does not expose integer-to-pointer construction until the verifier and IR surface make that provenance contract explicit.

### 1.1g Default / Convert / Memory (`std::default`, `std::convert`, `std::mem` vs `sa_std/default.sa`, `sa_std/convert.sa`, `sa_std/mem.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: primitive zero defaults (`DEFAULT_BOOL`, integer `DEFAULT_*`, `DEFAULT_F32`, `DEFAULT_F64`, `DEFAULT_CHAR`, `BOOL_DEFAULT`, `NUM_*_DEFAULT`, `NUM_F32_DEFAULT`, `NUM_F64_DEFAULT`, `CHAR_DEFAULT`), Rust-named `From` / `TryFrom` / `SaturatingFrom` / `Into` / `TryInto` / `SaturatingInto` aliases (`FROM_*` / `TRY_FROM_*` / `SAT_FROM_*` / `INTO_*` / `TRY_INTO_*` / `SAT_INTO_*`) wrapping existing `CONVERT_*` helpers, concrete primitive `std::convert::identity` lowering macros (`CONVERT_IDENTITY_U64`, `CONVERT_IDENTITY_I64`, `CONVERT_IDENTITY_USIZE`, `CONVERT_IDENTITY_BOOL`) plus `IDENTITY_*` aliases, `Infallible_SIZE` / `Infallible_ALIGN` constant aliases model the Rust `convert::Infallible` never-error type layout, complete `CONVERT_*` min/max coverage (`CONVERT_U64_MIN` / `CONVERT_U64_MAX` / `CONVERT_I64_MIN` / `CONVERT_USIZE_MIN` / `CONVERT_USIZE_MAX` / `CONVERT_ISIZE_MIN` / `CONVERT_ISIZE_MAX` / `CONVERT_BOOL_MIN` / `CONVERT_BOOL_MAX`), primitive bool/integer conversions (`CONVERT_BOOL_TO_U64`, `CONVERT_U64_TO_BOOL`, unsigned/signed widening helpers including current 64-bit `usize` / `isize` aliases, checked `CONVERT_TRY_*` integer narrowing helpers, and saturating `CONVERT_SAT_*` narrow casts), primitive layout helpers (`MEM_SIZE_OF_*`, `MEM_ALIGN_OF_*`) including f32, f64, ptr, and char types, concrete `u64` `size_of_val` / `align_of_val` helpers, concrete `u64` memory mutation/copy/drop-like helpers (`MEM_SWAP_U64`, `MEM_REPLACE_U64`, `MEM_TAKE_U64`, `MEM_COPY_U64`, `MEM_DROP_U64`), primitive `MEM_NEEDS_DROP_PRIMITIVE` / `MEM_FORGET_U64`, and concrete `MaybeUninitU64` / `ManuallyDropU64` layout helpers including pointer/ref/mut views, value reads, zeroing, drop-reset, byte-slice views, transparent deref pointer helpers, `ManuallyDropU64` default/copy/clone helpers, primitive-forwarding hash/hash_one helpers, and inner-value comparison helpers.
*   **Missing from Rust**: trait-level `Default`, `From`, `Into`, `TryFrom`, `TryInto`, `AsRef`, `AsMut`, `Borrow`, `ToOwned`, generic `convert::identity<T>` as a function item, `mem::transmute`, generic `MaybeUninit<T>` / `ManuallyDrop<T>`, real `forget` / `drop` behavior for owned resources, type-aware `needs_drop`, discriminant APIs, generic swap/replace/take, and type-system-driven layout introspection.
*   **Scope note**: These modules are primitive macro contracts. They give frontends stable building blocks for common Rust lowering patterns, including concrete `u64` maybe-uninit/manual-drop storage, explicit integer cast checks/clamps, and concrete identity value moves for selected primitive register types, but they do not implement Rust's trait system, generic conversions, function item identity, drop semantics, or unsafe layout/transmute model. The `MaybeUninitU64` byte-view helpers return SA `Slice` views over the 8-byte payload, `ASSUME_INIT_REF` / `ASSUME_INIT_MUT` return raw payload pointers, and `ASSUME_INIT_DROP` / `MEM_DROP_U64` reset or consume only the concrete primitive storage rather than running Rust drop glue. The `ManuallyDropU64` default/copy/clone helpers only write or copy the concrete inner `u64`; the hash helpers load that value and forward to the deterministic primitive hash path, matching Rust's `Hash for ManuallyDrop<T>` shape only for the existing concrete wrapper; the comparison helpers similarly forward to primitive `u64` equality/ordering and return direct bool/ordering scalars rather than Rust trait objects or `Option<Ordering>`. They do not imply generic derived traits, `MaybeUninit` initialization validity/hashing/comparison, Rust `Debug` formatting, or Rust drop-suppression safety.

### 1.1h Numeric primitives (`std::num` / integer inherent methods vs `sa_std/num.sa`)

for SA's 64-bit target, checked/saturating/wrapping unsigned `u8` / `u16` / `u32` add/sub/mul/div/rem and shift helpers, unsigned narrow bit count/zero-scan, `is_power_of_two`, `abs_diff`, `midpoint`, `isqrt`, and `pow` helpers, signed narrow `i8` / `i16` / `i32` sign-extension, sign/positive/negative/abs/unsigned_abs/abs_diff, checked add/sub/mul/div/rem/shift/neg/abs, direct `isqrt`, saturating `NUM_U64_ISOLATE_LOWEST_ONE`, `NUM_U64_HIGHEST_ONE`, `NUM_U64_LOWEST_ONE`), reverse bits, swap bytes, little/big-endian value conversion helpers, little/big/native-endian byte-array conversion
