`NUM_I64_MIDPOINT`, `NUM_I64_ISQRT`, `NUM_I64_POW`, 64-bit `usize` aliases for the unsigned Rust-named helpers including endian conversion, saturating division, bit count/zero-scan, `div_ceil`, `isqrt`, checked/direct/wrapping next power, and `ilog*`, 64-bit `isize` aliases for signed Rust-named helpers including endian conversion, signed bit-pattern counts/scans and bit-position helpers, `unsigned_abs`, `div_euclid`, `rem_euclid`, `div_ceil`, `next_multiple_of`, `isqrt`, `ilog*`, and overflowing add/sub/mul, and concrete `NonZeroU8` / `NonZeroU16` / `NonZeroU32` / `NonZeroU64` / `NonZeroUsize` plus `NonZeroI8` / `NonZeroI16` / `NonZeroI32` / `NonZeroI64` / `NonZeroIsize` layouts with construction, `get`, validity checks, checked add/sub/mul/div/rem where width-appropriate, signed checked abs/neg plus unsigned_abs, selected saturating operations including signed abs/neg, unsigned power-of-two predicates, signed predicate helpers, bit count/leading/trailing zero helpers, unsigned bit-width helpers returning `NonZeroU32`, unsigned isqrt helpers, same-width signed/unsigned bit-pattern cast helpers, concrete highest/lowest-one bit-position helpers, concrete isolate highest/lowest-one helpers, concrete rotate/reverse-bits helpers, endian value transforms, caller-buffer byte-array write/from helpers, concrete copy/clone/clone_from helpers, concrete comparison helpers, concrete min/max helpers, and primitive-forwarding hash/hash_one helpers. `NonZero*` associated constants (`NONZERO_U8` / `NONZERO_U16` / `NONZERO_U32` / `NONZERO_U64` / `NONZERO_USIZE` / `NONZERO_I8` / `NONZERO_I16` / `NONZERO_I32` / `NONZERO_I64` / `NONZERO_ISIZE` `MIN` / `MAX` / `ONE` where applicable) mirroring Rust 1.70+ stabilized associated constants. Unsigned MIN (0) and BYTES constants (NUM_U8_MIN through NUM_USIZE_MIN, NUM_U8_BYTES through NUM_ISIZE_BYTES) mirror Rust integer::MIN and 1.60+ integer::BYTES. f32 and f64 IEEE 754 associated constants (NUM_F32_INFINITY through NUM_F32_MIN_SUBNORMAL, NUM_F32_BITS, NUM_F32_BYTES, and matching NUM_F64_*) expose bit-level float class constants as integer constants. WrappingU64/WrappingU32/WrappingI64 and SaturatingU64/SaturatingI64 layout constants model Rust Wrapping<T> and Saturating<T> transparent struct layouts, with concrete construction/access/arithmetic/operator/default/copy/clone/comparison/pow/BITS helpers, unsigned power-of-two and Wrapping unsigned next-power helpers where Rust exposes them, and primitive-forwarding hash/hash_one helpers for the existing concrete wrapper widths. `DEFAULT_WRAPPING_U64`, `DEFAULT_WRAPPING_U32`, `DEFAULT_WRAPPING_I64`, `DEFAULT_SATURATING_U64`, and `DEFAULT_SATURATING_I64` zero-init aliases are available in default.sa.
*   **Additional implemented primitive pow subset**: `NUM_U{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_CHECKED_POW` and `NUM_I{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_CHECKED_POW` expose Rust primitive `checked_pow` as explicit `(ok, value)` pairs; matching `*_STRICT_POW` helpers trap with SA `panic(2204)` on overflow.
*   **Additional implemented strict arithmetic subset**: `NUM_U{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_STRICT_{ADD,SUB,MUL,DIV,REM}` and matching signed helpers return the checked arithmetic result or trap with SA `panic(2205)` / `panic(2206)` / `panic(2207)` / `panic(2208)` / `panic(2209)` on overflow or invalid division/remainder.
*   **Additional implemented unsigned overflowing subset**: `NUM_U{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_OVERFLOWING_{ADD,SUB,MUL,SHL,SHR,DIV,DIV_EUCLID,REM,REM_EUCLID,NEG,POW}` returns declared-width wrapping values and bool-style overflow/oversized-shift flags for concrete unsigned primitive widths.
*   **Scope note (primitive unsigned overflowing)**: arithmetic helpers use the existing checked and wrapping paths to produce Rust's `(value, overflow)` shape; shift helpers use wrapping shifts for the value and `shift >= BITS` for the flag; nonzero unsigned div/rem/euclid helpers always return overflow flag `0`; unsigned neg sets the flag when the input is nonzero; unsigned pow returns wrapping exponentiation plus an overflow flag derived from checked pow. This does not cover Rust tuple ABI, divide-by-zero panic object/message modeling, `u128` / `i128` public primitive support, or generic trait dispatch.
*   **Additional implemented signed overflowing subset**: `NUM_I{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_OVERFLOWING_{ADD,SUB,MUL,DIV,DIV_EUCLID,REM,REM_EUCLID,NEG,SHL,SHR,ABS,POW}` returns signed wrapping values and bool-style overflow/oversized-shift flags for concrete signed primitive widths.
*   **Scope note (primitive signed overflowing)**: add/sub/mul helpers use the existing signed checked and wrapping paths to produce Rust's `(value, overflow)` shape; div helpers return `MIN` with overflow flag `1` for `MIN / -1` and otherwise return the signed quotient with flag `0`; rem helpers return `0` with overflow flag `1` for `MIN % -1`; Euclidean variants perform Rust-style quotient/remainder adjustment for negative remainders; neg and abs helpers flag signed minimum values; shift helpers use wrapping shifts for the value and `shift >= BITS` for the flag; pow helpers return signed wrapping exponentiation plus an overflow flag derived from checked pow; `isize` uses the current 64-bit SA ABI. This does not cover Rust tuple ABI, divide-by-zero panic message/object modeling, `i128`, or generic trait dispatch.
*   **Additional implemented narrow signed bit helper subset**: `NUM_I{8,16,32}_{COUNT_ONES,COUNT_ZEROS,LEADING_ZEROS,TRAILING_ZEROS,LEADING_ONES,TRAILING_ONES,ISOLATE_HIGHEST_ONE,ISOLATE_LOWEST_ONE,HIGHEST_ONE,LOWEST_ONE}` exposes Rust signed primitive bit count/scan, bit-isolation, and bit-position methods for concrete narrow signed widths.
*   **Scope note (narrow signed bit helpers)**: helpers operate on the same-width unsigned bit pattern, isolate helpers sign-extend the selected bit back to the signed width, and highest/lowest helpers return explicit `ok/index` pairs rather than Rust `Option<u32>`. This does not cover Rust `Option` layout, `i128`, or generic trait dispatch.
*   **Additional implemented `isize` bit helper subset**: `NUM_ISIZE_{COUNT_ONES,COUNT_ZEROS,LEADING_ZEROS,TRAILING_ZEROS,LEADING_ONES,TRAILING_ONES,ISOLATE_HIGHEST_ONE,ISOLATE_LOWEST_ONE,HIGHEST_ONE,LOWEST_ONE}` delegates to the existing signed `i64` bit-pattern helpers for the current 64-bit SA ABI.
*   **Scope note (`isize` bit helpers)**: signed bit counts and one-scans operate on the two's-complement bit pattern, isolate helpers return the selected bit or zero, and highest/lowest helpers return explicit `ok/index` pairs rather than Rust `Option<u32>`. This does not cover portable target-width switching, Rust `Option` layout, `i128`, or generic trait dispatch.
*   **Additional implemented narrow signed midpoint subset**: `NUM_I{8,16,32}_MIDPOINT` exposes Rust signed primitive midpoint helpers for concrete narrow signed widths.
*   **Scope note (narrow signed midpoint)**: helpers sign-extend operands to the declared signed width, reuse the existing overflow-avoiding `i64` midpoint path, and sign-extend the result back to the concrete narrow width. This models Rust's sufficiently-wide signed average rounded toward zero for supported widths, without `i128`, Rust trait dispatch, or generic numeric abstractions.
*   **Additional implemented narrow unsigned bit count subset**: `NUM_U8_COUNT_ONES`, `NUM_U8_COUNT_ZEROS`, `NUM_U8_LEADING_ZEROS`, `NUM_U8_TRAILING_ZEROS`, `NUM_U8_LEADING_ONES`, `NUM_U8_TRAILING_ONES`, matching `U16` / `U32` helpers, and `NUM_USIZE_*` aliases model Rust's unsigned primitive bit count and zero/one scan methods for concrete SA widths.
*   **Scope note (narrow unsigned bit counts)**: narrow helpers mask inputs to the declared Rust width before counting, and zero/all-one scan results use the declared width rather than SA's 64-bit register width. `usize` uses the current 64-bit SA ABI and delegates to the existing `u64` helpers. This does not model `u128`, compiler intrinsic selection, or generic trait dispatch.
*   **Additional implemented unsigned carrying add/borrow subset**: `NUM_U{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_CARRYING_ADD` and `NUM_U{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_BORROWING_SUB` return the declared-width wrapped result and bool-style carry/borrow flag for Rust's unsigned bigint ternary add/sub helpers.
*   **Scope note (primitive carrying add/borrow)**: inputs and outputs are masked/truncated to the declared width, carry/borrow inputs are treated as bool-style nonzero values, and `usize` uses the current 64-bit SA ABI. This does not model Rust tuple ABI, `const_unsigned_bigint_helpers` feature-gate plumbing, compiler intrinsic selection, or generic trait dispatch.
*   **Additional implemented unsigned carrying multiplication subset**: `NUM_U{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_CARRYING_MUL` and `NUM_U{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_CARRYING_MUL_ADD` return low/high declared-width limbs for `lhs * rhs + carry` and `lhs * rhs + carry + addend`, matching Rust's unsigned bigint helper result semantics for concrete SA widths.
*   **Scope note (primitive carrying multiplication)**: operands, carry/addend inputs, and low/high outputs are masked/truncated to the declared width, while `usize` uses the current 64-bit SA ABI. The implementation uses two-limb long multiplication and carry propagation rather than a public `u128` scalar. This does not model Rust tuple ABI, `const_unsigned_bigint_helpers` feature-gate plumbing, compiler intrinsic selection, public `u128` / `i128` primitive support, or generic trait dispatch.
*   **Additional implemented unchecked disjoint bit-or subset**: `NUM_U{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_UNCHECKED_DISJOINT_BITOR` returns the declared-width bitwise OR for operands that satisfy Rust's `(lhs & rhs) == 0` safety precondition.
*   **Scope note (primitive unchecked disjoint bit-or)**: operands and results are masked/truncated to the declared width and `usize` uses the current 64-bit SA ABI. Overlapping inputs are outside the helper contract; this models valid-input result semantics without Rust immediate-UB enforcement, runtime overlap validation, `disjoint_bitor` feature-gate plumbing, compiler intrinsic selection, `u128`, or generic trait dispatch.
*   **Additional implemented gather/scatter bits subset**: `NUM_U{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_EXTRACT_BITS` packs source bits selected by a sparse mask into contiguous low bits, while matching `DEPOSIT_BITS` helpers distribute successive source low bits into successive set-bit positions in the mask for every existing SA unsigned width.
*   **Scope note (primitive gather/scatter bits)**: inputs, masks, and results are masked/truncated to the declared width, excess deposit source bits are ignored, and `usize` uses the current 64-bit SA ABI. This models the semantics of Rust's unstable `uint_gather_scatter_bits` methods without modeling feature-gate plumbing, hardware/compiler `pext` / `pdep` selection, `u128`, or generic trait dispatch.
*   **Additional implemented carryless multiplication subset**: `NUM_U{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_CARRYLESS_MUL` exposes Rust's unstable unsigned primitive `carryless_mul` method for every existing SA unsigned width, performing GF(2) binary polynomial multiplication with XOR accumulation and returning the declared-width low half.
*   **Scope note (primitive carryless multiplication)**: inputs, shifted terms, and final results are masked/truncated to the declared width, while `usize` uses the current 64-bit SA ABI. This does not model the `uint_carryless_mul` feature gate, compiler intrinsic selection, `u128`, or generic trait dispatch.
*   **Additional implemented funnel shift subset**: `NUM_U{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_FUNNEL_SHL` / `FUNNEL_SHR` and matching `UNCHECKED_FUNNEL_SHL` / `UNCHECKED_FUNNEL_SHR` helpers expose Rust's unstable unsigned primitive funnel shifts for every existing SA unsigned width.
*   **Scope note (primitive funnel shifts)**: safe helpers require `shift < BITS` and trap with SA `panic(2212)` / `panic(2213)` otherwise; unchecked helpers rely on that caller precondition. Zero shifts are handled before computing the complementary amount, narrow results are masked, and `usize` uses the current 64-bit SA ABI. This does not model Rust unsafe UB enforcement, the `funnel_shifts` feature gate, compiler intrinsic selection, `u128`, or generic trait dispatch.
*   **Additional implemented floor division subset**: `NUM_U{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_DIV_FLOOR` and matching signed helpers expose Rust's unstable primitive `div_floor` method for every existing SA integer width. Unsigned helpers use ordinary division; signed helpers correct a nonexact truncating quotient by subtracting one when the operand signs differ.
*   **Scope note (primitive floor division)**: zero divisors and signed `MIN / -1` trap with SA `panic(2208)`, independent of any overflow-check mode. Narrow signed results retain the existing sign-extended register convention, and `usize` / `isize` use the current 64-bit SA ABI. This does not model Rust panic objects/messages, the `int_roundings` feature gate, `u128` / `i128`, or generic trait dispatch.
*   **Additional implemented exact division subset**: `NUM_U{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_CHECKED_DIV_EXACT`, `DIV_EXACT`, and `UNCHECKED_DIV_EXACT` plus matching signed helpers expose Rust's unstable exact-division method family for all existing SA primitive widths.
*   **Scope note (primitive exact division)**: checked helpers return explicit `(ok, value)` pairs and fail on zero divisors, signed overflow, or nonzero remainders. Direct helpers return the same explicit Option-like result for exact versus inexact division but trap with SA `panic(2208)` on zero divisors or signed overflow. Unchecked helpers do not validate their positive-divisor, exact-divisibility, and no-overflow preconditions. This does not model Rust `Option` layout, unsafe UB enforcement, the `exact_div` feature gate, `u128` / `i128`, or generic trait dispatch.
*   **Additional implemented Euclidean checked/strict subset**: `NUM_U{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_CHECKED_{DIV,REM}_EUCLID`, `NUM_I{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_CHECKED_{DIV,REM}_EUCLID`, and matching `*_STRICT_*_EUCLID` helpers expose Rust primitive Euclidean checked result and strict panic control-flow shapes. Strict Euclidean division traps with SA `panic(2210)`, and strict Euclidean remainder traps with `panic(2211)`.
*   **Additional implemented narrow signed direct Euclidean subset**: `NUM_I{8,16,32}_DIV_EUCLID` and `NUM_I{8,16,32}_REM_EUCLID` expose direct signed Euclidean quotient/remainder helpers for narrow signed widths by reusing the existing checked Euclidean paths and matching the current `i64` direct helper shape. Existing `STRICT_*_EUCLID` helpers remain the panic-control-flow surface; this subset does not model Rust panic object/message identity, `i128`, or generic trait dispatch.
*   **Additional implemented narrow signed ceil-division subset**: `NUM_I{8,16,32}_CHECKED_DIV_CEIL` and `NUM_I{8,16,32}_DIV_CEIL` expose Rust signed primitive ceil-division helpers for narrow signed widths.
*   **Scope note (narrow signed ceil division)**: checked helpers reuse the existing narrow signed checked division paths, return explicit `ok/value` results for zero divisors and signed `MIN / -1`, and adjust the truncating quotient by `+1` only for nonzero remainders with same-sign operands. Direct helpers keep the existing SA direct-helper shape and write `0` on checked failure rather than materializing Rust panic objects. This does not model Rust panic object/message identity, `i128`, or generic trait dispatch.
*   **Additional implemented narrow signed next-multiple subset**: `NUM_I{8,16,32}_CHECKED_NEXT_MULTIPLE_OF` and `NUM_I{8,16,32}_NEXT_MULTIPLE_OF` expose signed primitive next-multiple helpers for narrow signed widths.
*   **Scope note (narrow signed next multiple)**: helpers sign-extend operands to the declared signed width, reuse the existing `i64` checked next-multiple path, and reject results outside the concrete narrow range. This preserves the current SA signed next-multiple subset where negative divisors use absolute magnitude and direct helpers write `0` on checked failure; it does not model Rust panic object/message identity, `i128`, or generic trait dispatch.
*   **Additional implemented narrow signed integer-log subset**: `NUM_I{8,16,32}_CHECKED_ILOG` / `ILOG`, matching `CHECKED_ILOG2` / `ILOG2`, and matching `CHECKED_ILOG10` / `ILOG10` expose signed primitive floor-log helpers for narrow signed widths.
*   **Scope note (narrow signed integer logs)**: helpers sign-extend values and arbitrary bases to the declared signed width before reusing the existing `i64` signed log path. Checked helpers return explicit `ok/value` pairs for nonpositive values or bases below 2, while direct helpers write `0` on checked failure rather than materializing Rust panic objects. This does not model Rust panic object/message identity, `i128`, or generic trait dispatch.
*   **Additional implemented narrow signed direct-pow subset**: `NUM_I{8,16,32}_POW` exposes direct signed primitive exponentiation for narrow signed widths.
*   **Scope note (narrow signed direct pow)**: helpers sign-extend the base to the declared signed width, reuse the existing `i64` direct pow accumulator, and sign-extend the result back to the concrete narrow width. This models the current SA direct pow / release-style wrapping result shape, not Rust debug overflow panic object/message identity, `i128`, or generic trait dispatch.
*   **Additional implemented strict shift subset**: `NUM_U{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_STRICT_{SHL,SHR}` and matching signed helpers expose Rust primitive strict shift control-flow shapes. Oversized left shifts trap with SA `panic(2212)`, and oversized right shifts trap with `panic(2213)`.
*   **Additional implemented unbounded shift subset**: `NUM_U{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_UNBOUNDED_{SHL,SHR}` and matching signed helpers expose Rust primitive unbounded shift shapes. In-range shifts preserve the declared width; oversized left shifts and unsigned right shifts return zero, while oversized signed right shifts return sign fill (`0` or `-1`).
*   **Scope note (primitive unbounded shifts)**: the unbounded helpers explicitly branch before executing a shift whose amount is greater than or equal to the declared width. Narrow unsigned results are masked, narrow signed in-range results are sign-extended, and `usize` / `isize` use the current 64-bit SA ABI. They do not model `u128` / `i128`, Rust feature gates, or generic trait dispatch.
*   **Additional implemented exact shift subset**: `NUM_U{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_{SHL,SHR}_EXACT` and matching signed helpers expose Rust's unstable exact shift methods as explicit `(ok, value)` pairs; matching `*_UNCHECKED_{SHL,SHR}_EXACT` helpers expose the caller-precondition unsafe lowering shape.
*   **Scope note (primitive exact shifts)**: checked exact helpers branch before oversized shifts and accept only results whose inverse shift restores the original declared-width value. Failure returns `ok=0/value=0`; unchecked helpers do not validate Rust's lossless/in-range safety preconditions. Narrow unsigned results are masked and narrow signed results are sign-extended. This does not model Rust `Option` layout, UB enforcement, the `exact_bitshifts` feature gate, `u128` / `i128`, or generic trait dispatch.
*   **Additional implemented checked/strict neg-abs subset**: `NUM_U{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_CHECKED_NEG` exposes Rust unsigned primitive `checked_neg` as explicit `(ok, value)` pairs; matching unsigned `*_STRICT_NEG` and signed `NUM_I{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_STRICT_NEG` helpers trap with SA `panic(2214)` on overflow, while signed `*_STRICT_ABS` helpers trap with `panic(2215)` on signed minimum input.
*   **Additional implemented mixed-sign checked/strict add-sub subset**: `NUM_U{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_CHECKED_ADD_SIGNED`, `*_STRICT_ADD_SIGNED`, `*_CHECKED_SUB_SIGNED`, and `*_STRICT_SUB_SIGNED` expose Rust unsigned primitive signed-RHS arithmetic shapes; `NUM_I{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_CHECKED_ADD_UNSIGNED`, `*_STRICT_ADD_UNSIGNED`, `*_CHECKED_SUB_UNSIGNED`, and `*_STRICT_SUB_UNSIGNED` expose the matching signed primitive unsigned-RHS arithmetic shapes. Checked helpers return explicit `(ok, value)` pairs; strict add-shaped failures reuse SA `panic(2205)`, and strict sub-shaped failures reuse `panic(2206)`.
*   **Additional implemented unsigned checked signed-difference subset**: `NUM_U{8,16,32,64,SIZE}_CHECKED_SIGNED_DIFF` exposes Rust unsigned primitive `checked_signed_diff` as explicit `(ok, value)` pairs returning the corresponding signed-width result.
*   **Additional implemented unsigned saturating division subset**: `NUM_U{8,16,32}_SATURATING_DIV` extends the existing `NUM_U64_SATURATING_DIV` surface to the narrow unsigned primitive widths, and `NUM_USIZE_SATURATING_DIV` exposes the same path for the current 64-bit target `usize` alias. Helpers return the quotient on checked-division success and the matching unsigned MAX value on checked failure.
*   **Missing from Rust**: `u128`/`i128`, float inherent methods beyond the current IEEE 754 associated constants (now covered: INFINITY, NEG_INFINITY, NAN, ZERO, MINUS_ZERO, MIN_POSITIVE, EPSILON, MAX, MIN, MAX_SUBNORMAL, MIN_SUBNORMAL, BITS, BYTES for both f32 and f64), complete Rust `NonZero*` method parity beyond the current primitive arithmetic/bit-position/bit-isolate/bit-rotate/reversal/bit/endian/copy/clone/comparison/min/max/hash/sign-cast/isqrt subset (remaining conversions, niche behavior, trait-level comparison/formatting impls, feature-gate modeling, and generic trait dispatch), Rust's niche optimization semantics, generic `Wrapping<T>` type/trait impls beyond the current concrete layout/arithmetic/operator/bit-count/bit-transform/comparison/pow/BITS/unsigned-next-power/hash subset, generic `Saturating<T>` beyond the current concrete layout/arithmetic/operator/signed-unary/bit-count/bit-transform/comparison/pow/BITS/hash subset, generic numeric traits, parsing/formatting error types, exact Rust overflow panic behavior beyond the current primitive strict add/sub/mul/div/rem/shift/neg/abs/euclidean-div/euclidean-rem/pow/mixed-sign-add-sub subset, complete target-dependent endian/platform abstractions beyond the explicit SA native-endian convention, and Rust's trait/panic/type-level integration around integer inherent methods.
*   **Scope note**: The current numeric facade is a concrete integer macro subset designed for frontend lowering and SA test ergonomics. Unsigned narrow helpers return `0` on checked failure and use explicit masks for wrapping/narrow results; unsigned saturating division follows the existing SA `u64` surface by mapping zero divisors or raw quotient range overflow to the matching unsigned MAX instead of Rust's zero-divisor panic object/control flow. Signed narrow helpers assume signed SA inputs and sign-extend wrapping results to their concrete bit width. Signed `unsigned_abs` returns the corresponding unsigned bit-pattern magnitude, so signed minimum values map to `2^(BITS-1)` rather than failing. Signed narrow checked division/rem reject division by zero and `MIN / -1` overflow through `ok=0`; wrapping division/rem map `MIN / -1` to Rust's wrapping result. Primitive `NUM_*_STRICT_DIV` and `NUM_*_STRICT_REM` helpers reuse checked division/remainder and trap with SA `panic(2208)` / `panic(2209)` on zero divisors or signed `MIN / -1` overflow rather than materializing Rust panic objects. Primitive `NUM_*_STRICT_SHL` and `NUM_*_STRICT_SHR` helpers reuse checked shift helpers and trap with SA `panic(2212)` / `panic(2213)` when the shift amount is greater than or equal to the declared bit width. Unsigned `NUM_*_CHECKED_NEG` succeeds only for zero, while `NUM_*_STRICT_NEG` traps with SA `panic(2214)` for unsigned nonzero values or signed `MIN`; signed `NUM_*_STRICT_ABS` traps with `panic(2215)` for signed `MIN`. Checked Euclidean div/rem helpers use explicit `(ok, value)` pairs; unsigned variants fail on zero divisors, while signed variants fail on zero divisors and signed `MIN / -1`. Strict Euclidean helpers wrap those checked paths and trap with SA `panic(2210)` / `panic(2211)` rather than materializing Rust panic objects. Mixed-sign checked add/sub helpers return explicit `(ok, value)` pairs rather than Rust `Option`; strict add-signed/add-unsigned helpers trap with `panic(2205)`, and strict sub-signed/sub-unsigned helpers trap with `panic(2206)`. Unsigned `checked_signed_diff` helpers return explicit `(ok, value)` pairs; positive differences must fit through the matching signed max, while negative differences may reach the matching signed min magnitude. `u64::unchecked_*` / `usize::unchecked_*` are direct operation aliases and rely on callers to uphold Rust's safety preconditions. `u64::is_multiple_of` follows Rust's zero divisor behavior (`0.is_multiple_of(0)` is true, nonzero values are false), while `checked_next_multiple_of` reports zero divisors or overflow through `ok=0`. Unsigned and signed `checked_ilog*` return explicit `(ok, value)` pairs instead of Rust `Option<u32>`; signed logarithms only succeed for positive values and bases greater than one. Unsigned `NUM_*_ISQRT` helpers return the floor integer square root; narrow unsigned variants mask to the declared Rust width first. Signed `NUM_I*_ISQRT` helpers return the signed floor root for nonnegative inputs and trap with SA `panic(2203)` for negative inputs, modeling Rust's panic control-flow shape but not panic object/message identity. Primitive `NUM_*_CHECKED_POW` helpers similarly return `(ok, value)` pairs and `NUM_*_STRICT_POW` traps with SA `panic(2204)` on overflow rather than materializing Rust panic objects. `NUM_U64_HIGHEST_ONE`, `NUM_U64_LOWEST_ONE`, `NUM_I64_HIGHEST_ONE`, and `NUM_I64_LOWEST_ONE` likewise expose Rust `Option`-shaped methods as `(ok, index)` pairs, and the signed bit-count/isolate/index helpers operate on the two's-complement bit pattern. Signed `checked_next_multiple_of` treats the divisor by absolute magnitude and returns the least multiple greater than or equal to the input, reporting zero divisors and positive overflow through `ok=0`. Signed ceil division helpers expose checked `(ok, value)` variants to avoid trapping on division-by-zero or `MIN / -1`. `NUM_U{8,16,32,64}_WRITE_{BE,LE,NE}_BYTES` and `NUM_U{8,16,32,64}_FROM_{BE,LE,NE}_BYTES` model Rust's byte-array conversions with caller-owned buffers; the signed `NUM_I{8,16,32,64}_*` variants preserve the same two's-complement bit pattern and sign-extend reads to the SA `i64` register shape. `NUM_*_{TO,FROM}_{BE,LE}` model Rust's endian value transforms using the current little-endian ABI assumption; `*_NE` byte helpers currently map to little-endian. `NUM_USIZE_*` and `NUM_ISIZE_*` are explicit 64-bit platform-sized aliases in the current SA ABI. `NonZero*` wrappers use explicit 8-byte value layouts for verifier-friendly SA storage rather than Rust's optimized `Option<NonZero*>` representation. Their checked operations only succeed when the arithmetic succeeds and the resulting value is nonzero; signed checked neg/abs additionally report the primitive `MIN` overflow through `ok=0`, while saturating neg/abs preserve the NonZero invariant by saturating `MIN` to the corresponding max value. Signed `NonZero*::unsigned_abs` wrappers write the unsigned NonZero counterpart layout and preserve the signed-minimum bit-pattern magnitude. Same-width `NonZero*::cast_signed` / `cast_unsigned` helpers preserve the stored bit pattern while writing the target signed or unsigned wrapper layout; narrow signed outputs are sign-extended into SA's register shape and narrow unsigned outputs are masked to the declared Rust width. Byte-array constructors and endian transform wrappers return `ok=0` when the decoded/transformed value is zero. Narrow `NonZero` bit count and bit-position helpers use the declared Rust primitive width rather than the SA register width; unsigned `NONZERO_U*_BIT_WIDTH_NZ_U32` helpers lower Rust's `bit_width` to `BITS - leading_zeros()` and write the guaranteed nonzero result into a `NonZeroU32` layout. Unsigned NonZero isqrt helpers lower Rust's unsigned `NonZero<T>::isqrt` by computing the primitive floor square root and writing the guaranteed nonzero result into the same concrete unsigned wrapper layout. `NONZERO_*_HIGHEST_ONE` lowers to `BITS - 1 - leading_zeros()` and `NONZERO_*_LOWEST_ONE` lowers to `trailing_zeros()` for the existing concrete widths. `NONZERO_*_ISOLATE_HIGHEST_ONE` / `NONZERO_*_ISOLATE_LOWEST_ONE` preserve only the declared-width most/least significant set bit and sign-extend narrow signed results back to their wrapper width. `NONZERO_*_ROTATE_LEFT` / `NONZERO_*_ROTATE_RIGHT` and `NONZERO_*_REVERSE_BITS` operate on the declared-width bit-pattern and sign-extend narrow signed results back to their wrapper width, modeling Rust's supportable `self.get().rotate_*()` / `reverse_bits()` shapes without feature-gate modeling. NonZero copy/clone helpers copy the concrete stored nonzero primitive into independent destination storage and intentionally do not expose a `Default` surface. NonZero comparison helpers compare the concrete stored primitive and return direct bool/ordering scalars rather than Rust trait objects or `Option<Ordering>`. NonZero min/max helpers write the selected concrete stored primitive into destination wrapper storage; `clamp` is intentionally absent until Rust's invalid-range panic semantics can be modeled. NonZero hash helpers forward the concrete stored value through the matching primitive integer hash path, modeling Rust's `self.get().hash(state)` shape without generic trait dispatch. Wrapping/Saturating default/copy/clone helpers write or copy only the existing concrete inner primitive field, comparison helpers compare that field with direct bool/ordering scalar results, and hash helpers forward that field through the matching primitive hash path. The Wrapping operator helpers similarly load concrete inner fields and write destination wrappers; `WrappingU32` masks stored values to the concrete 32-bit width before comparing or applying operators. Wrapper `pow` helpers forward to primitive pow/saturating_pow shapes, and `SaturatingI64` pow chooses signed overflow direction from the original base sign and exponent parity. Primitive unsigned checked next-power helpers return explicit `ok/out` results, while direct and wrapping next-power helpers write zero when the next power exceeds the concrete width. `SaturatingI64` signed unary helpers use existing saturating primitive neg/abs behavior, including `MIN -> MAX`. The module does not claim generic `Wrapping<T>` / `Saturating<T>`, absent wrapper widths, generic trait dispatch, Rust layout equivalence for narrow wrappers, parser APIs, or float semantics.

### 1.1i Ops ranges and control flow (`std::ops` range structs / `ControlFlow` vs `sa_std/ops.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: concrete `u64` layouts and helpers for `Range`, `RangeFrom`, `RangeTo`, `RangeInclusive`, `RangeToInclusive`, `RangeFull`, and `Bound`: `RANGE_U64_*`, `RANGE_FROM_U64_*`, `RANGE_TO_U64_*`, `RANGE_INCLUSIVE_U64_*`, `RANGE_TO_INCLUSIVE_U64_*`, `RANGE_FULL_*`, and `BOUND_U64_*` constructors, accessors, contains checks, copy/copied/cloned/map helpers, concrete `Bound<&u64>` ref-layout helpers for `as_ref`/`as_mut` and ref copied/cloned, concrete `RangeBounds::is_empty`-style checks over bound pairs, concrete `IntoBounds::intersect`-style bound-pair intersection helpers, length checks, slice-view helpers, RangeBounds/IntoBounds-style bound extraction helpers, and consuming cursor helpers for finite `Range<u64>` / `RangeInclusive<u64>` (`RANGE_U64_NEXT`, `RANGE_U64_NEXT_BACK`, `RANGE_U64_NTH`, `RANGE_U64_COUNT`, `RANGE_INCLUSIVE_U64_NEXT`, `RANGE_INCLUSIVE_U64_NEXT_BACK`, `RANGE_INCLUSIVE_U64_COUNT`, `RANGE_INCLUSIVE_U64_INTO_INNER`). `RangeUsize_*`, `RangeFromUsize_*`, `RangeToUsize_*`, `RangeInclusiveUsize_*`, and `RangeToInclusiveUsize_*` layout constants plus `RANGE_*_USIZE_*` constructors, accessors, contains, slice-view, RangeBounds/IntoBounds-style bound extraction aliases, finite cursor/count aliases, and `RANGE_INCLUSIVE_USIZE_INTO_INNER` expose the same concrete range subset over SA's 64-bit `usize` ABI. `BoundUsize_*` layout constants and `BOUND_USIZE_*` constructors, accessors, default, start/end/range contains helpers, concrete `Bound<&usize>` ref-layout helpers for `as_ref`/`as_mut` and ref copied/cloned, concrete `RangeBounds::is_empty`-style checks, concrete `IntoBounds::intersect`-style helpers, and copy/copied/cloned/map helpers expose the same Rust `Bound<T>` variant order for SA's 64-bit `usize` ABI. `ControlFlowU64_*` constants plus `CONTROL_FLOW_CONTINUE_U64`, `CONTROL_FLOW_BREAK_U64`, `CONTROL_FLOW_TAG`, `CONTROL_FLOW_VALUE_U64`, `CONTROL_FLOW_IS_CONTINUE`, `CONTROL_FLOW_IS_BREAK`, `CONTROL_FLOW_BREAK_VALUE_U64`, `CONTROL_FLOW_CONTINUE_VALUE_U64`, `CONTROL_FLOW_BREAK_OK_U64`, `CONTROL_FLOW_CONTINUE_OK_U64`, `CONTROL_FLOW_MAP_BREAK_U64`, `CONTROL_FLOW_MAP_CONTINUE_U64`, and `CONTROL_FLOW_INTO_VALUE_U64` record Rust `ControlFlow`'s Continue/Break tag order and concrete u64 method lowering.
*   **Missing from Rust**: operator overloading traits (`Add`, `Sub`, `Index`, `Deref`, `Drop`, etc.), generic `RangeBounds<T>`, the real `Step` trait, generic range iterators beyond concrete `u64` cursor mutation, slicing trait integration, generic `ControlFlow<B, C>`, `Try` / `FromResidual` integration, and all borrow/type-system semantics.
*   **Scope note**: The SA ops facade is a primitive range/bound/control-flow contract over `u64` and `Slice<u64>` views. Finite `Range<u64>` / `RangeInclusive<u64>` cursor helpers mutate the stored start/end/exhausted fields to model common Rust consuming iteration, including double-ended consumption and count exhaustion. Concrete RangeBounds/IntoBounds helpers materialize `BoundU64` / `BoundUsize` values and mirror Rust's `RangeInclusive` exhausted-end behavior by returning `Excluded(end)` after exhaustion. `BOUND_*_AS_REF` and `BOUND_*_AS_MUT` materialize concrete tag + pointer `Bound*Ref` layouts by pointing Included/Excluded at the original payload slot and using a null payload for Unbounded; `BOUND_*_REF_COPIED` / `BOUND_*_REF_CLONED` read those payload pointers back into value bounds. These are pointer-layout subsets and do not claim Rust borrow lifetimes or aliasing enforcement. `BOUND_*_RANGE_IS_EMPTY` mirrors Rust's bound-pair empty rule: unbounded endpoints are never empty, Included/Included uses `start <= end`, and all other bounded combinations use `start < end`. `BOUND_*_INTERSECT_START`, `BOUND_*_INTERSECT_END`, and `BOUND_*_INTERSECT_RANGE` mirror Rust's `IntoBounds::intersect` bound selection rules for concrete bounds: start intersections choose the stricter/larger lower bound, end intersections choose the stricter/smaller upper bound, and equal-value Included/Excluded pairs keep Excluded as the stricter edge. `BOUND_*_MAP` calls a concrete `u64 -> u64` function only for Included/Excluded payloads and preserves the original variant tag, while Unbounded bypasses the callback. `ControlFlowU64` is a tag + single `u64` payload slot, with `Continue = 0` and `Break = 1`, matching Rust's documented no-op conversion shape with `Result` at the variant-order level. The `ControlFlow` value/result helpers return existing concrete `Option` / `Result` layouts, `map_break` / `map_continue` call a concrete `u64 -> u64` function only on the targeted variant, and `into_value` is valid for the concrete same-payload `ControlFlow<u64, u64>` subset. It gives frontends concrete layouts for common Rust lowering, but does not implement Rust's generic `std::ops` trait system, `Step`, `Try`, residual conversions, or language-level range syntax.

### 1.1j FFI C strings (`std::ffi::CStr` subset vs `sa_std/ffi.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: borrowed C string view layout aliases over `Slice`, `CSTR_LEN`, `CSTR_FROM_PTR`, `CSTR_FROM_PARTS_UNCHECKED`, `CSTR_FROM_BYTES_WITH_NUL`, `CSTR_VALIDATE_WITH_NUL_DETAIL`, `CSTR_FROM_BYTES_UNTIL_NUL`, `CSTR_COUNT_BYTES`, `CSTR_IS_EMPTY`, `CSTR_TO_BYTES`, `CSTR_TO_BYTES_WITH_NUL`, `CSTR_TO_STR`, borrowed C string hash helpers (`DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_CSTR`, `CSTR_HASH`, `BUILD_HASHER_DEFAULT_HASH_ONE_CSTR`, `RANDOM_STATE_HASH_ONE_CSTR`), and an owned `CString` layout subset with `CSTRING_NEW`, `CSTRING_DEFAULT`, `CSTRING_LIT1`, `CSTRING_CLONE`, `CSTRING_FROM_BYTES_WITH_NUL`, `CSTRING_FROM_BYTES_WITH_NUL_UNCHECKED`, `CSTRING_AS_CSTR`, `CSTRING_AS_PTR`, `CSTRING_COUNT_BYTES`, `CSTRING_IS_EMPTY`, `CSTRING_AS_BYTES`, `CSTRING_AS_BYTES_WITH_NUL`, `CSTRING_TO_STR`, `CSTRING_TO_STRING_LOSSY`, `CSTR_TO_STRING_LOSSY`, owned C string hash helpers (`DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_CSTRING`, `CSTRING_HASH`, `BUILD_HASHER_DEFAULT_HASH_ONE_CSTRING`, `RANDOM_STATE_HASH_ONE_CSTRING`), `CSTRING_INTO_RAW`, `CSTRING_FROM_RAW`, and `CSTRING_FREE`.
*   **Missing from Rust**: full owned `CString` parity beyond the concrete byte-buffer subset, Windows/`WTF-8` `OsStr` / `OsString` parity beyond the concrete Unix byte-buffer facade, platform-specific `std::os::*` string extensions, lossy conversion methods beyond owned `*_TO_STRING_LOSSY` StringBuf helpers, trait impls, pointer lifetime/aliasing contracts, allocator-aware APIs, and Rust's full ownership/drop integration.
*   **Scope note**: The `CStr` and `CString` layouts use the same `(ptr, len_with_nul)` view contract as `Slice`; `CString` owns heap bytes allocated by `CSTRING_NEW`, `CSTRING_FROM_BYTES_WITH_NUL`, or `CSTRING_FROM_BYTES_WITH_NUL_UNCHECKED` until `CSTRING_FREE` or `CSTRING_INTO_RAW`. Validation returns explicit status codes instead of Rust `Result` values. `CSTR_VALIDATE_WITH_NUL_DETAIL` mirrors the validation status from `CSTR_FROM_BYTES_WITH_NUL` while also returning the first interior NUL byte index, the missing terminator position (`len`), or `CSTR_ERROR_POS_NONE` on success. `CSTRING_NEW` mirrors Rust's common `CString::new` check by rejecting any NUL byte in the input slice and appending one terminator on success. `CSTRING_FROM_BYTES_WITH_NUL` mirrors Rust's `from_vec_with_nul` validation shape by requiring exactly one trailing NUL and rejecting interior or missing NUL bytes; the unchecked variant copies bytes without revalidating. The FFI error constants now describe lightweight `NulError`, `FromBytesWithNulError`, `Utf8Error`, and `IntoStringError` layouts over the existing status and view contracts, but they do not model Rust's original-Vec ownership recovery, trait impls, Display/Debug text, or drop behavior. `CSTR_TO_STR` removes the trailing NUL and validates UTF-8 before returning a borrowed `Slice` view; `CSTRING_TO_STR` delegates through the borrowed `CStr` view. Invalid UTF-8 returns `CSTR_TO_STR_INVALID_UTF8` and an empty slice rather than materializing a full Rust error value. The CStr/CString hash helpers hash the bytes-with-NUL view through deterministic `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_SLICE_U8` / `HASH_SLICE_U8`, modeling the supportable backing-byte shape while not claiming platform `c_char` signedness nuance, generic trait dispatch, randomized `RandomState`, or SipHash output compatibility. It intentionally avoids plugin APIs and platform-specific OS string semantics.

### 1.1k Marker primitives (`std::marker` subset vs `sa_std/marker.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: `PhantomData` / `PhantomPinned` zero-sized layout constants, cross-module default aliases (`DEFAULT_PHANTOM_DATA`, `DEFAULT_PHANTOM_PINNED`), default/copy/clone/clone_from/equality/ordering helper macros (`PHANTOM_DATA_DEFAULT`, `PHANTOM_DATA_COPY`, `PHANTOM_DATA_CLONE`, `PHANTOM_DATA_CLONE_FROM`, `PHANTOM_DATA_EQ`, `PHANTOM_DATA_NE`, `PHANTOM_DATA_CMP`, `PHANTOM_DATA_PARTIAL_CMP`, `PHANTOM_DATA_LT`, `PHANTOM_DATA_LE`, `PHANTOM_DATA_GT`, `PHANTOM_DATA_GE`, plus the matching `PHANTOM_PINNED_*` helpers), marker hash helpers (`DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_PHANTOM_DATA`, `PHANTOM_DATA_HASH`, `BUILD_HASHER_DEFAULT_HASH_ONE_PHANTOM_DATA`, `RANDOM_STATE_HASH_ONE_PHANTOM_DATA`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_PHANTOM_PINNED`, `PHANTOM_PINNED_HASH`, `BUILD_HASHER_DEFAULT_HASH_ONE_PHANTOM_PINNED`, `RANDOM_STATE_HASH_ONE_PHANTOM_PINNED`), `PHANTOM_DATA_IS_*` and `PHANTOM_PINNED_IS_*` capability helpers, primitive marker trait query helpers (`MARKER_PRIMITIVE_IS_SIZED`, `MARKER_PRIMITIVE_IS_COPY`, `MARKER_PRIMITIVE_IS_SEND`, `MARKER_PRIMITIVE_IS_SYNC`, `MARKER_PRIMITIVE_IS_UNPIN`, `MARKER_PRIMITIVE_IS_FREEZE`), and generic `MARKER_ZST_*` / `MARKER_CAPABILITY` helpers.
*   **Missing from Rust**: the actual trait solver and auto-trait system, user-defined `Send` / `Sync` / `Unpin` inference, negative impls, derive machinery, `Sized` / `Copy` compiler semantics, `StructuralPartialEq`, `DiscriminantKind`, `Freeze`, `UnsafeUnpin`, and drop-check/lifetime effects from `PhantomData<T>`.
*   **Scope note**: This facade is a frontend lowering contract, not a Rust trait implementation. It gives SA code stable constants and helper macros for marker-like lowering, while real Rust marker semantics remain a compiler/frontend responsibility. The marker copy/clone/default/comparison helpers model only the supportable no-op/ZST shape: marker token inputs are ignored, equality is always true, strict ordering is always false, and comparison is always equal. The marker hash helpers likewise model only the supportable no-op/ZST shape: `PhantomData<T>` does not write into the hasher and `PhantomPinned` hashes as a zero-field derived value. They do not model generic marker trait dispatch, drop-check/lifetime effects, full pinning semantics, randomized `RandomState`, or SipHash output compatibility.

### 1.1l Pin primitives (`std::pin` subset vs `sa_std/pin.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: transparent pointer-layout wrappers for `Pin` and `PinMut`, plus `PIN_NEW`, `PIN_NEW_UNCHECKED`, `PIN_MUT_NEW`, `PIN_MUT_NEW_UNCHECKED`, `PIN_GET_REF`, `PIN_MUT_GET_REF`, `PIN_MUT_GET_MUT`, `PIN_MUT_GET_UNCHECKED_MUT`, `PIN_AS_REF`, `PIN_MUT_AS_REF`, `PIN_MUT_AS_MUT`, `PIN_MUT_INTO_REF`, `PIN_INTO_INNER_UNCHECKED`, `PIN_MUT_INTO_INNER_UNCHECKED`, `PIN_PTR_EQ`, and `PIN_MUT_PTR_EQ`.
*   **Missing from Rust**: real `Pin<Ptr>` generic type semantics, borrow-checker enforced immovability, projection safety, `pin!`, `Unpin` inference/trait integration, destructor/pinning guarantees, and safe APIs that depend on Rust's type system.
*   **Scope note**: This is a transparent pointer wrapper contract for frontend lowering and SA runtime tests. It intentionally does not claim Rust's pinning guarantees; frontends must enforce safety and only lower into these macros once their own type/lifetime analysis has accepted the operation.

### 1.1m Dynamic typing primitives (`std::any` subset vs `sa_std/any.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: concrete `TypeId` layout, primitive type-id constructor macros (`TYPE_ID_BOOL`, `TYPE_ID_U8`, `TYPE_ID_U16`, `TYPE_ID_U32`, `TYPE_ID_U64`, `TYPE_ID_I8`, `TYPE_ID_I16`, `TYPE_ID_I32`, `TYPE_ID_I64`, `TYPE_ID_PTR`, `TYPE_ID_SLICE`, `TYPE_ID_STRING`, `TYPE_ID_VEC`), `TYPE_ID_NEW`, `TYPE_ID_LO`, `TYPE_ID_HI`, `TYPE_ID_EQ`, `AnyRef` layout, `ANY_REF_NEW`, `ANY_REF_TYPE_ID`, `ANY_REF_IS`, and `ANY_REF_DOWNCAST_PTR`.
*   **Missing from Rust**: compiler-generated globally unique `TypeId::of::<T>()`, real `Any` blanket trait implementation, `dyn Any` trait objects, safe generic `downcast_ref` / `downcast_mut` / boxed downcast APIs, `Send` / `Sync` forwarding impls, reflection over arbitrary user-defined types, and Rust's layout/variance constraints around `TypeId`.
*   **Scope note**: This is a frontend lowering contract. Frontends assign stable concrete type IDs and lower accepted dynamic checks into the SA layout; the SA macros do not infer Rust types or implement trait-object semantics.

### 1.1n Borrow and clone-on-write primitives (`std::borrow` subset vs `sa_std/borrow.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: `BorrowRef`, `BorrowMut`, and `CowSlice` layouts, plus `BORROW_REF_NEW`, `BORROW_REF_GET`, `BORROW_MUT_NEW`, `BORROW_MUT_GET`, `BORROW_PTR`, `BORROW_MUT_PTR`, `COW_SLICE_BORROWED`, `COW_SLICE_OWNED`, `COW_SLICE_IS_BORROWED`, `COW_SLICE_IS_OWNED`, `COW_SLICE_AS_SLICE`, `COW_SLICE_TO_MUT`, `COW_SLICE_INTO_OWNED`, `COW_SLICE_LEN`, `COW_SLICE_PTR`, and concrete CowSlice `u64` hash forwarding helpers (`DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_COW_SLICE_U64`, `COW_SLICE_HASH_U64`, `BUILD_HASHER_DEFAULT_HASH_ONE_COW_SLICE_U64`, `RANDOM_STATE_HASH_ONE_COW_SLICE_U64`).
*   **Missing from Rust**: trait-level `Borrow`, `BorrowMut`, `ToOwned`, generic `Cow<'a, B>`, clone-on-write allocation/cloning, `Deref`, comparison/display impls, string-specific `Cow<str>` operations, and lifetime/borrow checking.
*   **Scope note**: `CowSlice` is a concrete slice-view contract. Borrowed variants intentionally cannot be converted to mutable/owned without frontend or allocator support; `TO_MUT` / `INTO_OWNED` succeed only for already-owned views. The CowSlice hash helpers view either tag as a `Slice<u64>` and hash the contents, not the Cow tag or pointer address, matching the supportable part of Rust's `Hash::hash(&**self, state)` shape.

### 1.1n2 Cell primitives (`std::cell::Cell` / `RefCell` subset vs `sa_std/core/cell.sa`, `sa_std/core/refcell.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: legacy 4-byte `Cell` helpers (`CELL_NEW`, `CELL_GET`, `CELL_SET`, `CELL_REPLACE`), concrete `CellU64` layout and Rust-style helpers (`CELL_U64_NEW`, `CELL_U64_DEFAULT`, `CELL_U64_GET`, `CELL_U64_SET`, `CELL_U64_REPLACE`, `CELL_U64_TAKE`, `CELL_U64_SWAP`, `CELL_U64_UPDATE`, `CELL_U64_REPLACE_WITH`, `CELL_U64_INTO_INNER`, `CELL_U64_AS_PTR`, `CELL_U64_GET_MUT_PTR`, `CELL_U64_FROM_MUT_PTR`), legacy `RefCell` shared/exclusive borrow counters (`REFCELL_NEW`, `REFCELL_BORROW`, `REFCELL_BORROW_MUT`, `REFCELL_RELEASE`, `REFCELL_GET`, `REFCELL_SET`), concrete non-overlapping `RefCellU64` helpers (`REFCELL_U64_NEW / REFCELL_U64_DEFAULT`, `REFCELL_U64_BORROW_COUNT`, `REFCELL_U64_IS_BORROWED`, `REFCELL_U64_TRY_BORROW`, `REFCELL_U64_TRY_BORROW_MUT`, `REFCELL_U64_RELEASE_SHARED`, `REFCELL_U64_RELEASE_MUT`, `REFCELL_U64_GET`, `REFCELL_U64_SET`, `REFCELL_U64_REPLACE`, `REFCELL_U64_TRY_REPLACE`, `REFCELL_U64_TAKE`, `REFCELL_U64_TRY_TAKE`, `REFCELL_U64_SWAP`, `REFCELL_U64_TRY_SWAP`, `REFCELL_U64_UPDATE`, `REFCELL_U64_REPLACE_WITH`, `REFCELL_U64_TRY_REPLACE_WITH`, `REFCELL_U64_INTO_INNER`, `REFCELL_U64_AS_PTR`, `REFCELL_U64_GET_MUT_PTR`), and zero-sized `BorrowError` / `BorrowMutError` layout constants.
*   **Missing from Rust**: generic `Cell<T>` / `RefCell<T>`, `Ref` / `RefMut` guard objects, borrow panic payload parity, Rust `Result<Ref<_>, BorrowError>` / `Result<RefMut<_>, BorrowMutError>` integration, generic `take` defaults, slice/cell projection APIs, `UnsafeCell`, borrow-checker/lifetime integration, and aliasing guarantees enforced by Rust's type system.
*   **Scope note**: `CellU64` and `RefCellU64` are concrete lowering contracts over 8-byte payloads. `CellU64::replace_with` is modeled as a macro callback from old value to new value. `RefCellU64` returns explicit status flags and raw value pointers for successful borrows; callers must release shared or mutable borrows explicitly. The `TRY_*` mutation helpers fail with `ok=0` when the borrow counter is nonzero and do not panic. `BorrowError` and `BorrowMutError` are recorded only as zero-sized layout markers; the current macros do not construct Rust `Result` error objects or implement Display/Debug text. `GET_MUT_PTR` assumes the caller has exclusive access to the cell object, matching the primitive SA lowering contract rather than Rust's borrow-checked `&mut self` proof. It does not expose Rust guard lifetimes or panic payloads.

### 1.1n3 Reference counting primitives (`std::rc::{Rc, Weak}` / `std::sync::{Arc, Weak}` subset vs `sa_std/core/rc.sa`, `sa_std/core/arc.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: concrete `RcBox` layout with strong count, weak count, and `u64` payload; `RC_NEW`, `RC_DEFAULT_U64`, `RC_CLONE`, `RC_DROP`, `RC_GET`, `RC_AS_PTR`, `RC_INTO_RAW`, `RC_FROM_RAW`, `RC_INCREMENT_STRONG_COUNT`, `RC_DECREMENT_STRONG_COUNT`, `RC_STRONG_COUNT`, `RC_WEAK_COUNT`, `RC_PTR_EQ`, `RC_GET_MUT`, `RC_TRY_UNWRAP`, `RC_MAKE_MUT_U64`, `RC_UNWRAP_OR_CLONE_U64`, `RC_DOWNGRADE`, `WEAK_NEW`, `WEAK_DEFAULT`, `WEAK_CLONE`, `WEAK_DROP`, `WEAK_UPGRADE`, `WEAK_STRONG_COUNT`, `WEAK_WEAK_COUNT`, and `WEAK_PTR_EQ`.
*   **Implemented in `sa_std` (Arc)**: concrete `ArcBox` layout with atomic strong/weak counts and `u64` payload; `ARC_NEW`, `ARC_DEFAULT_U64`, `ARC_CLONE`, `ARC_DROP`, `ARC_GET`, `ARC_AS_PTR`, `ARC_INTO_RAW`, `ARC_FROM_RAW`, `ARC_INCREMENT_STRONG_COUNT`, `ARC_DECREMENT_STRONG_COUNT`, `ARC_STRONG_COUNT`, `ARC_WEAK_COUNT`, `ARC_PTR_EQ`, `ARC_GET_MUT`, `ARC_TRY_UNWRAP`, `ARC_MAKE_MUT_U64`, `ARC_UNWRAP_OR_CLONE_U64`, `ARC_DOWNGRADE`, `ARC_WEAK_NEW`, `ARC_WEAK_DEFAULT`, `ARC_WEAK_CLONE`, `ARC_WEAK_DROP`, `ARC_WEAK_UPGRADE`, `ARC_WEAK_STRONG_COUNT`, `ARC_WEAK_WEAK_COUNT`, and `ARC_WEAK_PTR_EQ`.
*   **Additional implemented Rc/Arc hash subset**: `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_RC_U64`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_ARC_U64`, `HASH_RC_U64`, `HASH_ARC_U64`, `BUILD_HASHER_DEFAULT_HASH_ONE_RC_U64`, `BUILD_HASHER_DEFAULT_HASH_ONE_ARC_U64`, `RANDOM_STATE_HASH_ONE_RC_U64`, and `RANDOM_STATE_HASH_ONE_ARC_U64` model Rust's `Hash for Rc<T>` / `Hash for Arc<T>` forwarding shape for concrete stored `u64` payloads.
*   **Missing from Rust**: generic `Rc<T>` / `Arc<T>` / `Weak<T>`, allocator-aware APIs, unsized coercions, `new_cyclic`, real `Drop`/deallocation sequencing, `make_mut` clone-on-write allocation for shared values, `try_unwrap` returning ownership of `T`, weak raw pointer APIs, `new_uninit`, `pin`, panic/drop safety, and Rust's aliasing/lifetime/thread-safety guarantees.
*   **Scope note**: These are concrete reference-counting contracts over an in-place `u64` payload. `RC_WEAK_COUNT`, `WEAK_WEAK_COUNT`, `ARC_WEAK_COUNT`, and `ARC_WEAK_WEAK_COUNT` report explicit user weak handles and subtract the implicit sentinel weak count. Cleanup/free labels remain caller-managed; these macros do not own allocator behavior or run destructors. `Arc` uses SA atomic operations for the counters, traps on strong/weak count overflow during clone/downgrade/upgrade-style increments, but does not claim Rust's full synchronization or destructor semantics. The Rc/Arc hash helpers load the stored payload and hash that value rather than the allocation address, matching the supportable part of Rust's `(**self).hash(state)` shape; they do not model generic `T`, weak pointer hashing, or trait dispatch.

*   **Scope note**: The SA `Box` contract is a verifier-friendly heap cell for one `u64` value. Raw/leak helpers expose the payload pointer for frontend lowering and tests; ownership, destructor, and allocation-safety rules remain caller/frontend responsibilities.

*   **Additional implemented Wrapping/Saturating bit-transform subset**: `WRAPPING_U64_ROTATE_LEFT`, `WRAPPING_U64_ROTATE_RIGHT`, `WRAPPING_U64_REVERSE_BITS`, `WRAPPING_U64_SWAP_BYTES`, `WRAPPING_U64_TO_BE`, `WRAPPING_U64_FROM_BE`, `WRAPPING_U64_TO_LE`, `WRAPPING_U64_FROM_LE`, matching `WrappingU32` / `WrappingI64` helpers, and the corresponding `SATURATING_U64_*` / `SATURATING_I64_*` helpers model Rust's inherent rotate, reverse-bits, byte-swap, and endian conversion forwarding methods for the existing concrete transparent wrapper layouts.
*   **Scope note (Wrapping/Saturating bit transforms)**: the Wrapping/Saturating bit-transform helpers load the concrete inner primitive, apply the matching primitive bit-pattern transform, and write a destination wrapper. `WrappingU32` rotates and reverses within the concrete 32-bit width. They do not model generic `Wrapping<T>` / `Saturating<T>`, wrapper widths not represented in current SA layouts, `u128` / `i128`, Rust feature-gate handling, or generic trait dispatch.
*   **Additional implemented Wrapping/Saturating operator subset**: `WRAPPING_{U64,U32,I64}_{ADD,SUB,MUL}`, `WRAPPING_{U64,U32,I64}_DIV`, `*_REM`, `*_SHL`, `*_SHR`, `*_NOT`, `*_BITAND`, `*_BITOR`, and `*_BITXOR`, plus `WRAPPING_I64_NEG`, `WRAPPING_I64_ABS`, `WRAPPING_I64_SIGNUM`, `WRAPPING_I64_IS_POSITIVE`, `WRAPPING_I64_IS_NEGATIVE`, matching signed unary/sign helpers for `SaturatingI64`, `SATURATING_{U64,I64}_{ADD,SUB,MUL,DIV,REM}`, `SATURATING_{U64,I64}_NOT`, `*_BITAND`, `*_BITOR`, and `*_BITXOR`, model the supportable Rust operator and inherent signed helper shapes over the existing concrete transparent wrapper layouts.
*   **Scope note (Wrapping/Saturating operators)**: the Wrapping operator helpers load the concrete inner primitive, apply existing primitive wrapping or bit-pattern operations, and write a destination wrapper. `WrappingU32` masks operands/results and raw `SET` values to the concrete 32-bit width; `WrappingI64` uses signed add/sub/mul/div/remainder and arithmetic right shift while preserving the 64-bit raw setter shape. `SaturatingI64` add/sub/mul forward to signed saturating primitive helpers, its unary helpers forward to signed saturating neg/abs and signed predicates, and its division covers the Rust saturating `MIN / -1 -> MAX` branch. This subset does not model generic operator traits, assignment operators, missing wrapper widths, `u128` / `i128`, or Rust panic object behavior for Saturating div/rem zero divisors.
*   **Additional implemented Wrapping/Saturating hash subset**: `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_WRAPPING_U64`, `WRAPPING_U64_HASH`, `BUILD_HASHER_DEFAULT_HASH_ONE_WRAPPING_U64`, `RANDOM_STATE_HASH_ONE_WRAPPING_U64`, matching `WrappingU32` / `WrappingI64` helpers, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_SATURATING_U64`, `SATURATING_U64_HASH`, `BUILD_HASHER_DEFAULT_HASH_ONE_SATURATING_U64`, `RANDOM_STATE_HASH_ONE_SATURATING_U64`, and matching `SaturatingI64` helpers model Rust's derived single-field `Hash` shape for the existing concrete transparent wrapper layouts.
*   **Scope note (Wrapping/Saturating hash)**: the Wrapping/Saturating hash helpers load the concrete inner primitive and reuse the matching deterministic primitive writer. They do not model generic `Wrapping<T>` / `Saturating<T>`, wrapper widths not represented in current SA layouts, generic trait dispatch, randomized `RandomState`, or SipHash output compatibility.
*   **Additional implemented marker hash subset**: `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_PHANTOM_DATA`, `PHANTOM_DATA_HASH`, `BUILD_HASHER_DEFAULT_HASH_ONE_PHANTOM_DATA`, `RANDOM_STATE_HASH_ONE_PHANTOM_DATA`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_PHANTOM_PINNED`, `PHANTOM_PINNED_HASH`, `BUILD_HASHER_DEFAULT_HASH_ONE_PHANTOM_PINNED`, and `RANDOM_STATE_HASH_ONE_PHANTOM_PINNED` model Rust's zero-sized/no-op `Hash` shape for SA's marker token surface.
*   **Scope note (marker hash)**: the marker hash helpers delegate to the unit no-op hash path, so marker token values are ignored and writing a marker leaves the deterministic hasher state unchanged. They do not model generic `PhantomData<T>` type/lifetime effects, Rust's marker trait solver, full pinning semantics, randomized `RandomState`, or SipHash output compatibility.
*   **Additional implemented Reverse hash subset**: `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_CMP_REVERSE_U64`, `CMP_REVERSE_U64_HASH`, `BUILD_HASHER_DEFAULT_HASH_ONE_CMP_REVERSE_U64`, `RANDOM_STATE_HASH_ONE_CMP_REVERSE_U64`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_CMP_REVERSE_I64`, `CMP_REVERSE_I64_HASH`, `BUILD_HASHER_DEFAULT_HASH_ONE_CMP_REVERSE_I64`, and `RANDOM_STATE_HASH_ONE_CMP_REVERSE_I64` model Rust's transparent single-field `Hash` shape for SA's concrete `Reverse<u64>` and `Reverse<i64>` layouts.
*   **Scope note (Reverse hash)**: the Reverse hash helpers load the concrete inner primitive and reuse the matching deterministic primitive writer, matching Rust's derived `Hash` field-forwarding shape. They do not model generic `Reverse<T>`, comparison trait dispatch, randomized `RandomState`, or SipHash output compatibility.
*   **Additional implemented ManuallyDrop hash subset**: `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_MANUALLY_DROP_U64`, `MANUALLY_DROP_U64_HASH`, `BUILD_HASHER_DEFAULT_HASH_ONE_MANUALLY_DROP_U64`, and `RANDOM_STATE_HASH_ONE_MANUALLY_DROP_U64` model Rust's `Hash for ManuallyDrop<T>` forwarding shape for SA's concrete `ManuallyDropU64` layout.
*   **Scope note (ManuallyDrop hash)**: the ManuallyDrop hash helpers load the inner `u64` and reuse the deterministic primitive writer. They do not model generic `ManuallyDrop<T>`, `MaybeUninit<T>` hashing, initialization validity, Rust drop-suppression safety, randomized `RandomState`, or SipHash output compatibility.
*   **Missing from Rust**: trait-level `Hash`, `Hasher`, `BuildHasher`, `BuildHasherDefault<H>` generics, `DefaultHasher` `Debug` formatting, derive `Hash`, stable prefix-free hashing contracts across all Rust primitive/container types, OS-random `RandomState` key generation, SipHash compatibility, endian/type-size nuance, and collection integration through Rust traits.
*   **Scope note**: SA's `DefaultHasher` is deterministic and macro-local. It provides a stable state machine for frontend lowering and tests, not Rust's randomized default hasher or exact SipHash output. `DEFAULT_HASHER_CLONE` copies the current single-word state into an independent hasher, modeling the supportable behavior of Rust's derived `Clone` without exposing SipHasher internals. `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE` is the Rust-named `Hasher::write(&[u8])` surface over the existing byte-slice writer and intentionally performs no length-prefixing; sequence-prefixing remains explicit through `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_LENGTH_PREFIX` and the slice helpers. `HASH_U8`, `HASH_U16`, `HASH_U32`, `HASH_U64`, `HASH_USIZE`, `HASH_I8`, `HASH_I16`, `HASH_I32`, `HASH_I64`, and `HASH_ISIZE` use their matching `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_*` paths; the signed helpers preserve the current SA forwarding shape to same-width unsigned/register-sized writers and do not provide `u128` / `i128`. `HASH_BOOL` uses `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_BOOL`, which normalizes nonzero inputs to true for frontend-lowered booleans; `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_CHAR` and `HASH_CHAR` model Rust's `Hash for char` write shape by forwarding a frontend-provided valid scalar through `write_u32`. `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_UNIT`, `HASH_UNIT`, and the unit `hash_one` helpers model Rust's `Hash for ()` as a no-op; their placeholder unit argument exists only to satisfy SA macro invocation syntax and is not mixed into state. `HASH_PTR_VALUE` and the pointer `hash_one` helpers model sized raw pointer hashing by writing the pointer address and applying unit metadata hashing as a no-op; they do not model unsized pointer metadata. `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_REF_U64`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_REF_MUT_U64`, `HASH_REF_U64`, `HASH_REF_MUT_U64`, and the reference `hash_one` helpers model concrete `u64` reference forwarding by loading the pointee and hashing that value; they do not model generic `Hash for &T`, Rust borrow/lifetime semantics, or unsized pointee metadata. `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_BOX_U64`, `HASH_BOX_U64`, and the boxed `hash_one` helpers model concrete `Box<u64>` forwarding by loading the boxed value and hashing it, not the box address; they do not model generic `Box<T>`, allocator parameters, drop glue, or boxed `Hasher` forwarding. `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_RC_U64`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_ARC_U64`, `HASH_RC_U64`, `HASH_ARC_U64`, and the Rc/Arc `hash_one` helpers model concrete reference-counted `u64` forwarding by loading the stored value and hashing it, not the allocation address; they do not model generic `Rc<T>` / `Arc<T>`, weak pointer hashing, allocator parameters, drop glue, or thread-safety semantics beyond the existing concrete Arc payload. `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_TUPLE2_U64`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_TUPLE3_U64`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_TUPLE4_U64`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_TUPLE5_U64`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_TUPLE6_U64`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_TUPLE7_U64`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_TUPLE8_U64`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_TUPLE9_U64`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_TUPLE10_U64`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_TUPLE11_U64`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_TUPLE12_U64`, `HASH_TUPLE2_U64`, `HASH_TUPLE3_U64`, `HASH_TUPLE4_U64`, `HASH_TUPLE5_U64`, `HASH_TUPLE6_U64`, `HASH_TUPLE7_U64`, `HASH_TUPLE8_U64`, `HASH_TUPLE9_U64`, `HASH_TUPLE10_U64`, `HASH_TUPLE11_U64`, `HASH_TUPLE12_U64`, and the tuple hash_one helpers model concrete Rust tuple `Hash` shapes by writing `u64` fields in order; they do not implement generic tuple trait dispatch, tuple arities beyond the concrete helpers, or non-`u64` elements. These macros do not validate Rust type invariants or implement generic `Hash` dispatch. The signed write aliases model Rust's default `Hasher::write_i*` / `write_isize` forwarding shape over the current register-sized deterministic hash writer, not byte-for-byte Rust endian serialization. `BuildHasherDefault` is represented as a zero value; clone/copy recreate that zero-sized marker and equality always returns true, matching Rust's ZST marker behavior without generic type parameters. The `BUILD_HASHER_DEFAULT_HASH_ONE_*` and `RANDOM_STATE_HASH_ONE_*` helpers model Rust's default `BuildHasher::hash_one` shape for concrete values by building a fresh SA `DefaultHasher`, writing exactly one concrete value/slice/str/unit/sized-pointer/tuple/reference/box/reference-counted pointer, and finishing it. `RandomState` is represented as two explicit deterministic `u64` seed fields; `RANDOM_STATE_NEW` / `DEFAULT` use stable SA constants, `RANDOM_STATE_WITH_SEEDS` lets frontends provide distinct seed pairs, and `RANDOM_STATE_BUILD` derives the local `DefaultHasher` state from those fields. This models the supportable builder shape without Rust's per-thread OS-random key cache, key increment behavior, SipHash implementation, or randomized `HashMap` iteration guarantees.

*   **Scope note**: The current iterator facade is a concrete `u64` cursor over a `Slice` view. Consuming helpers update `Iter_index` and, for back traversal, `Iter_len` as the rear cursor. `ITER_BY_REF` returns a borrowed pointer alias to the same cursor storage so subsequent consumption mutates the original cursor, but it does not model Rust references or lifetimes. `ITER_SIZE_HINT` returns an exact `(lower, upper)` pair because the cursor has a known remaining length, while `ITER_EXACT_SIZE_LEN` / `ITER_EXACT_SIZE_IS_EMPTY` are Rust-named aliases over the same exact remaining-length state. `ITER_ADVANCE_BY` and `ITER_ADVANCE_BACK_BY` report explicit `(ok, remaining)` values rather than Rust's `Result<(), NonZero<usize>>`. `ITER_TRY_NEXT_CHUNK_U64` writes up to `%count` `u64` values into caller-provided storage, returns `(ok, filled)`, succeeds when exactly `%count` values were copied, treats a zero count as success without consuming, and consumes any copied short remainder on failure instead of materializing Rust's `Err(IntoIter<...>)` object. `ITER_TRY_PEEK_U64` returns `(has, value)` without consuming the cursor, modeling the supportable part of `Peekable::peek` without returning Rust references. Predicate, fold, reduce, for-each, compare, sortedness, partition checks, and key helpers call concrete SA functions and short-circuit where Rust does, but they materialize no lazy adapter structs and do not model Rust generic trait dispatch or closure capture. `ITER_TRY_FIND_U64` calls a concrete `(value, out_match_ptr) -> ok` callback, returns `(ok, has, value)`, consumes the matching or failing item, and leaves later suffix elements available. `ITER_FIND_MAP_U64` calls a concrete `value, out_mapped_ptr -> u64` callback and returns the first mapped value without sentinel ambiguity. `ITER_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_TRY_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_BY_REF_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_FUSE_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_PEEKABLE_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_ARRAY_CHUNKS_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_MAP_WINDOWS_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_COPIED_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_CLONED_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_COLLECT_INTO_U64`, `ITER_TAKE_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_SKIP_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_INTERSPERSE_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_INTERSPERSE_WITH_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_TRY_STEP_BY_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_STEP_BY_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_CYCLE_TAKE_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_REV_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_TAKE_WHILE_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_SKIP_WHILE_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_MAP_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_INSPECT_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_SCAN_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_FILTER_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_FILTER_MAP_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_MAP_WHILE_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_PARTITION_U64`, `ITER_ENUMERATE_COLLECT_U64`, `ITER_ZIP_COLLECT_U64`, and `ITER_CHAIN_COLLECT_U64` consume cursors and materialize concrete `Vec<u64>` outputs owned by the caller; they model common Rust `collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `try_collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `by_ref().collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `fuse().collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `peekable().collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `array_chunks::<N>().collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `map_windows(...).collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `copied().collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `collect_into(&mut Vec<_>)`, `take(n).collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `skip(n).collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `intersperse(...).collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `intersperse_with(...).collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `step_by(n).collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `cycle().take(n).collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `rev().collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `take_while(...).collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `skip_while(...).collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `map(...).collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `inspect(...).collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `scan(...).collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `filter(...).collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `filter_map(...).collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `map_while(...).collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `partition`, `enumerate().collect::<Vec<_>>()`, `zip(...).collect::<Vec<_>>()`, and `chain(...).collect::<Vec<_>>()` lowering shapes without claiming lazy adapter identity, generic `collect`, or Rust tuple values. `ITER_TRY_COLLECT_U64` calls a concrete `(value, out_value_ptr) -> ok` callback, pushes mapped successes, consumes the first failing item, returns `ok=0`, and leaves any later suffix available; it does not model Rust's generic `Try`, residual conversion, or outer `Option` / `Result` / `ControlFlow` objects. `ITER_BY_REF_COLLECT_U64` delegates through the same pointer alias as `ITER_BY_REF`, so collection mutates the original cursor while prior clones remain independent. `ITER_FUSE_COLLECT_U64` is a collect alias for the already-fused finite cursor; it does not expose a Rust `Fuse` adapter object or wrap non-fused iterators. `ITER_PEEKABLE_COLLECT_U64` is an eager collect alias over the current cursor remainder and does not expose buffered lazy `Peekable` state or `peek_mut`. `ITER_ARRAY_CHUNKS_COLLECT_U64` materializes complete non-overlapping chunks into a flat `Vec<u64>`, reports `ok=0` and leaves the cursor untouched for zero chunk size, consumes only complete nonzero chunks, and leaves an incomplete tail available in the cursor; it does not expose a lazy `ArrayChunks` adapter, const-generic array item values, or Rust's `IntoIter` remainder object. `ITER_MAP_WINDOWS_COLLECT_U64` calls a concrete `(window_ptr, window_len) -> u64` mapper for each overlapping window, consumes the source cursor for nonzero window sizes including short-input empty output, and reports `ok=0` without consuming for zero window size; it does not expose a lazy `MapWindows` adapter, const-generic array references, generic closure capture, or non-fused resume-after-None reset behavior. `ITER_COPIED_COLLECT_U64` and `ITER_CLONED_COLLECT_U64` are concrete value-copy aliases over the `u64` cursor; they do not model Rust's borrowed `&T` item inputs, generic `Copy` / `Clone`, or lifetime semantics. `ITER_INTERSPERSE_COLLECT_U64` and `ITER_INTERSPERSE_WITH_COLLECT_U64` materialize separators between adjacent yielded values and do not model Rust's non-fused resume-after-None behavior or lazy adapter state. `ITER_CYCLE_TAKE_COLLECT_U64` is explicitly bounded: it uses the cursor's current remaining window as the cycle seed, returns empty for empty seeds or zero count, and does not expose an infinite lazy `Cycle` object. `ITER_IS_PARTITIONED_U64` follows Rust's default `all(predicate) || !any(predicate)` shape over the current cursor, so non-partitioned detection consumes through the first later true item and can leave an unchecked suffix. `ITER_PARTITION_IN_PLACE_U64` reorders only the current remaining cursor storage in place with a concrete `u64 -> bool` predicate, returns the number of true values, marks the cursor consumed, and does not preserve relative order; it does not model borrow-scoped `iter_mut`, generic `&mut T` items, or Rust predicate panic/drop behavior. Pair-producing helpers write interleaved `u64` fields into the output vector (`index,value` or `left,right`). `ITER_TAKE_COLLECT_U64` leaves any uncollected suffix in the source cursor, `ITER_SKIP_COLLECT_U64` consumes skipped items before collecting the remainder, `ITER_TRY_STEP_BY_COLLECT_U64` and `ITER_STEP_BY_COLLECT_U64` collect the first item and then every step-th remaining item while consuming skipped items through the finite cursor; the try form reports `ok=0` and leaves the cursor untouched for `step == 0`, `ITER_TAKE_WHILE_COLLECT_U64` consumes but does not collect the first predicate-false item, `ITER_SKIP_WHILE_COLLECT_U64` collects the first predicate-false item and the remaining suffix, `ITER_MAP_WHILE_COLLECT_U64` consumes and maps values until the callback returns None-style zero, consumes that stopping item, and leaves any later suffix available, `ITER_SCAN_COLLECT_U64` keeps a concrete `u64` state slot, calls `(state_ptr, value, out_ptr) -> ok`, consumes the first None-style stopping item, and leaves any later suffix available, and `ITER_ZIP_COLLECT_U64` stops when either cursor ends. `ITER_TRY_FOLD_U64`, `ITER_TRY_RFOLD_U64`, and `ITER_TRY_REDUCE_U64` call concrete `(acc, value, out_next_ptr) -> u64` callbacks, only commit `out_next` when the callback returns nonzero, and leave not-yet-consumed elements available after early stop; `ITER_TRY_FOLD_U64` / `ITER_TRY_RFOLD_U64` return `(ok, acc)`, while `ITER_TRY_REDUCE_U64` returns `(ok, has, value)` so empty-input success can be distinguished from nonempty success. Reverse fold consumes from the back through `ITER_NEXT_BACK_U64`. `ITER_TRY_FOR_EACH_U64` calls a concrete `value -> u64` callback and returns `ok=0` after consuming the first item whose callback returns zero. These helpers model the common short-circuiting shape without Rust's generic `Try` trait, residual values, or error objects. `ITER_RFOLD_U64` is the reverse counterpart to `ITER_FOLD_U64` over the same `(acc, value) -> acc` callback shape. `ITER_IS_SORTED_U64`, `ITER_IS_SORTED_BY_U64`, and `ITER_IS_SORTED_BY_KEY_U64` check the cursor's remaining elements by delegating to the concrete slice sortedness helpers and then mark the cursor empty to model a consuming method surface. `ITER_CMP_U64`, `ITER_CMP_BY_U64`, `ITER_PARTIAL_CMP_U64`, `ITER_PARTIAL_CMP_BY_U64`, and the relation/equality aliases consume two cursors and implement lexicographic `u64` comparison over their remaining elements; `ITER_CMP_BY_U64` uses a concrete `(left, right) -> Ordering` callback, `ITER_PARTIAL_CMP_BY_U64` uses a concrete `(left, right, out_order_ptr) -> ok` callback and returns `(ok, ordering)`, `ITER_EQ_BY_U64` uses a concrete `(left, right) -> bool` callback, and `ITER_PARTIAL_CMP_U64` always returns an `Ordering` value for this concrete totally ordered element subset rather than a Rust `Option<Ordering>`.

### 1.1q Error primitives (`std::error::Error` subset vs `sa_std/error.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: `ErrorRef` layout with type id, numeric code, message byte slice, and optional source pointer; built-in error type ids (`ERROR_TYPE_ID_GENERIC`, `ERROR_TYPE_ID_IO`, `ERROR_TYPE_ID_PARSE`, `ERROR_TYPE_ID_UTF8`, `ERROR_TYPE_ID_FMT`); constructors (`ERROR_REF_NEW`, `ERROR_REF_STATIC`, `ERROR_REF_STATIC_WITH_SOURCE`); field/query helpers (`ERROR_REF_TYPE_ID`, `ERROR_REF_CODE`, `ERROR_REF_MESSAGE`, `ERROR_REF_MESSAGE_PTR`, `ERROR_REF_MESSAGE_LEN`, `ERROR_REF_SOURCE`, `ERROR_REF_HAS_SOURCE`); type checks/downcast pointer (`ERROR_REF_IS`, `ERROR_REF_DOWNCAST_PTR`); and common code predicates (`ERROR_REF_IS_NOT_FOUND`, `ERROR_REF_IS_PERMISSION_DENIED`, `ERROR_REF_IS_INVALID_INPUT`, `ERROR_REF_IS_INVALID_DATA`, `ERROR_REF_IS_TIMED_OUT`, `ERROR_REF_IS_INTERRUPTED`, `ERROR_REF_IS_CONNECTION_REFUSED`, `ERROR_REF_IS_CONNECTION_RESET`, `ERROR_REF_IS_CONNECTION_ABORTED`, `ERROR_REF_IS_NOT_CONNECTED`, `ERROR_REF_IS_ADDR_IN_USE`, `ERROR_REF_IS_ADDR_NOT_AVAILABLE`, `ERROR_REF_IS_BROKEN_PIPE`, `ERROR_REF_IS_ALREADY_EXISTS`, `ERROR_REF_IS_OUT_OF_MEMORY`, `ERROR_REF_IS_NETWORK_UNREACHABLE`, `ERROR_REF_IS_HOST_UNREACHABLE`, `ERROR_REF_IS_IS_A_DIRECTORY`, `ERROR_REF_IS_DIRECTORY_NOT_EMPTY`, `ERROR_REF_IS_READ_ONLY_FILESYSTEM`, `ERROR_REF_IS_RESOURCE_BUSY`, `ERROR_REF_IS_ARGUMENT_LIST_TOO_LONG`, `ERROR_REF_IS_NAME_TOO_LONG`, `ERROR_REF_IS_TOO_MANY_LINKS`, and `ERROR_REF_IS_STORAGE_FULL`).
*   **Missing from Rust**: the real `Error` trait, `dyn Error` trait objects, `Display` / `Debug` formatting integration, `source()` lifetime semantics, `provide` / request APIs, backtrace propagation, boxed error ownership, downcast through Rust's `Any` trait object machinery, and ecosystem error derives such as `thiserror` / `anyhow`.
*   **Scope note**: `ErrorRef` is a static borrowed error descriptor for frontend lowering and diagnostics. It gives SA code a stable way to carry typed error codes, messages, and source chains, but it intentionally does not implement Rust trait dispatch, owned error allocation, formatted payloads, or platform/plugin-specific error systems.

Search, try_for_each, and try-fold/reduce helpers short-circuit by consuming the matching/failing item and leaving only the later suffix; reverse position reports an index relative to the current remaining cursor window. `TRY_NEXT_CHUNK` writes sequential `u64` items into caller storage and reports explicit success/filled counts rather than Rust const-generic array/remainder objects. Partial comparison returns explicit `(ok, ordering)` channels for this concrete totally ordered `u64` subset rather than Rust's `Option<Ordering>` object. `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_PARTITION_U64` materializes caller-owned true/false `Vec<u64>` outputs, and `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_IS_PARTITIONED_U64` preserves the concrete short-circuit suffix behavior of the shared iterator helper. `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_TAKE_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_SKIP_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_REV_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_TRY_STEP_BY_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_STEP_BY_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_MAP_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_INSPECT_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_SCAN_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_FILTER_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_FILTER_MAP_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_MAP_WHILE_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_INTERSPERSE_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_INTERSPERSE_WITH_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_CYCLE_TAKE_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_TAKE_WHILE_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_SKIP_WHILE_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_ENUMERATE_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_ZIP_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_CHAIN_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_BY_REF_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_FUSE_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_PEEKABLE_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_ARRAY_CHUNKS_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_MAP_WINDOWS_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_TRY_COLLECT_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_COLLECT_INTO_U64`, `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_COPIED_COLLECT_U64`, and `VEC_DEQUE_ITER_CLONED_COLLECT_U64` eagerly materialize concrete `Vec<u64>` outputs or append to caller-provided Vecs and do not expose Rust lazy adapter object identity/state; `TRY_COLLECT` returns explicit `ok` plus mapped output and consumes the first failing item, `COPIED` and `CLONED` are concrete value-copy aliases over the `u64` cursor, `ARRAY_CHUNKS` flattens complete chunks and leaves incomplete tails available, `MAP_WINDOWS` calls a concrete `(window_ptr, window_len) -> u64` mapper and consumes valid finite inputs, `BY_REF` mutates the original cursor through the shared helper alias, `FUSE` and `PEEKABLE` collect the current finite cursor remainder without exposing lazy adapter state, `TRY_STEP_BY` reports explicit `ok=0` for step zero without consuming, `CYCLE_TAKE` is finite and leaves the cursor untouched for count zero, `TAKE_WHILE` consumes the first predicate-false item, `SKIP_WHILE` collects the first predicate-false item and suffix, `ENUMERATE` and `ZIP` flatten pair lanes into interleaved `u64` values, `ZIP` stops at the shorter cursor, `CHAIN` consumes left then right, and `SCAN` / `MAP_WHILE` consume the first stopping item while leaving only the later suffix. `VEC_DEQUE_GET_MUT_PTR` / `GET_MUT`, `VEC_DEQUE_FRONT_MUT_PTR` / `BACK_MUT_PTR`, and the mutating push/insert aliases are Rust-named lowerings over the fallible raw-pointer helpers and return explicit `(ok, ptr)` results. `VEC_DEQUE_PUSH_BACK_MUT_PTR`, `VEC_DEQUE_PUSH_FRONT_MUT_PTR`, `VEC_DEQUE_PUSH_BACK_MUT`, `VEC_DEQUE_PUSH_FRONT_MUT`, `VEC_DEQUE_TRY_INSERT_MUT_PTR`, and `VEC_DEQUE_INSERT_MUT` lower Rust's mutating insert-and-borrow convenience methods into raw pointers to the newly inserted `u64` slot; insert miss paths return `(ok=0, null)` rather than Rust's panic object/control flow. These helpers do not model Rust's scoped mutable reference lifetime. `VEC_DEQUE_DRAIN_U64` drains the full deque in logical front-to-back order into a materialized `Vec<u64>` and clears the original deque; it is not Rust's lazy range drain iterator. `VEC_DEQUE_*_POP_*_IF_U64` and `VEC_DEQUE_RETAIN_U64` use caller-supplied `u64 -> u64` predicates and preserve deque order; retain first forces contiguous storage so wrapped queues compact correctly. These are not Rust's borrow-scoped reference, generic, lazy iterator, or allocator error semantics.
