*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: `MAP_NEW`, `MAP_WITH_CAPACITY`, `MAP_TRY_WITH_CAPACITY`, `MAP_FREE`, `MAP_LEN`, `MAP_CAPACITY`, `MAP_EXTRA_CAPACITY`, `MAP_RESERVE`, `MAP_TRY_RESERVE`, `MAP_SHRINK_TO`, `MAP_SHRINK_TO_FIT`, `MAP_IS_EMPTY`, `MAP_CONTAINS_KEY`, `MAP_CLEAR`, `MAP_RETAIN`, `MAP_DRAIN`, `MAP_EXTRACT_IF`, `MAP_PUT`, `MAP_GET`, `MAP_TRY_GET`, `MAP_GET_KEY_VALUE`, `MAP_GET_MUT_PTR`, `MAP_TRY_GET_DISJOINT_MUT_PTRS`, `MAP_INSERT`, `MAP_TRY_INSERT`, `MAP_ENTRY_OR_INSERT`, `MAP_OR_INSERT`, `MAP_ENTRY_OR_DEFAULT`, `MAP_ENTRY_AND_MODIFY`, `MAP_DEL`, `MAP_REMOVE_ENTRY`, `MAP_KEYS`, `MAP_VALUES`, `MAP_VALUES_MUT_PTRS`, `MAP_LIT1`, `MAP_LIT2`, `MAP_LIT3`.
*   **Missing from Rust**: `iter`, `iter_mut`, lazy `drain` / `extract_if` iterator semantics, full `entry` API object model (`VacantEntry`, `OccupiedEntry`, closure `or_insert_with`, key/value ownership variants) beyond the concrete `or_insert` / `or_default` / `and_modify` lowerings, full N-key scoped `get_disjoint_mut`, generic hash/build-hasher support, and allocator-aware constructors.
*   **Safety note**: HashMap capacity growth, reserve length arithmetic, rehash allocation bytes, clear bytes, and eager drain/extract capacity hints now use `NUM_U64_CHECKED_*` and trap before a wrapped byte count can reach allocation or clearing paths.
*   **Scope note**: The SA map remains a concrete pointer-key / pointer-value open-addressing map. `MAP_INSERT` returns an old-value flag plus old value, `MAP_TRY_INSERT` returns an inserted flag plus the stored value-slot pointer, `MAP_ENTRY_OR_INSERT` is the Rust-named entry-or-insert lowering over the same slot-pointer contract, `MAP_REMOVE_ENTRY` returns stored key/value pointers, `MAP_GET_MUT_PTR` returns the slot pointer containing the stored value pointer, and `MAP_TRY_GET_DISJOINT_MUT_PTRS` only covers two distinct pointer keys. `MAP_RETAIN` scans filled slots in place and calls a concrete `key_ptr, value_ptr -> u64` predicate, tombstoning entries whose predicate returns zero. `MAP_DRAIN` clears the map and materializes removed entries as an interleaved `Vec<u64>` of key pointer bits and value pointer bits. `MAP_EXTRACT_IF` calls a concrete `key_ptr, value_ptr -> u64` predicate, tombstones matching entries, returns the remaining length, and materializes removed entries as the same interleaved key/value pointer-bit vector. `MAP_KEYS`, `MAP_VALUES`, and `MAP_VALUES_MUT_PTRS` materialize `Vec<u64>` views of key pointer bits, value pointer bits, or raw value-slot pointer bits. `MAP_INTO_KEYS` and `MAP_INTO_VALUES` materialize the same concrete Vec views and clear the map object; the caller still owns and frees the map storage. These helpers are not Rust iterator, ownership-transfer, or scoped borrow semantics.

### 1.4 Hash Set (`std::collections::HashSet` vs `sa_std/hashset.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: `SET_NEW`, `SET_WITH_CAPACITY`, `SET_TRY_WITH_CAPACITY`, `SET_FREE`, `SET_LEN`, `SET_CAPACITY`, `SET_RESERVE`, `SET_TRY_RESERVE`, `SET_SHRINK_TO`, `SET_SHRINK_TO_FIT`, `SET_IS_EMPTY`, `SET_CLEAR`, `SET_RETAIN`, `SET_DRAIN`, `SET_KEYS`, `SET_INTO_KEYS`, `SET_EXTRACT_IF`, `SET_INSERT`, `SET_CONTAINS`, `SET_GET`, `SET_GET_OR_INSERT`, `SET_ENTRY_GET_OR_INSERT`, `SET_OR_INSERT`, `SET_EXTRA_CAPACITY`, `SET_REPLACE`, `SET_TAKE`, `SET_REMOVE`, `SET_IS_DISJOINT`, `SET_IS_SUBSET`, `SET_IS_SUPERSET`, `SET_UNION`, `SET_INTERSECTION`, `SET_DIFFERENCE`, `SET_SYMMETRIC_DIFFERENCE`, `SET_LIT1`, `SET_LIT2`, `SET_LIT3`.
*   **Missing from Rust**: `iter`, lazy `drain` / `extract_if` iterator semantics, full entry API objects and key ownership variants beyond concrete `get_or_insert`, bit-operator set algebra adapters, generic hash/build-hasher support, and allocator-aware constructors.
*   **Scope note**: The SA set is the pointer-key subset implemented over `sa_std/hashmap.sa` with a sentinel value. `SET_GET`, `SET_GET_OR_INSERT`, `SET_TAKE`, and `SET_REPLACE` return stored key pointers; equality is pointer identity. `SET_GET_OR_INSERT` returns `(inserted, stored_key)` and inserts the pointer key when missing. `SET_RETAIN` scans filled slots in place and calls a concrete `key_ptr -> u64` predicate, tombstoning keys whose predicate returns zero. `SET_KEYS` materializes current keys as a `Vec<u64>` of key pointer bits without consuming the set. `SET_DRAIN` and `SET_INTO_KEYS` clear the set and materialize removed keys as key pointer bits. `SET_EXTRACT_IF` calls a concrete `key_ptr -> u64` predicate, tombstones matching keys, returns the remaining length, and materializes removed keys as key pointer bits. Set relation helpers scan the current slot table and call `SET_CONTAINS` on the opposite set. `SET_UNION`, `SET_INTERSECTION`, `SET_DIFFERENCE`, and `SET_SYMMETRIC_DIFFERENCE` materialize new SA sets instead of returning Rust lazy iterator adapters.

### 1.5 B-Tree Map (`std::collections::BTreeMap` vs `sa_std/btree_map.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: `BTREE_MAP_NEW`, `BTREE_MAP_FREE`, `BTREE_MAP_LEN`, `BTREE_MAP_IS_EMPTY`, `BTREE_MAP_GET`, `BTREE_MAP_TRY_GET`, `BTREE_MAP_GET_KEY_VALUE`, `BTREE_MAP_GET_MUT_PTR`, `BTREE_MAP_FIRST_ENTRY_MUT_PTR`, `BTREE_MAP_LAST_ENTRY_MUT_PTR`, `BTREE_MAP_TRY_GET_DISJOINT_MUT_PTRS`, `BTREE_MAP_FIRST_KEY_VALUE`, `BTREE_MAP_LAST_KEY_VALUE`, `BTREE_MAP_CONTAINS_KEY`, `BTREE_MAP_CLEAR`, `BTREE_MAP_RETAIN`, `BTREE_MAP_REMOVE`, `BTREE_MAP_REMOVE_ENTRY`, `BTREE_MAP_INSERT`, `BTREE_MAP_INSERT_OLD`, `BTREE_MAP_TRY_INSERT`, `BTREE_MAP_ENTRY_OR_INSERT`, `BTREE_MAP_OR_INSERT`, `BTREE_MAP_ENTRY_OR_DEFAULT`, `BTREE_MAP_ENTRY_AND_MODIFY`, `BTREE_MAP_KEYS`, `BTREE_MAP_VALUES`, `BTREE_MAP_INTO_KEYS`, `BTREE_MAP_INTO_VALUES`, `BTREE_MAP_VALUES_MUT_PTRS`, `BTREE_MAP_POP_FIRST`, `BTREE_MAP_POP_LAST`, `BTREE_MAP_APPEND`, `BTREE_MAP_SPLIT_OFF`, `BTREE_MAP_RANGE`, `BTREE_MAP_LIT1`, `BTREE_MAP_LIT2`, `BTREE_MAP_LIT3`.
*   **Additional implemented BTreeMap hash subset**: `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_BTREE_MAP_STR_U64`, `BTREE_MAP_HASH_STR_U64`, `BUILD_HASHER_DEFAULT_HASH_ONE_BTREE_MAP_STR_U64`, and `RANDOM_STATE_HASH_ONE_BTREE_MAP_STR_U64` hash concrete sorted `BTreeMap<str, u64>` entries by length, key, and value.
*   **Missing from Rust**: `range_mut`, full `entry` / `OccupiedEntry` / `VacantEntry` object APIs beyond concrete entry-or-insert / or-default / and-modify lowerings, `iter`, `iter_mut`, lazy `keys` / `values` iterators, generic `Ord` support, allocator-aware constructors, and full mutable-reference borrow semantics.
*   **Scope note**: The SA BTreeMap is a concrete sorted-array map with `Slice` keys and `u64` values. `GET_MUT_PTR`, `FIRST_ENTRY_MUT_PTR`, `LAST_ENTRY_MUT_PTR`, and `TRY_GET_DISJOINT_MUT_PTRS` expose verifier-friendly raw value-slot pointers; `BTREE_MAP_ENTRY_OR_INSERT` is a Rust-named entry-or-insert lowering over the existing try-insert slot-pointer contract. `KEYS`, `VALUES`, `VALUES_MUT_PTRS`, `INTO_KEYS`, `INTO_VALUES`, and `RANGE` materialize concrete SA collections instead of Rust lazy iterators; `RANGE` is half-open `[start, end)`. `BTREE_MAP_INTO_KEYS` and `BTREE_MAP_INTO_VALUES` materialize the current key set or value vector and clear the map object; the caller still owns and frees the map storage. `APPEND` copies entries from the source and clears it; `SPLIT_OFF` moves entries with keys greater than or equal to the split key into a new map. `BTREE_MAP_RETAIN` is a macro-level stable in-place compaction helper using a concrete `key_slice, value_u64 -> u64` predicate. The BTreeMap hash helpers follow the sorted storage order and use deterministic `str`/`u64` hash paths. It does not model Rust's node tree internals, generics, scoped borrows, ownership-transfer iterator types, or entry object semantics.

### 1.5a B-Tree Set (`std::collections::BTreeSet` vs `sa_std/btree_set.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: `BTREE_SET_NEW`, `BTREE_SET_FREE`, `BTREE_SET_LEN`, `BTREE_SET_IS_EMPTY`, `BTREE_SET_CONTAINS`, `BTREE_SET_GET`, `BTREE_SET_GET_OR_INSERT`, `BTREE_SET_ENTRY_GET_OR_INSERT`, `BTREE_SET_OR_INSERT`, `BTREE_SET_FIRST`, `BTREE_SET_LAST`, `BTREE_SET_CLEAR`, `BTREE_SET_RETAIN`, `BTREE_SET_INSERT`, `BTREE_SET_REPLACE`, `BTREE_SET_REMOVE`, `BTREE_SET_TAKE`, `BTREE_SET_POP_FIRST`, `BTREE_SET_POP_LAST`, `BTREE_SET_IS_DISJOINT`, `BTREE_SET_IS_SUBSET`, `BTREE_SET_IS_SUPERSET`, `BTREE_SET_APPEND`, `BTREE_SET_SPLIT_OFF`, `BTREE_SET_RANGE`, `BTREE_SET_UNION`, `BTREE_SET_INTERSECTION`, `BTREE_SET_DIFFERENCE`, `BTREE_SET_SYMMETRIC_DIFFERENCE`, `BTREE_SET_LIT1`, `BTREE_SET_LIT2`, `BTREE_SET_LIT3`.
*   **Additional implemented BTreeSet hash subset**: `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_BTREE_SET_STR`, `BTREE_SET_HASH_STR`, `BUILD_HASHER_DEFAULT_HASH_ONE_BTREE_SET_STR`, and `RANDOM_STATE_HASH_ONE_BTREE_SET_STR` hash concrete sorted `BTreeSet<str>` keys by length and key order.
*   **Missing from Rust**: iterator APIs, lazy `union` / `intersection` / `difference` / `symmetric_difference` adapters, bit-operator set algebra adapters, generic `Ord` support, allocator-aware constructors, and full entry/key ownership variants beyond concrete `get_or_insert`.
*   **Scope note**: The SA facade reuses `BTreeMap` storage and comparison; it is a concrete string-slice-key subset, not Rust's generic `Ord`-driven implementation. `BTREE_SET_GET_OR_INSERT` returns `(inserted, stored_key_slice)` and inserts the concrete slice key when missing. `RANGE` is half-open `[start, end)`, and set algebra macros materialize new SA sets instead of returning Rust lazy adapters. `BTREE_SET_RETAIN` is a macro-level stable in-place compaction helper using a concrete `key_slice -> u64` predicate. Set relation helpers scan stored ordered entries and call `BTREE_SET_CONTAINS` on the opposite set. The BTreeSet hash helpers follow sorted key order and treat the backing set value as zero-sized/no-op.

*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIter cursor/terminal ops)**: These aliases consume the explicit owned backing-Vec cursor in heap-array backing order, or from the back for `nth_back`, and delegate to the same concrete `u64` iterator terminal helpers used by `Vec::IntoIter`. They return explicit scalar `(has, value)` and count outputs rather than Rust `Option<T>` ABI, and they do not model allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, generic item move/drop, or panic/drop cleanup.
*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIter advance/chunk)**: These aliases consume the explicit owned backing-Vec cursor in heap-array backing order, or from the back for `advance_back_by`, and delegate to the same concrete iterator helpers used by `Vec::IntoIter`. Advance helpers return explicit `(ok, remaining)` scalars and chunk writes caller-provided `u64` storage, rather than modeling Rust `array::IntoIter` remainder ABI, `NonZero<usize>` layout, const-generic array item ABI, allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, or panic/drop cleanup.
*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIter search/predicate)**: These aliases consume the explicit owned backing-Vec cursor in heap-array backing order, or from the back for reverse variants, and delegate to the same concrete search helpers used by `Vec::IntoIter`. They use concrete callbacks and explicit scalar status outputs rather than Rust closure capture, generic `Try` residual conversion, `Option` ABI, allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, generic item move/drop, or panic/drop cleanup.
*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIter any/all)**: These aliases consume the explicit owned backing-Vec cursor in heap-array backing order, short-circuit through the existing concrete iterator helpers, and return direct bool scalars. They do not model Rust closure capture, generic `Try` residual conversion, `Option` ABI, allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, generic item move/drop, or panic/drop cleanup.
*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIter for_each)**: These aliases consume the explicit owned backing-Vec cursor in heap-array backing order and delegate to the same concrete callback helpers used by `Vec::IntoIter`. `TRY_FOR_EACH` returns an explicit success scalar and leaves the suffix after the failing item available, without modeling Rust closure capture, generic `Try` residual conversion, allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, generic item move/drop, or panic/drop cleanup.
*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIterSorted cursor/terminal ops)**: These aliases consume the materialized descending cursor greatest-first and delegate to concrete forward iterator helpers for `nth`, `count`, and `last`. They intentionally do not add reverse cursor aliases because Rust `IntoIterSorted` implements `Iterator` / `ExactSizeIterator` but not `DoubleEndedIterator`; they also do not model Rust `Option<T>` ABI, lazy pop-on-next storage, allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, generic item move/drop, or panic/drop cleanup.
*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIterSorted advance/chunk)**: These aliases consume the materialized descending cursor greatest-first and delegate to concrete forward iterator helpers for `next_chunk` and `advance_by`. They intentionally do not add `advance_back_by` because Rust `IntoIterSorted` implements `Iterator` / `ExactSizeIterator` but not `DoubleEndedIterator`; they also do not model Rust `array::IntoIter` remainder ABI, `NonZero<usize>` layout, const-generic array item ABI, lazy pop-on-next storage, allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, generic item move/drop, or panic/drop cleanup.
*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIterSorted search/predicate)**: These aliases consume the materialized descending cursor greatest-first and delegate to concrete forward search helpers for `find`, `try_find`, `find_map`, and `position`. They intentionally do not add `rfind` or `rposition` because Rust `IntoIterSorted` implements `Iterator` / `ExactSizeIterator` but not `DoubleEndedIterator`; they also do not model Rust closure capture, generic `Try` residual conversion, Rust `Option<T>` ABI, lazy pop-on-next storage, allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, generic item move/drop, or panic/drop cleanup.
*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIterSorted any/all/for_each)**: These aliases consume the materialized descending cursor greatest-first and delegate to concrete forward predicate/callback helpers for `any`, `all`, `for_each`, and `try_for_each`. They intentionally do not add reverse traversal because Rust `IntoIterSorted` implements `Iterator` / `ExactSizeIterator` but not `DoubleEndedIterator`; they also do not model Rust closure capture, generic `Try` residual conversion, Rust `Option<T>` ABI, lazy pop-on-next storage, allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, generic item move/drop, or panic/drop cleanup.
*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIterSorted fold/aggregate)**: These aliases consume the materialized descending cursor greatest-first and delegate to concrete forward fold/aggregate helpers for `fold`, `try_fold`, `sum`, `product`, `min`, `max`, `reduce`, and `try_reduce`. They intentionally do not add reverse fold aliases because Rust `IntoIterSorted` implements `Iterator` / `ExactSizeIterator` but not `DoubleEndedIterator`; they also do not model Rust closure capture, generic `Try` residual conversion, `Sum` / `Product` / `Ord` trait dispatch, Rust `Option<T>` ABI, lazy pop-on-next storage, allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, generic item move/drop, or panic/drop cleanup.
*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIterSorted comparison/key aggregates)**: These aliases consume one or two materialized descending cursors greatest-first and delegate to concrete forward iterator comparison/key aggregate helpers. They intentionally do not add reverse traversal because Rust `IntoIterSorted` implements `Iterator` / `ExactSizeIterator` but not `DoubleEndedIterator`; they also do not model Rust generic `Ord` / `PartialOrd` dispatch, closure capture, `Option<Ordering>` ABI, lazy pop-on-next storage, allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, generic item move/drop, or panic/drop cleanup.
*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIterSorted sortedness)**: These aliases consume the materialized descending cursor greatest-first and delegate to concrete forward iterator sortedness helpers for `is_sorted`, `is_sorted_by`, and `is_sorted_by_key`. They use explicit concrete callbacks and bool outputs rather than Rust generic `PartialOrd` dispatch or closure capture, and do not model lazy pop-on-next storage, allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, generic item move/drop, or panic/drop cleanup.
*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIterSorted partition)**: These aliases consume the materialized descending cursor greatest-first and delegate to concrete forward iterator partition helpers for `partition` and `is_partitioned`. They use caller-owned `Vec<u64>` buckets and explicit bool outputs rather than Rust generic `Default` / `Extend` / `Try` dispatch, closure capture, lazy pop-on-next storage, allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, generic item move/drop, or panic/drop cleanup.
*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIterSorted take/skip)**: These aliases consume the materialized descending cursor greatest-first and eagerly collect `take` / `skip` results into caller-owned `Vec<u64>` outputs. They intentionally do not add `rev` because Rust `IntoIterSorted` implements `Iterator` / `ExactSizeIterator` but not `DoubleEndedIterator`; they also do not model lazy adapter identity/state, generic `IntoIterator` / `FromIterator`, closure capture, allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, generic item move/drop, or panic/drop cleanup.
*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIterSorted transform/filter)**: These aliases consume the materialized descending cursor greatest-first and eagerly collect concrete `map`, `filter`, `filter_map`, and `map_while` results into caller-owned `Vec<u64>` outputs. `MAP_WHILE` uses an explicit callback status and leaves the suffix after the first failure available; these helpers do not model lazy adapter identity/state, generic `Option` / `Try`, closure capture, allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, generic item move/drop, or panic/drop cleanup.
*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIterSorted enumerate/adapter-state)**: These aliases consume or view the materialized descending cursor greatest-first and eagerly collect concrete `enumerate`, `by_ref`, `fuse`, and `peekable` results into caller-owned `Vec<u64>` outputs. `ENUMERATE` flattens `(index, value)` lanes into `u64` pairs; these helpers do not model lazy adapter identity/state, Rust borrow lifetimes, tuple ABI, generic `IntoIterator` / `FromIterator`, allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, generic item move/drop, or panic/drop cleanup.
*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIterSorted array/window)**: These aliases consume the materialized descending cursor greatest-first and eagerly collect concrete `array_chunks` and `map_windows` results into caller-owned `Vec<u64>` outputs. `ARRAY_CHUNKS` leaves an incomplete tail available; `MAP_WINDOWS` uses an explicit window pointer/length callback. They do not model const-generic array ABI, borrowed window lifetimes, lazy adapter identity/state, generic closure/`FromIterator`, allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, generic item move/drop, or panic/drop cleanup.
*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIterSorted sequence)**: These aliases consume the materialized descending cursor greatest-first and eagerly collect concrete `intersperse`, `intersperse_with`, finite `cycle().take`, `take_while`, and `skip_while` results into caller-owned `Vec<u64>` outputs. They do not model lazy adapter identity/state, infinite `Cycle` object semantics, generic `Option` / closure capture, allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, generic item move/drop, or panic/drop cleanup.
*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIterSorted zip/chain)**: These aliases consume one or two materialized descending cursors greatest-first and eagerly flatten concrete `zip` / `chain` pairs into caller-owned `Vec<u64>` outputs. They do not model Rust tuple ABI, generic `IntoIterator` conversion, lazy adapter identity/state, allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, generic item move/drop, or panic/drop cleanup.
*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIterSorted inspect/scan/step_by)**: These aliases consume the materialized descending cursor greatest-first and eagerly collect concrete `inspect`, `scan`, and `step_by` results. `SCAN` preserves callback short-circuit consumption; `TRY_STEP_BY` returns failure for step zero without consuming the cursor. They do not model lazy adapter identity/state, generic `Option` / closure capture, `NonZero<usize>` or array remainder ABI, allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, generic item move/drop, or panic/drop cleanup.
*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIter comparison/key aggregates)**: These aliases consume the explicit owned backing-Vec cursor or paired cursors in heap-array backing order, delegating to the same concrete `u64` iterator comparison and key aggregate helpers used by `Vec::IntoIter`. They return scalar ordering/bool/status values and do not model Rust generic `Ord` / `PartialOrd` dispatch, closure capture, `Option<Ordering>` ABI, allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, or generic item move/drop semantics.
*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIter fold)**: These aliases consume the explicit owned backing-Vec cursor in heap-array backing order, or from the back for reverse folds, and delegate to the same concrete callback-based fold helpers used by `Vec::IntoIter`. Try variants use explicit success/value scalars, consume the failing item, and leave only not-yet-consumed suffix elements available; they do not model Rust closure capture, generic `Try` residual conversion, allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, or panic/drop cleanup.
*   **Scope note (BinaryHeap IntoIter aggregate)**: These aliases consume the explicit owned backing-Vec cursor in heap-array backing order and delegate to the same concrete `u64` aggregate/reduce helpers used by `Vec::IntoIter`. Aggregate outputs use concrete arithmetic and explicit scalar `(has, value)` / `(ok, has, value)` status channels rather than Rust `Sum` / `Product` / `Ord` trait dispatch, generic `Try` residual conversion, `Option` ABI, allocator-parametric owned iterator layout, or generic item semantics.
*   **Scope note**: The SA facade is a concrete `u64` max-heap. `AS_SLICE` / `AS_MUT_SLICE` expose the internal heap array order, `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_U64` builds a slice-backed concrete `Iter` cursor over that same arbitrary heap-array order without consuming or reordering the heap, `BINARY_HEAP_REF_INTO_ITER_U64` is the `IntoIterator for &BinaryHeap` naming alias over that same cursor, `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_DEFAULT_U64` creates an empty cursor, and the `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_CLONE_U64` / `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_NEXT*` / `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_NTH*` / `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_TRY_NEXT_CHUNK_U64` / `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_ADVANCE*` / `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_COUNT_U64` / `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_LAST_U64` / `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_SIZE_HINT_U64` / `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_EXACT_SIZE*` aliases expose the supportable borrowed `Iter` Clone, Iterator, DoubleEndedIterator, ExactSizeIterator, and fused exhaustion shapes over the same heap storage without cloning elements. `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_FIND*`, `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_POSITION_U64`, `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_RPOSITION_U64`, `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_RFIND_U64`, `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_ANY_U64`, `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_ALL_U64`, `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_FOR_EACH_U64`, `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_TRY_FOR_EACH_U64`, `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_FOLD_U64`, `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_TRY_FOLD_U64`, `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_RFOLD_U64`, `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_TRY_RFOLD_U64`, `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_SUM_U64`, `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_PRODUCT_U64`, `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_MIN_U64`, `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_MAX_U64`, `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_REDUCE_U64`, and `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_TRY_REDUCE_U64` consume only the borrowed cursor in heap-array order, or from the back for reverse variants, while leaving the heap itself intact; they use explicit scalar status outputs and concrete callbacks rather than Rust closure capture, generic `Try`, `Sum` / `Product` / `Ord` trait dispatch, `Option` ABI, or reference lifetime semantics. `BINARY_HEAP_ITER_COLLECT*` aliases consume only the borrowed cursor in heap-array order into caller-owned `Vec<u64>` outputs while leaving the heap itself intact. `BINARY_HEAP_INTO_ITER_U64` consumes the heap into an explicit caller-owned backing `Vec<u64>` plus a cursor over that Vec, `BINARY_HEAP_INTO_ITER_DEFAULT_U64` creates an empty backing Vec and empty consuming cursor, the `BINARY_HEAP_INTO_ITER_NEXT*` / `NTH*` / `TRY_NEXT_CHUNK` / `ADVANCE*` / `COUNT` / `LAST` / `FIND*` / `POSITION` / `RPOSITION` / `RFIND` / `ANY` / `ALL` / `FOR_EACH` / `TRY_FOR_EACH` / `SIZE_HINT` / `EXACT_SIZE*` aliases expose the supportable Iterator, DoubleEndedIterator, ExactSizeIterator, and fused exhaustion shapes over that cursor, `BINARY_HEAP_INTO_ITER_CLONE_U64` copies the current remainder into a separate caller-owned backing Vec and cursor, and the `BINARY_HEAP_INTO_ITER_COLLECT*` aliases consume the cursor in heap-array backing order into caller-owned `Vec<u64>` outputs. `BINARY_HEAP_INTO_ITER_SORTED_U64` consumes the heap, materializes a descending backing `Vec<u64>`, and returns a cursor whose `next` yields greatest values first; its `SORTED_NEXT` / `SORTED_NTH` / `SORTED_TRY_NEXT_CHUNK` / `SORTED_ADVANCE_BY` / `SORTED_COUNT` / `SORTED_LAST` / `SORTED_SIZE_HINT` / `SORTED_EXACT_SIZE*` aliases expose only the forward Iterator, ExactSizeIterator, and fused exhaustion surface implemented by Rust's `IntoIterSorted`, not DoubleEndedIterator, `BINARY_HEAP_INTO_ITER_SORTED_CLONE_U64` copies the current materialized descending remainder into a separate backing Vec and cursor, and the `BINARY_HEAP_INTO_ITER_SORTED_COLLECT*` aliases consume the descending cursor into caller-owned `Vec<u64>` outputs. `INTO_VEC` reinterprets the owned heap storage as a `Vec`-layout object, and `INTO_SORTED_VEC` consumes the heap and returns ascending `u64` values. `BINARY_HEAP_DRAIN` materializes the current heap-array order into a `Vec<u64>` and clears the heap, rather than returning Rust's lazy draining iterator. `BINARY_HEAP_TRY_PEEK_MUT_PTR` returns a raw pointer to the root slot, so callers can update the top element in place without Rust's scoped borrow semantics; `BINARY_HEAP_TRY_PEEK_MUT_SET_U64` writes a replacement root value, returns the previous root, and immediately reheapifies the concrete max-heap. `BINARY_HEAP_RETAIN_U64` uses a concrete `u64 -> u64` predicate, compacts retained values in place, and rebuilds heap order before returning. `BINARY_HEAP_TRY_PEEK` / `_U64` provide a non-destructive fallible value view, `BINARY_HEAP_POP` / `_U64` wrap the try-pop ABI as value-only helpers, and `BINARY_HEAP_TRY_POP_IF_U64` / `POP_IF_U64` eagerly lower Rust's unstable `pop_if` shape to `(ok, value)` over a concrete `u64 -> u64` predicate: empty heaps do not call the predicate, false predicates leave the heap unchanged, and true predicates pop the current root. `BINARY_HEAP_EXTRA_CAPACITY` reports `capacity - len`. It does not implement Rust's generic ordering, generic `T: Clone`, scoped mutable peek guard, Rust reference item/lifetime semantics, generic `FromIterator` / `Extend` / `Try` residual conversion, `Option<T>` layout, owned iterator object layout/drop glue, marker trait wiring, or allocator cloning/error/access semantics.

### 1.7 Environment (`std::env` vs `sa_std/env.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: `ENV_GET`, `ENV_GET_PTR`, `ENV_TRY_GET`, `ENV_TRY_GET_PTR`, `ENV_HAS`, `ENV_HAS_PTR`, `ENV_SET_VAR`, `ENV_SET_VAR_PTR`, `ENV_REMOVE_VAR`, `ENV_REMOVE_VAR_PTR`, `ENV_STATUS_IS_OK`, `ENV_EXISTS`, `ENV_EXISTS_PTR`, `ENV_CURRENT_DIR`, `ENV_SET_CURRENT_DIR`, `ENV_TEMP_DIR`, `ENV_CURRENT_EXE`, `ENV_TRY_HOME_DIR`, `ENV_HOME_DIR`, `ENV_ARGS_JSON`, `ENV_VARS_JSON`, `ENV_SPLIT_PATHS_JSON`, `ENV_SPLIT_PATHS_JSON_PTR`, `ENV_JOIN_PATHS_JSON`, `ENV_JOIN_PATHS_JSON_PTR`, Rust-named aliases `ENV_VAR` / `ENV_VAR_OS` / `ENV_ARGS` / `ENV_VARS` / `ENV_ARGS_OS` / `ENV_VARS_OS` / `ENV_SPLIT_PATHS_OS` / `ENV_JOIN_PATHS_OS` / `ENV_SPLIT_PATHS` / `ENV_JOIN_PATHS`, `ENV_BUFFER_DATA`, `ENV_BUFFER_LEN`, `ENV_BUFFER_AS_SLICE`, `ENV_GET_PARTS`, `ENV_GET_PARTS_PTR`, `ENV_GET_SLICE`, `ENV_GET_SLICE_PTR`, `ENV_TRY_GET_SLICE`, `ENV_TRY_GET_SLICE_PTR`, `ENV_BUFFER_FREE`, `ENV_HOME_DIR_OS`, XDG helpers (`ENV_XDG_*` and `ENV_XDG_*_OS` aliases).
*   **Plugin alternative, not `sa_std`**: Deno facade env/args helpers live in `sa_plugin_deno`; they are intentionally not counted as compiler std coverage because plugins can be installed and removed independently.
*   **Missing from Rust**: true iterator/object-returning `args` / `vars` / `args_os` / `vars_os` beyond JSON buffer aliases, full platform `split_paths` / `join_paths`, and platform-specific Unicode/OsString semantics.
*   **Scope note**: `sa_std` env access is a current-process facade over raw UTF-8/byte keys. The `_PTR` helpers expose the runtime ABI directly as `(ptr, len)`, while the Slice helpers load `Slice_ptr` / `Slice_len` and delegate to that ABI. Returned values are owned env buffer handles and must be freed with `ENV_BUFFER_FREE`. `ENV_CURRENT_DIR`, `ENV_TEMP_DIR`, `ENV_CURRENT_EXE`, `ENV_CURRENT_DIR_OS`, `ENV_TEMP_DIR_OS`, `ENV_CURRENT_EXE_OS`, `ENV_TRY_HOME_DIR`, `ENV_ARGS_JSON`, `ENV_VARS_JSON`, `ENV_SPLIT_PATHS_JSON`, and `ENV_JOIN_PATHS_JSON` reuse the same owned env-buffer contract for path-like process queries rather than introducing separate path/OS-string handle types; `ENV_ARGS_JSON` returns a JSON array of current-process arguments with Rust-style `env::args()` inclusion of `argv[0]`, `ENV_VARS_JSON` returns a JSON array of `{key,value}` records for current-process environment variables, `ENV_SPLIT_PATHS_JSON` returns a JSON array of POSIX `:`-split path segments for the default Linux platform, and `ENV_JOIN_PATHS_JSON` accepts a JSON string array and returns a POSIX `:`-joined path list. `ENV_TEMP_DIR` follows the current `TMPDIR` / `TMP` / `TEMP` fallback chain and otherwise returns `/tmp`. `ENV_SET_CURRENT_DIR` changes the current process directory from a borrowed byte slice and returns an explicit status where Rust returns `io::Result<()>`. `ENV_SET_VAR`/`ENV_SET_VAR_OS` and `ENV_REMOVE_VAR`/`ENV_REMOVE_VAR_OS` expose Rust-style process environment mutation under compiler-std names; Deno facade aliases remain plugin/compatibility territory and must not be used as std API names. The `ENV_TRY_*` helpers model Rust `var_os`-style optional lookup: missing keys return `ok=0`, buffer `0`, and an empty slice where applicable. Rust-named aliases map `ENV_VAR`→`ENV_GET`, `ENV_VAR_OS`→`ENV_TRY_GET`, `ENV_ARGS`/`ENV_VARS`/`ENV_SPLIT_PATHS`/`ENV_JOIN_PATHS` onto the JSON helpers, and `ENV_HOME_DIR` onto `ENV_TRY_HOME_DIR`; they do not introduce true `OsString` iterators.

*   **Scope note**: `STR_TRY_STRIP_PREFIX/SUFFIX`, `STR_TRIM_PREFIX/SUFFIX`, and legacy `STR_TRY_SPLIT_AT` implement byte-slice view subsets. The byte scan helpers operate on raw `u8` values and do not create Rust iterator adapters. `STR_IS_UTF8` validates a byte slice as UTF-8 and returns a bool-style scalar rather than Rust's `Result<&str, Utf8Error>`. `STR_CHAR_COUNT`, `STR_TRY_CHAR_AT`, and `STR_TRY_CHAR_RANGE_AT` validate UTF-8 while walking scalar values and return a scalar codepoint or byte-slice view for the selected scalar; they do not provide Rust's lazy `Chars` iterator, grapheme clusters, Unicode normalization, or pattern traits. `STR_TRY_GET_RANGE` checks both byte bounds and UTF-8 character boundaries before returning a `Slice` view; the prefix/suffix/range-to/range-from/range-between helpers are Rust range-shape aliases over that checked primitive, and `STR_TRY_SPLIT_AT_CHECKED` returns checked left/right views. `STR_FLOOR_CHAR_BOUNDARY` and `STR_CEIL_CHAR_BOUNDARY` clamp byte indexes to the nearest boundary using the same continuation-byte rule as `STR_IS_CHAR_BOUNDARY`. `STR_SPLIT_BYTE_COUNT` and `STR_TRY_SPLIT_BYTE_AT` model a byte-delimiter `split` view subset; leading, trailing, and consecutive delimiters produce empty fields, and an empty string has one empty split field. `STR_LINE_COUNT` and `STR_TRY_LINE_AT` model the practical `lines()` view subset for LF and CRLF terminators; line terminators are excluded from returned slices and a trailing terminator does not create an extra empty line. The legacy `STRING_*` mutation helpers are byte-level `Slice` rewrites: `push_str`, `insert_str`, and `replace_range` materialize new concatenated byte buffers, `split_off` truncates the left view and returns the right view, and `pop` removes one trailing byte. The `STRING_BUF_*` helpers are a separate owned byte-buffer subset backed by the existing `Vec` layout, so they can model `String::with_capacity`, `capacity`, `reserve`, `reserve_exact`, `try_reserve`, `shrink_to`, `shrink_to_fit`, `clear`, `truncate`, byte `push`, Unicode-scalar `try_push`/`push` via UTF-8 encoding, `push_str`, byte `pop`, `split_off`, byte/string insert, Unicode-scalar insert via UTF-8 encoding, `replace_range`, `into_bytes`, unchecked byte-slice construction, ASCII-checked byte-slice construction, and eager byte-slice-needle `replace` without changing existing slice-string semantics. These operations are byte-indexed where they take byte offsets; `STRING_BUF_TRY_PUSH_CHAR` / `STRING_BUF_PUSH_CHAR` and `STRING_BUF_TRY_INSERT_CHAR` / `STRING_BUF_INSERT_CHAR` accept any valid Unicode scalar value and reject invalid scalar values such as surrogate codepoints without claiming Rust's full `Pattern` machinery or Rust scoped borrow semantics. `STR_SPLIT_NEEDLE_COUNT` / `STRING_SPLIT_NEEDLE_COUNT` / `STRING_BUF_SPLIT_NEEDLE_COUNT` model non-overlapping `&str`-needle `split` count (matches-plus-one) rather than lazy `Split`/`RSplit` iterator adapters; `STR_TRY_SPLIT_NEEDLE_AT` / `*_SPLIT_NEEDLE_AT` return caller-indexed `Slice` views over the split fields with `ok=1`/`ok=0` for present/absent fields, where leading, trailing, and consecutive needles produce empty fields and an empty-needle match yields the whole haystack at index 0 and absent thereafter. `STR_SPLIT_NEEDLE_TERM_COUNT` / `STRING_SPLIT_NEEDLE_TERM_COUNT` / `STRING_BUF_SPLIT_NEEDLE_TERM_COUNT` model `split_terminator` count by counting the base `split` field count and then subtracting the trailing run of needle-terminated empty fields, so a non-empty empty-needle case returns zero trailing fields; an empty string or empty needle returns zero terms. `STR_MATCHES_NEEDLE_COUNT` / `STRING_MATCHES_NEEDLE_COUNT` / `STRING_BUF_MATCHES_NEEDLE_COUNT` model the count of non-overlapping `&str`-needle matches equivalent to Rust `str::matches().count()`, and `STR_TRY_MATCHES_NEEDLE_AT` / `*_MATCHES_NEEDLE_AT` return caller-indexed `Slice` views over each non-overlapping needle occurrence, with empty needles yielding zero matches and `ok=0`. These slice-needle view subsets return explicit `(ok, Slice)` shapes rather than Rust lazy `Split` / `SplitTerminator` / `Matches` / `RSplit` iterator adapters or Rust `Option<&str>` values, and they do not claim Rust `Pattern` trait machinery, `&[u8]`-needle or closure pattern variants, or borrow-scoped reference lifetimes.
`STR_RSPLIT_NEEDLE_COUNT` / `STRING_RSPLIT_NEEDLE_COUNT` / `STRING_BUF_RSPLIT_NEEDLE_COUNT` model the same field count as `*_SPLIT_NEEDLE_COUNT` (the `split` and `rsplit` field sets are identical, only enumerated in opposite order), and `STR_RMATCHES_NEEDLE_COUNT` / `STRING_RMATCHES_NEEDLE_COUNT` / `STRING_BUF_RMATCHES_NEEDLE_COUNT` model the same non-overlapping match count as `*_MATCHES_NEEDLE_COUNT`. `STR_TRY_RSPLIT_NEEDLE_AT` / `*_RSPLIT_NEEDLE_AT` and `STR_TRY_RMATCHES_NEEDLE_AT` / `*_RMATCHES_NEEDLE_AT` compute the corresponding forward caller index (`count - 1 - reverse_index`) and delegate to the existing forward `*_TRY_SPLIT_NEEDLE_AT` / `*_TRY_MATCHES_NEEDLE_AT` helpers, so reverse field/match enumeration, empty-field behavior, and empty-needle behavior stay aligned with the forward batch. These reverse split/matches aliases are separate from the dedicated split_terminator/rsplit_terminator helpers, still return explicit `(ok, Slice)` shapes rather than Rust lazy `RSplit` / `RMatches` iterator adapters or Rust `Option<&str>` values, and do not claim Rust `Pattern` trait machinery, `&[u8]`-needle or closure pattern variants, `splitn`/`rsplitn` limited-count variants, or borrow-scoped reference lifetimes.

### 1.9 File System (`std::fs` vs `sa_std/fs.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: Handles (`open_read`, `open_write`, `create`, `close`, `read`, `read_exact`, `write`, `write_all`, `flush`, `sync_data`, `sync_all`, `set_len`, `seek`), Full-file IO (`read_file`, UTF-8-validating `read_to_string`, `write_file`, base64 read/write), Buffer helpers, Metadata (`metadata`, JSON metadata, `is_file`, `is_dir`, `is_symlink`, `modified_ms`, `created_ms`, `free`), directory JSON listing (`read_dir_json`), path operations (`exists`, status-returning `try_exists`, `len`, `remove_file`, `remove`, `rename`, single-directory `create_dir`, recursive `make_dir` / `create_dir_all`, single-directory `remove_dir`, recursive `remove_dir_all` / `remove_path`, `copy`, `hard_link`, symlink creation, `read_link`, `canonicalize`), POSIX-mode permission setting (`set_permissions`), and POSIX millisecond atime/mtime setting (`set_times_ms`).
*   **Plugin alternative, not `sa_std`**: Deno facade file helpers live in `sa_plugin_deno` and are not part of compiler std coverage.

`FS_DIR_BUILDER_WITH_RECURSIVE`, `FS_DIR_BUILDER_WITH_MODE`, `FS_DIR_BUILDER_CREATE`, `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_NEW`, `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_READ`, `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_WRITE`, `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_APPEND`, `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_CREATE`, `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_TRUNCATE`, `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_MODE`, `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_CUSTOM_FLAGS`, and `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_OPEN`, `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_SYNC`, `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_DSYNC`, `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_NONBLOCK`, `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_NOFOLLOW`, `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_DIRECT`, `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_DIRECTORY`, `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_CLOEXEC`, `FS_FILE_TYPE_IS_FILE`, `FS_FILE_TYPE_IS_DIR`, `FS_FILE_TYPE_IS_SYMLINK`, `FS_FILE_TYPE_IS_BLOCK_DEVICE`, `FS_FILE_TYPE_IS_CHAR_DEVICE`, `FS_FILE_TYPE_IS_FIFO`, `FS_FILE_TYPE_IS_SOCKET`, and File fd aliases (`FS_FILE_AS_FD`, `FS_FILE_AS_RAW_FD`, `FS_FILE_INTO_RAW_FD`, `FS_FILE_FROM_RAW_FD`, `FS_FILE_INTO_OWNED_FD`, `FS_FILE_FROM_OWNED_FD`) expose status plus out-value results where Rust would return `io::Result<T>`.
*   **Missing from Rust**: typed streaming `ReadDir` iterator entries beyond JSON listing / concrete entry buffers, Windows ACL `Permissions`, platform birth/creation time mutation, and portable permission/time semantics beyond explicit POSIX mode and millisecond timestamp helpers.
*   **Scope note**: `FS_READ_TO_STRING` maps Rust `fs::read_to_string` into the existing owned read-buffer contract after validating UTF-8; invalid UTF-8 returns a failing status and no buffer handle. `FS_TRY_EXISTS` maps Rust `fs::try_exists` into explicit `(status, exists)` outputs: missing paths return `status=OK, exists=0`, while other filesystem errors can still return a non-OK status. `FS_EXISTS` remains the legacy bool-only helper. `FS_CREATE_DIR` maps Rust's non-recursive `fs::create_dir` behavior and fails when parents are missing or the directory already exists; `FS_MAKE_DIR` / `FS_CREATE_DIR_ALL` keep the existing recursive `makePath` behavior. `FS_REMOVE_DIR` maps Rust's non-recursive `fs::remove_dir` behavior and fails on non-empty directories, while `FS_REMOVE_DIR_ALL` / `FS_REMOVE_PATH` keep the explicit recursive delete-tree behavior. `FS_SET_PERMISSIONS` lowers Rust's common `fs::set_permissions` path mutation into a POSIX numeric mode (`u32`) and explicit status result. `FS_PERMISSIONS_*` / `FS_METADATA_PERMISSIONS` / `FS_SET_PERMISSIONS_OBJ` provide a concrete mode-object surface with Unix-style `readonly` helpers; `FS_FILE_TYPE_IS_*` aliases rename the existing metadata kind predicates. `FS_SET_TIMES_MS` lowers Rust's file-time mutation shape into explicit Unix-millisecond atime/mtime values and updates the path through POSIX `futimens`; it does not model Rust's `FileTimes` builder object, nanosecond precision API, platform-specific unsupported fields, readonly helper semantics,  Windows ACL behavior, or platform-specific permission extension traits. `FS_DIR_BUILDER_NEW` / `FS_DIR_BUILDER_WITH_RECURSIVE` / `FS_DIR_BUILDER_WITH_MODE` / `FS_DIR_BUILDER_CREATE` map Rust's `DirBuilder` builder pattern: the builder state is two propagating SSA `u64` values (a `recursive` boolean 0/1 and a POSIX `mode`) rather than a heap-allocated builder object; `FS_DIR_BUILDER_NEW` initializes to `recursive=false` and `mode=0o755` (Rust's default), `FS_DIR_BUILDER_WITH_RECURSIVE` normalizes any nonzero flag to `1` and otherwise preserves the `mode`, `FS_DIR_BUILDER_WITH_MODE` sets the POSIX `mode` while preserving the `recursive` flag, and `FS_DIR_BUILDER_CREATE` branches on the `recursive` flag to dispatch to the existing `FS_CREATE_DIR_MODE` (non-recursive) or `FS_CREATE_DIR_ALL_MODE` (recursive) lowerings, so it adds no new FFI/sycall surface. It does not model Rust's owned `DirBuilder` value semantics (move/build ownership), the `Permissions`/`metadata`-style builder set on the returned handle, Windows ACL permission layers, or the `create`-returns-the-directory-handle variants. `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_NEW` / `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_READ` / `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_WRITE` / `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_APPEND` / `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_CREATE` / `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_TRUNCATE` / `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_MODE` / `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_CUSTOM_FLAGS` / `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_OPEN` map Rust's `std::fs::OpenOptions` builder pattern: the builder state is three propagating SSA `u64` values (`flags`, a POSIX `create_mode`, and `custom_flags`) rather than a heap-allocated builder object. `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_NEW` initializes to `flags=0`, `create_mode=SA_FS_OPEN_MODE_DEFAULT`, `custom_flags=0` (Rust's `OpenOptions::new()` defaults: all `bool` fields `false`). Each `/_WITH_READ` / `/WRITE` / `/APPEND` / `/CREATE` / `/TRUNCATE` macro normalizes the supplied flag (any nonzero bit = true) and OR's in the existing SA_FS_OPEN_* bit, leaving the bit untouched when the supplied flag is zero (matching Rust's `.x(true)` set-the-bit idiom; Rust's `.x(false)` clear-the-bit is intentionally not lowered because there is no flag-clearing OpenOptions shape outside NetBSD/Hurd marginal helpers and a Clear-shape would require `and %reg, ~bit` on the same SSA). `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_MODE` sets the full POSIX `create_mode`, `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_WITH_CUSTOM_FLAGS` sets the full `custom_flags`, and `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS_BUILDER_OPEN` delegates to the existing `FS_OPEN_OPTIONS` FFI lowering, so it adds no new syscall/FFI surface. It does not model Rust's `create_new` (`O_CREAT|O_EXCL`) semantics (the `O_EXCL` bit is not exposed by the `SA_FS_CUSTOM_*` table), cross-platform `truncate`/`append` edges on read-only opens, the `set_permissions`-equivalent builder method, Windows ACL fields, or owned builder move/build value semantics.

### 1.10 Input/Output (`std::io` vs `sa_std/io.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: `stdin`, `stdout`, `stderr`, fixed stdio fd aliases (`IO_STDIN_AS_RAW_FD`, `IO_STDOUT_AS_RAW_FD`, `IO_STDERR_AS_RAW_FD`, `IO_STDIN_AS_FD`, `IO_STDOUT_AS_FD`, `IO_STDERR_AS_FD`), `PRINTLN`, `READ_LINE`, `read`, `write`, etc.; macro-level `ErrorKind` mapping and retry checks including `InvalidData`, and full `IO_ERROR_KIND_*` enum constants covering the complete Rust `io::ErrorKind` variant set; `IoSlice` / `IoSliceMut` view layouts; `Cursor` over a byte buffer; `Cursor` exact-read, write-all, fill-buf, consume, read-to-end, and read-to-string helpers; `Take` read-to-end and read-to-string helpers; and pure utility macros for `empty`, `repeat`, `sink`, including exact-read/write-all convenience helpers, and slice copying, `IO_COPY_N`, and stdio flush helpers `IO_FLUSH_STDOUT` / `IO_FLUSH_STDERR`.
*   **Plugin alternative, not `sa_std`**: Deno facade stdio helpers live in `sa_plugin_deno` and are not part of compiler std coverage.
*   **Rust-aligned macro surface**: `IO_ERROR_KIND_FROM_STATUS`, `IO_ERROR_KIND_IS_RETRYABLE`, `IO_SLICE_*`, `IO_SLICE_MUT_*`, `IO_CURSOR_*` including `get_ref` / `get_mut` / `into_inner` style pointer-or-slice access, `remaining`/`remaining_slice` views, exact-read, fill-buf, consume, write-all, read-to-end and read-to-string buffer helpers, and `SeekFrom::Start` / `Current` / `End` style position helpers, `IO_EMPTY_*`, `IO_REPEAT_*`, `IO_TAKE_*` including `take.read_to_end` and `take.read_to_string`, `IO_SINK_WRITE`, `IO_SINK_WRITE_ALL`, and `IO_COPY_SLICE`, handle-to-handle `IO_COPY` provide concrete data-layout and utility subsets where Rust would expose trait methods and typed `io::Result` values.
*   **Missing Infrastructure**: `Read`, `Write`, `Seek`, `BufRead` traits; full `Error` object/custom error system; vectored trait dispatch; `copy` over arbitrary readers/writers; allocation-appending `read_to_end` / `read_to_string`, `bytes`, `chain`, `BufReader`/`BufWriter` parity, and borrow-initialization guarantees from `BorrowedBuf` / `BorrowedCursor`.
*   **Scope note**: `IoSlice` / `IoSliceMut` and `Cursor` are concrete pointer/length/position contracts. `IO_CURSOR_AS_PTR`, `IO_CURSOR_AS_SLICE`, `IO_CURSOR_AS_MUT_SLICE`, and `IO_CURSOR_INTO_INNER` expose the underlying borrowed buffer as SA pointer/length views rather than Rust references or owned values. `IO_CURSOR_READ` / `IO_CURSOR_WRITE` clamp to the remaining in-memory buffer. `IO_CURSOR_READ_EXACT` succeeds only when the requested byte count fits in the remaining buffer; otherwise it returns `SA_IO_ERR_EOF` and places the cursor at EOF, matching Rust `Cursor`'s exact-read cursor-position behavior. `IO_CURSOR_WRITE_ALL` returns `SA_IO_OK` only when the full source slice fits in the remaining cursor buffer and `SA_IO_ERR_EOF` after a short write. `IO_CURSOR_FILL_BUF` returns the unread tail as a borrowed `IoSlice`, and `IO_CURSOR_CONSUME` advances the concrete position by the requested amount. `IO_CURSOR_REMAINING_SLICE` exposes the unread cursor tail as a borrowed `IoSlice`, `IO_CURSOR_READ_TO_END` copies the unread tail into caller-provided storage up to the caller's capacity while advancing the cursor, and `IO_CURSOR_READ_TO_STRING` additionally validates the copied bytes as UTF-8 before returning a borrowed output slice. Invalid UTF-8 returns `SA_IO_ERR_INVALID_DATA` / `IO_ERROR_KIND_INVALID_DATA` and an empty slice. These helpers do not allocate or append to a Rust `Vec<u8>` / `String`. `IO_CURSOR_SEEK_START`, `IO_CURSOR_SEEK_CURRENT`, and `IO_CURSOR_SEEK_END` update the concrete cursor position directly; negative current/end offsets saturate at zero, and positive offsets may seek beyond the buffer end, where later reads/writes clamp to zero remaining bytes. `IO_TAKE_NEW` wraps a cursor pointer with a remaining byte limit, `IO_TAKE_READ`, `IO_TAKE_READ_TO_END`, and `IO_TAKE_READ_TO_STRING` read at most that limit and decrement it by the actual read length, and `IO_TAKE_INTO_INNER` exposes the wrapped cursor pointer. `IO_EMPTY_READ` always returns zero bytes, `IO_EMPTY_READ_EXACT` succeeds only for a zero-byte request and otherwise returns `SA_IO_ERR_EOF`, `IO_REPEAT_READ` / `IO_REPEAT_READ_EXACT` fill caller storage with the repeated byte, and `IO_SINK_WRITE` / `IO_SINK_WRITE_ALL` report all bytes consumed without storing them. These are verifier-friendly SA utility macros, not Rust trait implementations.

### 1.10a OS file descriptors (`std::os::fd` / `std::os::unix::io` vs `sa_std/os/fd.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: raw/owned fd helpers (`FD_AS_RAW`, `FD_INTO_RAW`, `FD_FROM_RAW`, `FD_DUP`, `FD_DUP_RAW`, `FD_CLOSE_RAW`, `FD_IS_TERMINAL`), Rust-named `RawFd` reflexive helpers (`FD_RAW_AS_RAW_FD`, `FD_RAW_INTO_RAW_FD`, `FD_RAW_FROM_RAW_FD`), Rust-named `BorrowedFd` helpers (`FD_BORROWED_BORROW_RAW`, `FD_BORROWED_AS_RAW_FD`, `FD_BORROWED_AS_FD`, `FD_BORROWED_TRY_CLONE_TO_OWNED`), Rust-named `OwnedFd` helpers (`FD_OWNED_AS_RAW_FD`, `FD_OWNED_AS_FD`, `FD_OWNED_INTO_RAW_FD`, `FD_OWNED_FROM_RAW_FD`, `FD_OWNED_TRY_CLONE`), `std::fs::File` fd aliases in `sa_std/fs.sa` including `FS_FILE_AS_FD`, socket fd aliases in `sa_std/net.sa` including `NET_TCP_LISTENER_AS_FD`, `NET_TCP_STREAM_AS_FD`, `NET_UDP_AS_FD`, `NET_UNIX_LISTENER_AS_FD`, `NET_UNIX_STREAM_AS_FD`, and `NET_UNIX_DATAGRAM_AS_FD`, process fd aliases in `sa_std/process.sa` including `PIDFD_AS_FD`, `PROCESS_CHILD_STDOUT_AS_FD`, and `PROCESS_CHILD_STDERR_AS_FD`, and stdio `AsFd` aliases in `sa_std/io.sa` including `IO_STDIN_AS_FD`, `IO_STDOUT_AS_FD`, and `IO_STDERR_AS_FD`.
*   **Missing from Rust**: native Rust lifetime tracking for `BorrowedFd<'_>`, trait dispatch for `AsFd` / `AsRawFd` / `IntoRawFd` / `FromRawFd`, and Rust's object layout/niche guarantees.
*   **Scope note**: The SA borrowed fd representation is the raw fd scalar. The `AsFd` aliases therefore return the same borrowed scalar for `BorrowedFd` and delegate through the existing owned handle raw-fd view for `OwnedFd`; ownership transfer and duplication remain explicit through the existing raw/owned helpers.

### 1.11 Networking (`std::net` vs `sa_std/net.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: TCP connect/listener bind/accept/local_addr/close, `TcpListener::incoming`-style concrete adapter state, TcpListener options (`set_nonblocking`, `set_ttl`, `ttl`, `take_error`), TcpStream read/peek/write/write_all/flush/local_addr/peer_addr/shutdown/close, stream options (`set_read_timeout`, `set_write_timeout`, `set_nonblocking`, `set_nodelay`, `set_ttl`, `read_timeout`, `write_timeout`, `nodelay`, `ttl`, `take_error`), TCP listener/stream fd aliases including `as_fd`, UDP bind/local_addr/peer_addr/connect/send/recv/peek/send_to/recv_from/peek_from/close, UDP options (`set_read_timeout`, `set_write_timeout`, `set_nonblocking`, `set_broadcast`, `set_ttl`, `set_multicast_loop_v4`, `set_multicast_ttl_v4`, `read_timeout`, `write_timeout`, `broadcast`, `ttl`, `multicast_loop_v4`, `multicast_ttl_v4`, `take_error`, `join_multicast_v4`, `leave_multicast_v4`, `join_multicast_v6`, `leave_multicast_v6`), UDP fd aliases including `as_fd`, `ToSocketAddrs`-style first-address resolution, owned `NetAddr` accessors and formatting (`host`, `host_len`, `port`, `family`, `scope_id`, `format`, `is_ipv4`, `is_ipv6`, `free`), typed address layouts and macros for `Ipv4Addr`, `Ipv6Addr`, `IpAddr`, `SocketAddrV4`, `SocketAddrV6`, and `SocketAddr`, and Async Reactor macros.
*   **Plugin alternative, not `sa_std`**: Deno facade networking helpers live in `sa_plugin_deno`; HTTP client/server live in `sa_plugin_http_client` and `sa_plugin_http_server`. None of these count as compiler std coverage.
*   **Rust-aligned macro surface**: `NET_TCP_CONNECT`, `NET_TCP_LISTENER_BIND`, `NET_TCP_LISTENER_BIND_PORT`, `NET_TCP_LISTENER_ACCEPT`, `NET_TCP_LISTENER_LOCAL_ADDR`, `NET_TCP_LISTENER_INCOMING`, `NET_TCP_INCOMING_NEW`, `NET_TCP_INCOMING_LISTENER`, `NET_TCP_INCOMING_NEXT`, listener option helpers, `NET_TCP_LISTENER_CLOSE`, `NET_TCP_STREAM_READ`, `NET_TCP_STREAM_PEEK`, `NET_TCP_STREAM_WRITE`, `NET_TCP_STREAM_WRITE_ALL`, `NET_TCP_STREAM_FLUSH`, `NET_TCP_STREAM_LOCAL_ADDR`, `NET_TCP_STREAM_PEER_ADDR`, `NET_TCP_STREAM_SHUTDOWN`, stream option setters/getters, `NET_TCP_STREAM_TAKE_ERROR`, `NET_TCP_STREAM_CLOSE`, `NET_UDP_BIND`, `NET_UDP_LOCAL_ADDR`, `NET_UDP_PEER_ADDR`, `NET_UDP_CONNECT`, `NET_UDP_SEND`, `NET_UDP_RECV`, `NET_UDP_PEEK`, `NET_UDP_SEND_TO`, `NET_UDP_RECV_FROM`, `NET_UDP_PEEK_FROM`, UDP option setters/getters, `NET_UDP_JOIN_MULTICAST_V4`, `NET_UDP_LEAVE_MULTICAST_V4`, `NET_UDP_JOIN_MULTICAST_V6`, `NET_UDP_LEAVE_MULTICAST_V6`, `NET_UDP_TAKE_ERROR`, `NET_UDP_CLOSE`, `NET_TO_SOCKET_ADDR_FIRST`, and `NET_ADDR_*` helpers including `NET_ADDR_FORMAT` expose status plus out-value results where Rust would return `io::Result<T>` or format through traits. Typed address helpers include `NET_IPV4_*` (including `NET_IPV4_DEFAULT`), `NET_IPV6_*` (including `NET_IPV6_DEFAULT`), `NET_IP_ADDR_*` (including `NET_IP_ADDR_DEFAULT`), and `NET_SOCKET_ADDR_*` (including `NET_SOCKET_ADDR_DEFAULT / NET_SOCKET_ADDR_V4_DEFAULT / NET_SOCKET_ADDR_V6_DEFAULT`) constructors, field accessors, enum-dispatch accessors, copy/setter helpers, equality/tag checks, address constants (`UNSPECIFIED`, `LOCALHOST`, and IPv4 `BROADCAST`), `Ipv4Addr::BITS` / `Ipv6Addr::BITS` style constants, IPv4 and IPv6 octets/from-octets/from-segments helpers, `Ipv4Addr::as_octets`-style direct pointer/write helpers, `Ipv4Addr::parse_ascii`-style dotted-decimal byte parsing, `Ipv6Addr::parse_ascii`-style pure IPv6 byte parsing, `IpAddr::parse_ascii` V4-then-V6 enum parsing, `SocketAddrV4`/`SocketAddrV6`/`SocketAddr` `parse_ascii` for `ipv4:port` and bracketed `[ipv6]:port`, `Ipv6Addr::as_octets`-style network-byte write helpers, `IpAddr::as_octets`-style octet length plus write dispatch helpers, `Ipv4Addr::to_bits` / `from_bits`-style macros, SA `Ipv6Addr` high/low `u64` bits helpers, IPv4 shared/benchmarking/reserved/global checks, IPv4-to-IPv6 compatible/mapped conversions, IPv6 documentation/benchmarking/global/unicast/multicast-scope checks, IPv6-to-IPv4 mapped/compatible extraction helpers, `Ipv6Addr::to_canonical` and `IpAddr` mapped-IPv6 `to_canonical` lowering, `IpAddr` enum-dispatch predicate helpers including `is_private`, `is_link_local`, `is_shared`, `is_reserved`, `is_unicast`, `is_unicast_link_local`, `is_unique_local`, `is_ipv4_mapped`, `multicast_scope`, `to_ipv4`, `is_benchmarking`, and Rust-style IPv6 `is_global`, `IpAddr` v4/v6 constant constructors, enum copy, and socket-family helpers, plus `SocketAddrV4::ip` / `SocketAddrV6::ip` value-copy helpers.
*   **Hash macro surface**: `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_NET_IPV4`, `NET_IPV4_HASH`, `BUILD_HASHER_DEFAULT_HASH_ONE_NET_IPV4`, `RANDOM_STATE_HASH_ONE_NET_IPV4`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_NET_SOCKET_ADDR_V4`, `NET_SOCKET_ADDR_V4_HASH`, `BUILD_HASHER_DEFAULT_HASH_ONE_NET_SOCKET_ADDR_V4`, and `RANDOM_STATE_HASH_ONE_NET_SOCKET_ADDR_V4` cover the supportable `Ipv4Addr` and `SocketAddrV4` hash shapes. IPv4 hashing uses native-endian octets through the existing `u32` hasher path; SocketAddrV4 hashing writes `ip` then `port` in Rust's derived field order. IPv6 `u128::from_ne_bytes`, `IpAddr` enum trait hashing, and `SocketAddr` enum trait hashing remain out of scope for the current concrete net facade.
*   **Missing from Rust**: full typed-address Display/formatting traits beyond the concrete IPv4/IPv6/`IpAddr`/`SocketAddr` `parse_ascii`/`format_ascii` subset, full IPv6 zone/interface-name resolution beyond numeric `%scope` digits, full lazy `ToSocketAddrs` iterator semantics, Rust's `io::Error`/`ErrorKind` integration, native `Ipv6Addr::to_bits` / `from_bits` `u128` values, and trait impls/conversions beyond the concrete macro subset. `NET_IPV6_IS_GLOBAL` follows the current Rust core exclusion/exception shape for the common special-purpose ranges but should still be audited if Rust updates the IANA matrix.
*   **Scope note**: The current net facade is a thin Zig-backed runtime handle layer plus pure SA typed-address layouts. It intentionally avoids HTTP, Deno compatibility, DNS-rich helpers, async executors, and plugin-specific APIs; those remain optional plugin territory and must not be assumed installed. The typed address macros model concrete storage and common predicates, not Rust's trait impls, display behavior, or platform socket conversion internals. `NET_IPV4_TRY_PARSE_ASCII` / `NET_IPV4_PARSE_ASCII` parse borrowed ASCII bytes in dotted-decimal form into concrete `Ipv4Addr` storage and return `ok=0` for empty octets, non-digits, out-of-range octets, missing separators, or trailing bytes. `NET_IP_ADDR_TRY_PARSE_ASCII` / `NET_IP_ADDR_PARSE_ASCII` accept IPv4 dotted-decimal or pure IPv6 text (no brackets) and materialize `IpAddr::V4` / `IpAddr::V6`. `NET_SOCKET_ADDR_V4_TRY_PARSE_ASCII` / `NET_SOCKET_ADDR_V4_PARSE_ASCII` parse `ipv4:port` into concrete `SocketAddrV4` storage and return `ok=0` for invalid IPv4 bytes, missing or empty ports, non-digit ports, and ports above `65535`. `NET_SOCKET_ADDR_TRY_PARSE_ASCII` / `NET_SOCKET_ADDR_PARSE_ASCII` accept `ipv4:port` or bracketed `[ipv6]:port` (with optional numeric `%scope` in the IPv6 text) into enum `SocketAddr::V4` / `SocketAddr::V6`, writing an unspecified IPv4 socket with port 0 on parse failure and returning `ok=0`. `AddrParseError_*` constants record Rust's parse-error kind categories for layout checks, but these parsers still return local bool-style status rather than materializing Rust `Result<_, AddrParseError>` objects or generic `FromStr` trait behavior. `NET_TCP_LISTENER_INCOMING` / `NET_TCP_INCOMING_*` provide a concrete listener-handle wrapper that lowers `incoming().next()` into the existing `accept` ABI with explicit `status + ok + stream` outputs instead of Rust trait iterator objects. `NET_TO_SOCKET_ADDR_FIRST` lowers Rust's common host/port resolution entry point into a single owned `NetAddr` handle for the first resolved address rather than exposing Rust's lazy iterator, parser, or formatting traits. `NET_ADDR_FORMAT` writes Rust-style socket address text for an owned runtime `NetAddr` into caller-provided storage and returns `(status, len)`, avoiding a new string allocation ABI. `NET_IP_ADDR_*` predicate macros dispatch to existing IPv4/IPv6 concrete predicates where Rust exposes enum methods; IPv4-only concepts such as shared/reserved return false for IPv6, IPv6-only concepts such as unique-local and IPv4-mapped return false for IPv4, and multicast scope returns an explicit `(ok, scope)` pair. `NET_IP_ADDR_FAMILY` and `NET_SOCKET_ADDR_FAMILY` return the existing `SA_NET_AF_INET` / `SA_NET_AF_INET6` constants for concrete enum tags rather than querying a platform socket.

### 1.12 Process (`std::process` vs `sa_std/process.sa`)
*   **Implemented in `sa_std`**: `SaProcessArgv` layout helpers (`PROCESS_ARGV_SLOT`, `PROCESS_ARGV_SET_CONST`, `PROCESS_ARGV_SET_PTR`, `PROCESS_ARGV_SET_SLICE`, `PROCESS_ARGV_DATA`, `PROCESS_ARGV_LEN`), status/exit-code helpers (`PROCESS_STATUS_IS_OK`, `PROCESS_EXIT_SUCCESS`), typed `ExitStatus` layout helpers (`PROCESS_EXIT_STATUS_NEW`, `PROCESS_EXIT_STATUS_CODE`, `PROCESS_EXIT_STATUS_SUCCESS`), typed `Output` layout/access/free helpers (`PROCESS_OUTPUT_NEW`, `PROCESS_OUTPUT_STATUS_PTR`, `PROCESS_OUTPUT_STDOUT_BUFFER`, `PROCESS_OUTPUT_STDERR_BUFFER`, `PROCESS_OUTPUT_STDOUT_DATA`, `PROCESS_OUTPUT_STDOUT_LEN`, `PROCESS_OUTPUT_STDERR_DATA`, `PROCESS_OUTPUT_STDERR_LEN`, `PROCESS_OUTPUT_FREE`), concrete `Stdio` value-object helpers (`PROCESS_STDIO_PIPED`, `PROCESS_STDIO_INHERIT`, `PROCESS_STDIO_NULL`, `PROCESS_STDIO_KIND`, `PROCESS_STDIO_MAKES_PIPE`), `run`, `run_cwd`, `spawn`, `spawn_cwd`, `spawn_stream`, `spawn_stream_cwd`, `PROCESS_ID`, `PROCESS_ABORT`, `PROCESS_CHILD_ID`, `wait`, `try_wait`, `kill`, `PROCESS_WAIT_EXIT_STATUS`, `PROCESS_TRY_WAIT_EXIT_STATUS`, `read_stdout`, `read_stderr`, `exec_capture`, `PROCESS_EXEC_CAPTURE_OUTPUT`, `exec_capture_cwd`, `PROCESS_EXEC_CAPTURE_OUTPUT_CWD`, and `close` macro wrappers over the Zig-backed runtime externs, plus concrete `PROCESS_COMMAND_BUILDER_*` SSA config builder helpers (`NEW` / `WITH_CURRENT_DIR` / `WITH_ARG0` / `WITH_PROCESS_GROUP` / `WITH_SETSID` / `STATUS` / `SPAWN` / `EXEC` / `SPAWN_STREAM` / `SPAWN_STREAM_PIDFD` / `SPAWN_STREAM_UID_GID` / `SPAWN_STREAM_GROUPS` / `SPAWN_STREAM_CHROOT` / `EXEC_CAPTURE` / `OUTPUT` / `STATUS_PIDFD` / `SPAWN_PIDFD` / `STATUS_UID_GID` / `SPAWN_UID_GID` / `STATUS_GROUPS` / `SPAWN_GROUPS` / `STATUS_CHROOT` / `SPAWN_CHROOT`) over the existing CommandExt/capture/pidfd/uid-gid/groups/chroot ABI, `PROCESS_EXIT_CODE`, `PROCESS_SUCCESS`, `PROCESS_IS_SUCCESS`, `PROCESS_CODE`, `PROCESS_OUTPUT_STATUS`.
*   **Plugin alternative, not `sa_std`**: Deno facade process helpers live in `sa_plugin_deno` and are not part of compiler std coverage.
*   **Missing from Rust**: full Rust `Command` builder object with env maps and pipe Stdio redirection beyond the concrete `PROCESS_COMMAND_BUILDER_*` scalar-flag facade (capture helpers honor cwd only; arg0/process-group/setsid are not applied on capture paths), wiring `PROCESS_STDIO_*` values into command spawn/capture redirection, `ChildStdin` trait/fd integration, Rust trait dispatch for `ChildStdout` / `ChildStderr` beyond the concrete fd macros, environment mutation through `Command`, and platform-specific process semantics.
*   **Scope note**: `ProcessExitStatus`, `ProcessOutput`, and `ProcessStdio` are concrete SA memory layouts layered over the current runtime ABI rather than native Rust structs. `PROCESS_STDIO_PIPED`, `PROCESS_STDIO_INHERIT`, and `PROCESS_STDIO_NULL` model Rust's `Stdio::{piped,inherit,null}` as a local one-byte kind value, and `PROCESS_STDIO_MAKES_PIPE` matches only the piped kind; these values are not yet consumed by the process-spawn ABI. `PROCESS_ID` lowers directly to the current process `pid` as a plain `u32`, without Rust's typed process object layering. `PROCESS_ABORT` lowers Rust `std::process::abort()` to a terminating runtime ABI that raises the platform abort path and never returns. `PROCESS_CHILD_ID` exposes the pid stored in an existing child process handle without changing ownership or waiting on the process. `PROCESS_WAIT_EXIT_STATUS` lowers `wait` into a caller-owned exit-status slot, `PROCESS_TRY_WAIT` lowers Rust `try_wait` into explicit `(status, ready, code)` outputs, and `PROCESS_TRY_WAIT_EXIT_STATUS` packages a ready code into a caller-owned exit-status slot. `PROCESS_KILL` sends a forceful kill signal and waits for the process handle to become exited before returning. `PROCESS_EXEC_CAPTURE_OUTPUT*` packages the existing captured stdout/stderr owned read buffers plus that exit-status slot into a single `Output`-style view. `PROCESS_OUTPUT_FREE` releases only the owned stdout/stderr buffers; the caller still owns the `ProcessOutput` storage itself and any caller-allocated `ProcessExitStatus` backing used outside the capture helpers.

`PATH_BUF_CHOWN`, `PATH_BUF_LCHOWN`, `PATH_BUF_CHROOT`, `PATH_BUF_MKFIFO`, `PATH_BUF_MAKE_DIR`, `PATH_BUF_MAKE_DIR_WITH_MODE`.
*   **Missing from Rust**: Type-safe Rust `Path` / `PathBuf` system types, true component iterator APIs (`components`, `iter`, `ancestors`), Windows prefix/root semantics, rich `Metadata` / `ReadDir` / `io::Error` object integration, and platform-specific Windows/`WTF-8` `OsStr` / `OsString` semantics beyond the concrete Unix `OS_STRING_*` / `PATH_BUF_*` byte-buffer facades.
*   **Scope note**: The SA path facade is a POSIX-style byte-slice subset over `Slice`. `STARTS_WITH`, `ENDS_WITH`, and `TRY_STRIP_PREFIX` enforce slash-boundary component semantics, `TRIM_PREFIX` returns the stripped path or a copy of the original path, `JOIN`, `WITH_FILE_NAME`, `WITH_EXTENSION`, `WITH_ADDED_EXTENSION`, and `WITH_TRAILING_SEPARATOR` materialize concatenated string slices through existing `STR_CONCAT`, and `PARENT` / `FILE_NAME` / `FILE_STEM` / `EXTENSION` remain aliases over concrete basename/dirname/stem/ext helpers for existing callers. `PATH_TRY_PARENT`, `PATH_TRY_FILE_NAME`, `PATH_TRY_FILE_PREFIX`, `PATH_TRY_FILE_STEM`, `PATH_TRY_EXTENSION`, and `PATH_TRY_TO_STR` expose Rust `Option`-style `(ok, Slice)` query results so callers can distinguish absent values from empty slices or invalid UTF-8. `PATH_TRY_TO_STR` validates the path bytes as UTF-8 and returns a borrowed slice view on success; invalid UTF-8 returns `ok=0` and an empty slice rather than Rust's `Utf8Error` object. `PATH_TRIM_TRAILING_SEPARATOR` trims repeated trailing POSIX separators while preserving `/`. `PATH_FILE_PREFIX` mirrors Rust's first-dot split including hidden filename handling, `PATH_COMPONENT_COUNT` counts non-empty slash-separated POSIX components, `PATH_TRY_COMPONENT_AT` returns a caller-indexed component `Slice`, and `PATH_TRY_COMPONENT_PREFIX_AT` returns the path prefix through that component. `PATH_HASH` follows the supportable POSIX part of Rust's `Path::hash` by writing normalized non-empty component bytes and a final `chunk_bits` value; it skips repeated `/` and ordinary `.` components, while `PATH_BUF_HASH` forwards through `PATH_BUF_AS_PATH`. `PATH_ANCESTOR_COUNT` / `PATH_TRY_ANCESTOR_AT` expose indexed ancestor views rather than lazy iterators. Filesystem methods are thin wrappers over existing `FS_*` handles: `PATH_EXISTS` is a boolean `access`-style query, `PATH_TRY_EXISTS` returns `(status, exists)`, `PATH_METADATA` returns the current metadata handle contract, and `PATH_CANONICALIZE` / `PATH_READ_LINK` return owned read-buffer handles that callers must free with `FS_READ_BUFFER_FREE`. `PATH_IS_FILE`, `PATH_IS_DIR`, and `PATH_IS_SYMLINK` lower through metadata handles and return `0` on lookup failure, matching Rust's practical query shape instead of surfacing metadata errors. `PATH_BUF_*` provides a concrete owned byte-buffer path facade over `STRING_BUF` with push/pop/set_file_name/set_extension helpers plus capacity-remaining aliases, multi-part `PATH_BUF_LIT1/2/3` constructors, Unix `OsStr`/`OsString` byte conversions (`AS_OS_STR` / `FROM_OS_*` / `INTO_OS_STRING*`), and PathBuf query aliases (`IS_ABSOLUTE` / `COMPONENT_COUNT` / `TRY_FILE_*` / `TRY_PARENT` / ancestor helpers) plus `PUSH_PATH_BUF` / `JOIN*`, PathBuf FS query wrappers, and Path/PathBuf open/create/read/write/remove/copy/rename/hard_link/symlink/read_to_string/create_dir_all/remove_dir_all/set_permissions/set_times/read_dir/DirBuilder/OpenOptions/permissions-object/FileTimes filesystem ops over existing `FS_*` helpers; it still does not model Windows prefixes, component iterators, or borrow-scoped path types.

`RWLOCK_TRY_WRITE`, `RWLOCK_TRY_READ_NOERR`, `RWLOCK_TRY_WRITE_NOERR`, release and force-unlock helpers), primitive atomic wrappers (`AtomicBool`, `AtomicU8`, `AtomicI8`, `AtomicU16`, `AtomicI16`, `AtomicU32`, `AtomicI32`, `AtomicU64`, `AtomicI64`, `AtomicUsize`, `AtomicIsize` macro layouts and load/store/swap/fetch/cmpxchg/compare_exchange_weak helpers plus transparent `get_mut` / `from_mut` / `into_inner`-style pointer/value helpers, `AtomicOrdering` one-byte enum layout and load/store/failure/fence validation helpers, concrete width-specific `ATOMIC_*_FETCH_UPDATE` helpers for Bool/U8/I8/U16/I16/U32/I32/U64/I64/USIZE/ISIZE/PTR and `ATOMIC_*_FROM_PTR` aliases, plus `AtomicPtr` init/load/store/swap/fetch-byte-add/fetch-byte-sub/fetch-u64-add/fetch-u64-sub/cmpxchg/compare_exchange_weak and transparent pointer-layout helpers), `Arc`, `RefCell` shared/exclusive borrow helpers, and the matching core macros in `sa_std/core/*`, `ATOMIC_I8_DEFAULT`, `ATOMIC_I16_DEFAULT`, `ATOMIC_I32_DEFAULT`, `ATOMIC_I64_DEFAULT`, `ATOMIC_ISIZE_DEFAULT`, `ATOMIC_PTR_DEFAULT`, `ONCE_LOCK_DEFAULT`, `LAZY_LOCK_DEFAULT`, `TIME_SLEEP_DURATION_NS`, `TIME_INSTANT_DEFAULT`, `TIME_SYSTEM_TIME_DEFAULT`.
*   **Plugin alternative, not `sa_std`**: Deno facade time helpers live in `sa_plugin_deno` and are not part of compiler std coverage.
*   **Missing from Rust (Time)**: Rust's typed `Duration` / `Instant` / `SystemTime` structs, `Duration::from_nanos_u128`, signed/float duration conversions, `SystemTimeError` runtime `Result` integration beyond layout constants, platform-specific clock semantics, and rigorous overflow/error semantics beyond the current `(ok, value)` nanosecond macro subset. `TIME_DURATION_NEW` and the `from_*` helpers are concrete `u64` nanosecond constructors. The `TIME_DURATION_CHECKED_FROM_*` and `TIME_DURATION_CHECKED_NEW` helpers expose verifier-friendly overflow checks for SA's single-`u64` nanosecond representation; they do not model Rust's typed two-field `Duration` storage, panic behavior, or `u128` nanosecond constructor. Duration hashing is the narrow exception that splits the concrete nanosecond scalar into Rust's derived `secs: u64` plus `nanos: u32` hash field order before writing to the SA hasher. Instant/SystemTime hashing is similarly limited to SA's non-negative nanosecond scalar and Rust's Unix `Timespec { tv_sec: i64, tv_nsec: Nanoseconds(u32) }` hash shape; it does not cover pre-epoch negative `SystemTime` or non-Unix platform storage. `TIME_DURATION_CHECKED_ADD`, `TIME_DURATION_CHECKED_SUB`, `TIME_DURATION_CHECKED_MUL`, `TIME_DURATION_CHECKED_DIV`, `TIME_DURATION_SATURATING_ADD`, `TIME_DURATION_SATURATING_SUB`, `TIME_DURATION_SATURATING_MUL`, and `TIME_DURATION_ABS_DIFF` are Rust method-name aliases over the same concrete nanosecond scalar helpers. `TIME_DURATION_DIV_DURATION_FLOOR` and `TIME_DURATION_DIV_DURATION_CEIL` return explicit `(ok, ratio)` pairs and report division by zero as `ok=0` rather than trapping or panicking. `TIME_SATURATING_DIV_NS` is an SA convenience over checked division and saturates division-by-zero to `u64::MAX`; Rust `Duration` has `checked_div` and arithmetic trait division but no exact same saturating-div method.
*   **Safety note (Sync)**: `MPSC_NEW` checks slot-data and total allocation byte arithmetic before allocation, and MPSC send/receive plus length/availability/fullness helpers guard against `tail < head` underflow or damaged ring state. `RWLOCK_*` reader and writer state now uses atomic loads/stores, `atomic_rmw_add/sub`, and `cmpxchg` writer claims so concurrent readers cannot lose increments through load/add/store races; read acquisition rolls back if a writer appears during the claim window, and read-release traps on counter underflow. `MUTEX_LOCK` uses bounded exponential sleep backoff instead of a fixed 1ns sleep on every failed spin.
*   **Missing from Rust (Sync)**: `Condvar`, `Barrier`, unbounded `std::sync::mpsc` channel flavor, typed `Sender` / `Receiver` handle split/disconnect semantics, Rust `Result` integration for mpsc error objects beyond layout constants, `Once::call_once_force` runtime poisoning behavior beyond `OnceState` layout/query constants, `OnceLock<T>` generics and wait/take lifecycle parity beyond the concrete u64 cell, full generic `LazyLock<T,F>` beyond `LAZY_LOCK_*` aliases, `Atomic*::from_ptr` strict provenance semantics beyond the concrete address-transparent `ATOMIC_*_FROM_PTR` aliases, Rust panic integration for invalid `Ordering` arguments beyond the concrete validation predicates, full generic/`Ordering`-object `fetch_update` beyond the concrete width-specific helpers covering Bool/U8/I8/U16/I16/U32/I32/U64/I64/USIZE/ISIZE/PTR, RAII `MutexGuard`, runtime poisoning and Rust `Result` integration beyond `PoisonErrorU64`/`TryLockErrorU64` layout constants, and scoped borrow/thread-safety semantics. `ATOMIC_*_COMPARE_EXCHANGE_WEAK` is implemented as a strong cmpxchg alias; Rust permits weak compare-exchange to succeed without spurious failure, so this is a conservative SA subset rather than a distinct weak hardware lowering. `ATOMIC_PTR_FETCH_U64_*` models pointer-sized movement over SA's concrete 8-byte element layout rather than Rust's generic `T` element size.

---

## 2. Missing Full Rust Module Parity

The following Rust `std` modules still lack full Rust-level parity in `sa_std`:

1.  **Memory & Data Abstraction**: full Rust `std::any` reflection/trait-object semantics, full `std::ascii::Char` / escaping iterator APIs, full Unicode `std::char`, full Rust `std::pin` guarantees, and full Rust-level `std::array` / `std::ptr` semantics. `Box`, `Cell` / `CellU64`, `RefCell` / `RefCellU64`, concrete `Rc` / `Weak`, `Arc`, `std::any` primitive `TypeId` / `AnyRef`, byte-level ASCII helpers, `std::char` primitive scalar helpers, transparent `std::pin` pointer wrappers, `std::array` primitive `u64` helpers, and `std::ptr` primitive raw-pointer / `NonNull` helpers have macro-level SA subsets, but not full Rust module parity.
2.  **Core Trait Paradigm**: full Rust `std::error` trait/dyn-object behavior, full `std::borrow` / `ToOwned` / generic `Cow`, full `std::hash` trait/build/random-state behavior, full `std::iter` trait/adaptor system, full `std::marker` trait inference, and full `std::ops` trait/operator behavior. `std::borrow` primitive borrowed/Cow slice views, `std::cmp`, `std::convert`, `std::default`, `std::error` primitive `ErrorRef` descriptors, `std::hash` deterministic hasher primitives, slice-backed `std::iter` cursor/adaptor macros, `std::mem`, `std::num`, `std::marker` primitives, and `std::ops` ranges now have primitive macro coverage, but not full trait/generic parity.
3.  **FFI & Platform Specific**: full `std::ffi` (`CString`, `OsString`) and `std::os` (Unix/Windows extensions). Borrowed `CStr` and concrete owned `CString` byte-buffer helpers now have primitive macro coverage including bytes-with-NUL hash helpers, and Unix `OsStr`/`OsString` now have a concrete byte-buffer facade (`OS_STR_*` / `OS_STRING_*` over `Slice`/`Vec<u8>`, including capacity-remaining aliases, `OS_STRING_LIT1/2/3`, lossy `TO_STRING_LOSSY` helpers, and encoded-byte hash helpers), but Windows WTF-8, lossy display, and Rust's full error/trait/drop model are still missing.
4.  **Concurrency Infrastructure**: `std::thread (builder detached facade via `THREAD_BUILDER_*`)` (System thread management, `JoinHandle`) and a bundled async runtime/reactor. Primitive atomic macro wrappers now cover common scalar operations, but not Rust's full atomic type and ordering model. `sa_std/core/waker.*` models the Rust `std::task` waker ABI subset with `RawWaker`, `RawWakerVTable`, `Waker`, `LocalWaker`, and `Wake` layout/vtable helper macros. `sa_std/core/future.*` models `Poll`, `Poll<Result<..>>`, `Poll<Option<Result<..>>>`, `Context`, `ContextBuilder`, `Future::poll` vtable calls, ready/pending futures, poll_fn-style state, stateful `join2` pairs, and biased `select2` either results. `sa_std/core/task.*` provides `Task`, single-task executor polling, and ready-count batch polling. `sa_std/libsa_async.sa` remains the CPS/state-machine helper layer (`ASYNC_CTX_DEF`, `ASYNC_AWAIT_POINT`, etc.). SA intentionally does not provide native `async` / `await` syntax, Rust generics/traits/pin semantics, a hidden executor, or a bundled reactor.

## 4. Rust Core Minimal Closed Loop

The project now treats the following Rust core items as a **SA layout + macro contract**, not as native SA type-system features:

- `Option<T>`: represented by a tag + payload memory contract and helper macros in `sa_std/core/option.sa`, including `RESULT_DEFAULT` (Ok(0) constructor), concrete `u64`-payload Rust-style combinators such as `as_ref`, `as_mut`, `as_slice`, `as_mut_slice`, pointer-payload `copied` / `cloned`, `expect`, `unwrap_unchecked`, `map`, `map_or_default`, `inspect`, `filter`, `and`, `or`, `xor`, `and_then`, `or_else`, `ok_or`, `ok_or_else`, `contains`, `is_some_and`, `is_none_or`, `unwrap_or_else`, `unwrap_or_default`, `take`, `take_if`, `replace`, `insert`, `get_or_insert`, `get_or_insert_with`, `get_or_insert_default`, and pointer-payload `flatten` / `transpose` helpers for nested `Option<Option<u64>>` and `Option<Result<u64, u64>>` lowering.
- `Result<T, E>`: represented by a tag + ok/err payload memory contract and helper macros in `sa_std/core/result.sa`, including concrete `u64`-payload Rust-style combinators such as `as_ref`, `as_mut`, pointer-payload `copied` / `cloned`, `expect`, `expect_err`, `unwrap_unchecked`, `unwrap_err_unchecked`, checked-lowering `into_ok` / `into_err`, `map`, `map_or_default`, `inspect`, `inspect_err`, `map_err`, `and`, `or`, `and_then`, `or_else`, `ok`, `err`, `is_ok_and`, `is_err_and`, `unwrap_or_else`, `unwrap_or_default`, and pointer-payload `flatten` / `transpose` helpers for nested `Result<Result<u64, u64>, u64>` and `Result<Option<u64>, u64>` lowering.
- `panic` / `panic_msg`: represented by wrapper macros in `sa_std/core/panic.sa` and lowered as builtin termination paths. `sa_std/core/panic.sal` records a lightweight panic metadata layout surface for Rust `Location` (`PanicLocation_*`) and a concrete `AssertUnwindSafeU64` wrapper layout.
- `iter` / iterator-like traversal: represented by slice-backed cursor and consuming adaptor helpers in `sa_std/core/iter.sa`.
- ASCII byte classification, conversion, and escape-default writing: represented by byte/slice helper macros in `sa_std/core/ascii.sa` / `.sal` and re-exported through `sa_std/ascii.sa`.
- `Future` / `Poll` / `Context` / `Task`: represented by concrete layout contracts and vtable/helper macros in `sa_std/core/future.sa`, `sa_std/core/waker.sa`, and `sa_std/core/task.sa`. The covered async contract includes `RawWaker` / `RawWakerVTable` / `LocalWaker` / `Wake`, `ContextBuilder`, `Poll::map`, `Poll<Result>::map_ok/map_err`, `Poll<Option<Result>>::map_ok/map_err`, `ready` / `pending` state futures, `Future::poll` out-param ABI, stateful two-way `join` / `select` helpers, and executor ready-count polling.

The async coverage is deliberately a layout and macro contract. It is not native Rust `async fn` lowering, `.await` syntax, `Pin<&mut T>` enforcement, Rust trait objects/generics, or a scheduler/reactor implementation. Frontends should lower those higher-level semantics into the concrete SA ABI described above.

These helpers intentionally stop short of native Rust `trait` / `generic` semantics. In SA, those remain a frontend lowering concern: monomorphization, concrete ABI selection, and call-site rewriting belong in the compiler frontend, not in SA source.

This closed loop is already backed by concrete files and smoke coverage:

- `sa_std/core/option.sa` / `.sal`
- `sa_std/core/result.sa` / `.sal`
- `sa_std/core/panic.sa` / `.sal`
- `sa_std/core/iter.sa` / `.sal`
- `sa_std/core/ascii.sa` / `.sal`
- `sa_std/core/future.sa` / `.sal`
- `sa_std/core/task.sa` / `.sal`
- `sa_std/rust_core.sa` / `.sal`
- `tests/rust_core_fixture.sa`
- `tests/unit_framework/std_future_task_macro_surface.sa`

---

## 3. Common Macros Comparison

Rust relies heavily on declarative and procedural macros. `sa_std` provides functional parity for some through assembly `[MACRO]` definitions, but many are missing or limited.

### 3.1 Implemented in `sa_std` (Macro Parity)
| Rust Macro | `sa_std` Equivalent | Note |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `print!` / `println!` | `PRINT` / `PRINTLN` | Limited to string/bytes, no complex interpolation. |
| `eprint!` / `eprintln!` | `EPRINT` / `EPRINTLN` | Limited to string/bytes through stderr. |
| `assert!` | `ASSERT_TRUE` | Basic boolean check. |
| `assert_eq!` | `ASSERT_EQ` | Basic equality check. |
| `assert_ne!` | `ASSERT_NE` | Basic inequality check. |
| `vec!` | `VEC_NEW` / `VEC_PUSH` | No literal initialization like `vec![1, 2, 3]`. |
| `concat!` | `STR_CONCAT` | Concatenates two slices. |
| `stringify!` | `STRINGIFY!` | Flattener macro emits a source-text slice. |

### 3.1a Newly landed base macros
These are now implemented as first-wave portability helpers rather than missing gaps:
- Container and field access: `STRUCT_NEW`, `FIELD_GET`, `FIELD_SET`, `STRUCT_FREE`, `PTR_FIELD`
- Structural copy and equality: `STRUCT_COPY_FIELD`, `STRUCT_COPY`, `STRUCT_EQ_FIELD`, `STRUCT_EQ4`
