`ARRAY_INTO_ITER_INSPECT_COLLECT_U64`, `ARRAY_INTO_ITER_SCAN_COLLECT_U64`, `ARRAY_INTO_ITER_FILTER_COLLECT_U64`, `ARRAY_INTO_ITER_FILTER_MAP_COLLECT_U64`, `ARRAY_INTO_ITER_MAP_WHILE_COLLECT_U64`, `ARRAY_INTO_ITER_SIZE_HINT_U64`, `ARRAY_INTO_ITER_EXACT_SIZE_LEN_U64`, `ARRAY_INTO_ITER_EXACT_SIZE_IS_EMPTY_U64`)
- Scope note (Array IntoIter comparator/key aggregates): these helpers are concrete `u64` comparator/key aggregate aliases over the explicit backing-Vec cursor and do not model Rust `Option<T>` ABI, generic `Ord` trait dispatch, closure capture, or generic item move/drop semantics.
- Scope note (Array IntoIter search/predicate): these helpers consume through the explicit backing-Vec cursor and short-circuit like the underlying iterator primitives, returning explicit bool, `(has, value)`, `(has, index)`, or `(ok, has, value)` scalars instead of Rust `Option<T>` / `Option<usize>` / generic `Try` ABI.
- Scope note (Array IntoIter for_each): these helpers consume through the explicit backing-Vec cursor, with `try_for_each` returning an explicit success scalar and leaving the suffix after the failing item available; they do not model Rust closure capture, generic `Try` residual conversion, or panic/drop cleanup.
- Scope note (Array IntoIter sortedness): these helpers consume the remaining explicit backing-Vec cursor and return concrete bool scalars; they do not model Rust generic `Ord` trait dispatch, closure capture, or borrowed reference item types.
- Scope note (Array IntoIter lexicographic comparison): these helpers consume both explicit backing-Vec cursors and return concrete ordering/bool scalars, with `PARTIAL_CMP_BY` returning explicit `(ok, ordering)` instead of Rust `Option<Ordering>`; they do not model generic `Ord` / `PartialOrd` trait dispatch, closure capture, or generic item move/drop semantics.
- Scope note (Array IntoIter collect): these helpers consume the explicit backing-Vec cursor and materialize caller-owned `Vec<u64>` outputs; `TRY_COLLECT` uses a concrete mapper and explicit success scalar rather than Rust generic `Try` residual conversion, `Result` / `Option` ABI, or allocator-parametric collection.
- Scope note (Array IntoIter eager adapter collect): these helpers materialize concrete `u64` `take(...).collect`, `skip(...).collect`, `rev().collect`, and `step_by(...).collect` shapes over the explicit backing-Vec cursor; they do not model Rust lazy adapter identity/state types, generic iterator composition, or panic object behavior for `step_by(0)`.
- Scope note (Array IntoIter transform/filter collect): these helpers materialize concrete `u64` `map(...).collect`, `inspect(...).collect`, `scan(...).collect`, `filter(...).collect`, `filter_map(...).collect`, and `map_while(...).collect` shapes over the explicit backing-Vec cursor; they do not model Rust lazy adapter identity/state types, closure capture, generic `Option` ABI, or generic iterator composition.
- Additional Array IntoIter sequence eager collect aliases: `ARRAY_INTO_ITER_INTERSPERSE_COLLECT_U64`, `ARRAY_INTO_ITER_INTERSPERSE_WITH_COLLECT_U64`, `ARRAY_INTO_ITER_CYCLE_TAKE_COLLECT_U64`, `ARRAY_INTO_ITER_TAKE_WHILE_COLLECT_U64`, and `ARRAY_INTO_ITER_SKIP_WHILE_COLLECT_U64`.
- Scope note (Array IntoIter sequence collect): these helpers materialize concrete `u64` `intersperse(...).collect`, `intersperse_with(...).collect`, finite `cycle().take(...).collect`, `take_while(...).collect`, and `skip_while(...).collect` shapes over the explicit backing-Vec cursor; they do not model Rust lazy adapter identity/state types, generic closure capture, infinite `Cycle` objects, or generic item move/drop semantics.
- Additional Array IntoIter pair/composition eager collect aliases: `ARRAY_INTO_ITER_ENUMERATE_COLLECT_U64`, `ARRAY_INTO_ITER_ZIP_COLLECT_U64`, and `ARRAY_INTO_ITER_CHAIN_COLLECT_U64`.
- Scope note (Array IntoIter pair collect): pair-producing helpers materialize interleaved `u64` fields into caller-owned vectors (`index,value` or `left,right`), while `chain` materializes left then right cursor remainders; they do not model Rust tuple ABI, generic `IntoIterator` conversion, lazy adapter identity/state types, or generic item move/drop semantics.
- Additional Array IntoIter adapter-state eager collect aliases: `ARRAY_INTO_ITER_BY_REF_COLLECT_U64`, `ARRAY_INTO_ITER_FUSE_COLLECT_U64`, and `ARRAY_INTO_ITER_PEEKABLE_COLLECT_U64`.
- Scope note (Array IntoIter adapter-state collect): `by_ref`, `fuse`, and `peekable` collect helpers operate over the explicit backing-Vec cursor and materialize caller-owned outputs; they do not expose Rust lazy adapter objects, borrow lifetimes, buffered `Peekable` state, reference item semantics, or generic item move/drop behavior.
- Additional Array IntoIter partition aliases: `ARRAY_INTO_ITER_PARTITION_U64` and `ARRAY_INTO_ITER_IS_PARTITIONED_U64`.
- Scope note (Array IntoIter partition): `PARTITION` materializes concrete true/false `Vec<u64>` outputs and `IS_PARTITIONED` uses the iterator helper's consuming short-circuit shape, including leaving an unchecked suffix after non-partitioned detection; these helpers do not model Rust generic `Default + Extend` collection, tuple ABI, nightly adapter trait wiring, closure capture, or generic item move/drop behavior.
- Additional Array IntoIter nightly array/window eager collect aliases: `ARRAY_INTO_ITER_ARRAY_CHUNKS_COLLECT_U64` and `ARRAY_INTO_ITER_MAP_WINDOWS_COLLECT_U64`.
- Scope note (Array IntoIter array/window collect): `ARRAY_CHUNKS_COLLECT` materializes complete non-overlapping chunks into a flat `Vec<u64>` and leaves incomplete tails available, while `MAP_WINDOWS_COLLECT` maps overlapping windows with a concrete callback; these helpers do not model Rust const-generic array item/reference ABI, lazy adapter objects, generic closure capture, or generic item move/drop behavior.
- Pointer primitives: `PTR_NULL`, `PTR_IS_NULL`, `PTR_IS_NONNULL`, `PTR_EQ`, `PTR_NE`, `PTR_ADDR`, `PTR_OFFSET_BYTES`, `PTR_OFFSET_U64`, `PTR_IS_ALIGNED_TO`, `PTR_READ_U64`, `PTR_READ_U8`, `PTR_WRITE_U64`, `PTR_WRITE_U8`, `PTR_SWAP_U64`, `PTR_REPLACE_U64`, `PTR_TAKE_U64`, `PTR_COPY_BYTES`, `PTR_WRITE_BYTES`, `PTR_COPY_U64S`, and `NONNULL_*` transparent pointer-wrapper helpers
- Comparison sugar: `CMP_COMPARE_U64`, `CMP_COMPARE_I64`, `CMP_MIN_U64`, `CMP_MAX_U64`, `CMP_MIN_BY_U64`, `CMP_MAX_BY_U64`, `CMP_MIN_BY_KEY_U64`, `CMP_MAX_BY_KEY_U64`, `CMP_MIN_I64`, `CMP_MAX_I64`, `CMP_CLAMP_U64`, `CMP_CLAMP_I64`, `CMP_ORDERING_*`, and concrete `CMP_REVERSE_U64_*` / `CMP_REVERSE_I64_*` helpers including hash/hash_one forwarding

`DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_OS_STRING`, `PATH_HASH`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_PATH`, `PATH_BUF_HASH`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_PATH_BUF`, `NET_IPV4_HASH`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_NET_IPV4`, `NET_SOCKET_ADDR_V4_HASH`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_NET_SOCKET_ADDR_V4`, `NONZERO_*_HASH`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_NONZERO_*`, `WRAPPING_*_HASH`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_WRAPPING_*`, `SATURATING_*_HASH`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_SATURATING_*`, `TIME_DURATION_HASH`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_DURATION`, `TIME_INSTANT_HASH`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_INSTANT`, `TIME_SYSTEM_TIME_HASH`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_SYSTEM_TIME`, `PHANTOM_DATA_HASH`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_PHANTOM_DATA`, `PHANTOM_PINNED_HASH`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_PHANTOM_PINNED`, `CMP_REVERSE_*_HASH`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_CMP_REVERSE_*`, `MANUALLY_DROP_U64_HASH`, `DEFAULT_HASHER_WRITE_MANUALLY_DROP_U64`, `HASH_TUPLE2_U64`, `HASH_TUPLE3_U64`, `HASH_TUPLE4_U64`, `HASH_TUPLE5_U64`, `HASH_TUPLE6_U64`, `HASH_TUPLE7_U64`, `HASH_TUPLE8_U64`, `HASH_TUPLE9_U64`, `HASH_TUPLE10_U64`, `HASH_TUPLE11_U64`, `HASH_TUPLE12_U64`, `HASH_PTR_VALUE`, `BUILD_HASHER_DEFAULT_*`, and deterministic `RANDOM_STATE_*` hasher-builder helpers
- Bit and mask helpers: `BIT_MASK`, `BIT_SET`, `BIT_GET`, `BIT_CLEAR`, `BIT_TEST`, `BIT_INDEX_BYTE`, `BIT_INDEX_BIT`
- Hash and probe helpers: `HASH_PTR`, `HASH_MIX`, `HASH_MOD`, `PROBE_START`, `PROBE_NEXT`, `MAP_LOOKUP`, `MAP_INSERT_OR_UPDATE`
- Cleanup sugar: `DEFER`, `CLEANUP_ON_ERROR`, `WITH_TEMP`, `RETURN_CLEAN`, `FREE_AND_RETURN`
- Control-flow sugar: `MATCH_BOOL`, `ELIF`, `WHILE_LET`, `BREAK_IF`, `CONTINUE_IF`

### 3.2 Missing or Partially Implemented (Gaps)
*   **Formatting & Printing**:
    *   Missing `format!` (No dynamic string interpolation/formatting macro).
    *   `WRITE` / `WRITELN` cover raw byte writes to a handle, but Rust-style formatted `write!` / `writeln!` interpolation is still missing.
*   **Error Handling & Control Flow**:
    *   `PANIC`, `PANIC_MSG`, `TODO`, `UNIMPLEMENTED`, and `UNREACHABLE` cover the basic panic-style macro paths, and `PanicLocation_*` / `AssertUnwindSafeU64_*` record lightweight panic metadata layout constants. SA still does not model Rust's full panic hook payload objects, formatting payload surface, unwinding ABI, or `catch_unwind` runtime behavior.
    *   `MATCHES_OPTION` / `MATCHES_RESULT` cover the tag-checking subset of Rust `matches!` for `Option` / `Result` layouts.
    *   `OPTION_*` and `RESULT_*` combinators cover concrete `u64` payload lowering patterns, including Rust-style payload-slot `as_ref` / `as_mut`, 0/1 length `as_slice` / `as_mut_slice` views, pointer-payload `copied` / `cloned`, `expect`, unchecked unwrap helpers, `map_or_default` using `0` as the concrete default value, map/inspect/filter/and/or/and_then/or_else, predicate helpers, unwrap default/else helpers, Option `take` / `take_if` / `replace`, Option insert/get-or-insert helpers returning the value slot pointer, pointer-payload `flatten` / `transpose` helpers, and Option/Result conversion helpers. They still do not implement Rust generics, trait impls, borrow-scoped reference lifetimes, iterator collection, or formatted panic payloads. `RESULT_INTO_OK` / `RESULT_INTO_ERR` are checked SA lowering aliases over unwrap paths; they do not prove Rust's `Into<!>` never-type bound.
    *   `TRY_OPTION` and `TRY_RESULT` provide Zig-style explicit early-return helpers over SA's concrete `Option` / `Result` memory layouts. SA still does not provide Rust's generic `Try` trait, `FromResidual` conversion, or a fully generic `try!` macro surface.
*   **Compile-time & Metaprogramming**:
    *   `cfg!` is now covered by SA flattener macro expansion tests.
    *   `env!`, `option_env!` are now covered by SA flattener macro expansion tests.
    *   `include_str!` / `include_bytes!` are now covered by SA flattener macro expansion tests.
    *   `include!` is now covered by SA flattener macro expansion tests.
    *   `line!`, `file!`, `column!` are now covered by SA flattener macro expansion tests.
    *   `module_path!` is now covered by SA flattener macro expansion tests.
    *   `stringify!` is now covered by SA flattener macro expansion tests.
*   **Collection Initializers**:
    *   Missing literal initializers for all collections (e.g., no `hashmap!{...}`, `set!{...}`).
